# Relay rework ledger

Branch: `jaimecernuda-relay-rework` (integration, in every repository). Session branches in clio-relay are `jaimecernuda-relay-rework-<ws>`.

Contract: [relay-rework-plan.md](relay-rework-plan.md). Execution plan: [relay-rework-execution.md](relay-rework-execution.md). Overview: [relay-rework-workstreams.md](relay-rework-workstreams.md).

## How to write here

- Each session writes only in its own section. The header and the summary belong to W1, then W16.
- Record decisions with their reason, the facts the contract asks for with their source, and qualification evidence: the commands, handles and screenshots read.
- A mock, a text search or an owner-pending row is never recorded as a live pass.

## Decisions across workstreams

Kept by W1 as the ledger's header owner. Each is the coordinator's.

- **LQ14 reads as one server instance.** Raised by W2. Protocol `2026-07-28` has no MCP session: `ctx.session_id` changes on every call, over Streamable HTTP and over stdio. "One session" in LQ14 therefore means continuity of one server instance (and one client connection). W2's counter reports `server_instance` for this. W9 and W16 test against it.
- **Tab action tools are listed only to A2UI clients, as app-only (amends LQ1 and review round 2 #2).** Raised by W14: clio-agent's dispatcher (`server_actions.resolve_server_tool`) and its executor permission gate call only tools that appear in `tools/list`, so tab actions kept out of every listing would leave each Compute tab button broken. Relay lists its tab action tools, marked `_meta.ui.visibility: ["app"]`, only in a `tools/list` request whose `_meta` declares `org.a2ui/a2ui`. That is the standard MCP Apps app-only pattern and needs no host change. A client without A2UI sees exactly the five verbs plus mounted tools. Every action call still needs its single-use action token, so a host that ignores visibility still cannot act. W14 builds it in `surface/a2ui/`, and asks W1 if it needs a hook in the `tools/list` path.
- **clio-kit relay rework work targets main, per the execution plan's base and drift row.** Raised by W15. clio-kit's develop (8375600b) is the old layout, without mcp-servers/ and with ParaView 2.2.3, and has diverged from main (38 ahead, 175 behind, 80 conflicts). W15 stays on main; its branch line is updated when it sends its final commit with the paraview-mcp 3.2.0 bump.

## W1. Relay core

Branch `jaimecernuda-relay-rework-w1`, merged into `jaimecernuda-relay-rework` at M1.

### Decisions taken from W3's contract

Accepted from the coordinator on W3's two open points, with what they mean for relay:

- O9 is settled. Relay's plugin ships its server command in `mcp.json` beside `plugin.json`, with no connector entry.
- HS1a. Relay sends A2UI only when that request's `_meta["io.modelcontextprotocol/clientCapabilities"].extensions` carries `org.a2ui/a2ui`, and always beside the full text result.
- HS4a. Relay reads the grant file named by its own setting (`--grant-file globus=<path>` or `CLIO_RELAY_GRANT_FILE_GLOBUS`). The plugin maps it from `${CLIO_ACCOUNT_GLOBUS_FILE}` in `mcp.json`. Relay's code never reads the CLIO name.
- Tab actions arrive as plain `tools/call` with `arguments` set to the action context, so the single-use action token travels in the context.

### Protocol and pins

- MCP `2026-07-28` with the `io.modelcontextprotocol/tasks` extension is the primary path. `2025-11-25` clients are served too and use the RL2a handle path. Source: `src/clio_relay/surface/server.py` (`PROTOCOL_VERSION`).
- Python `mcp==2.3.0`, `fastmcp==4.2.1`, `fastmcp-slim[client,server]==4.2.1`, `fastmcp-tasks==4.2.1`, `psij-python` with `ResourceSpecV1`. Pinned in `pyproject.toml` and locked in `uv.lock`.
- fastmcp moved from 4.0.0b1 to 4.2.1 because pip-audit flagged GHSA-vqqp-x5qm-5qp4 (M2M OAuth issuer binding, fixed in 4.1.2). anyio 4.16.0, dnspython 2.9.2, filelock 4.2.12 and pyjwt 2.15.1 were upgraded in the lock for advisories that develop's lock also carries.

### Decisions

- **Relay is a plain MCP server.** Five tools: `describe`, `run`, `exec`, `files`, `serve`. There is no client-specific path. The proof clients are the plain `mcp` 2.3.0 `Client` and the fastmcp `Client`, over stdio and Streamable HTTP. Why: user rule 1.
- **Tasks first, handles as the fallback.** `run` is task-optional on `2026-07-28` (`fastmcp-tasks`, poll interval 2 s, TTL 7 days). The task id is the operation id. On `2025-11-25` the `run` listing says `taskSupport: forbidden`, because tasks there are only the extension, so that client gets an ordinary result or a handle. A call without a task waits up to `wait_s` (default 20, max 300) and then returns the handle. Every result carries `_meta["ai.iowarp.relay/operation"]`. Why: user rule 2.
- **Asks reach a person in four ways.** In a task: `input_required`, answered with `tasks/update`. Inline: form or URL elicitation when the client declares that mode. Neither available: a typed refusal (`needs_approval` or `needs_sign_in`) that carries a single-use loopback page address. And `clio-relay-next asks` on the command line. An answer is recorded once. An approval binds the site, the spec digest and the charge, so a changed spec is refused with `approval_mismatch`.
- **Policy.** Sites are `open` or `guarded`. A guarded site with no guard hook installed refuses with `guard_unavailable`, rather than running unguarded. Spend asks are `never`, `always` or a threshold.
- **Restart safety.** Operation records are written before submit. A submit interrupted by a restart is reconciled by the adapter's `find(operation_id)` (for `local` on a scheduler, the job name `relay-<operation_id>`) after a 2-minute grace, so a job is submitted once.
- **Extension points are entry points.** Adapters: `clio_relay.adapters` (`local`, `fixture`). Hooks: `clio_relay.hooks` (`asks` for W6, `guard` for W13, `serve` for W9), with stub defaults that keep M1 runnable.
- **Grant files.** Relay reads `CLIO_RELAY_GRANT_FILE_<PROVIDER>` or `--grant-file <provider>=<path>`. It never reads a CLIO name (HS4a above).
- **Change events at `2026-07-28`.** FastMCP 4.2.1 does not serve `subscriptions/listen`, so relay registers the SDK's `ListenHandler` over an `InMemorySubscriptionBus` itself (`src/clio_relay/surface/changes.py`). `RelayServer.changes.resource_updated(uri)`, `resources_list_changed()` and `tools_list_changed()` publish on the bus. They also send the bare notification to every `2025-11-25` connection relay has seen. Relay keeps the connection, because mcp 2.3's `ServerSession` lasts only one request. Why: W3's HS3 server obligation. W14's Compute tab resource calls `resource_updated`.
- **Cutover (coordinator, once W8's gate and W9's LQ12 to LQ15 passed).** `clio_relay.cli` is the new surface (it was `cli_next.py`). `clio-relay` and `python -m clio_relay` (`__main__.py`) run it, and the `clio-relay-next` script is gone. The `worker` adapter's CLI is mounted as `clio-relay worker`, because a remote worker runs `clio-relay worker api start`. Proven on Ares at 6285a7d with a wheel installed in a clean venv: `clio-relay --help` lists `serve`, `connect`, `adapters`, `site`, `rendezvous`, `asks` and `worker`; `python -m clio_relay` runs the same app; and an `mcp` stdio client lists exactly `describe`, `exec`, `files`, `run` and `serve`.
- **Adapter extras and the universal lock (coordinator, wave 1).** uv's lock is universal, so an extra's caps apply to every install.
  - `tapis = ["tapipy==25.4.0"]`. Its openapi-core 0.16 lowers jsonschema-path to 0.3.4, pathable to 0.4.4 and referencing to 0.36.2. Accepted.
  - `globus` pins `globus-compute-sdk>=4.21,<4.22`. Without the cap, relay's `prerelease = "allow"` picks the 4.22.0a0 alpha. Every globus-compute-sdk caps `rich<15`, so rich is 14.3.4. Accepted.
  - There is no `sky` extra. skypilot 0.14.3 caps `click<8.2` and `uvicorn<0.36`. W11 runs skypilot from its own environment as a subprocess.
  - keyring is in core (W6), and `keyrings.alt` is in the `testing` extra.
- **RL39 and the builtin repo (coordinator, option A).** Relay uses no `builtin` package, so the worker bootstrap must not add or require the `builtin` slot. It leaves a registration made by `jarvis init` alone, and the receipt reports it as evidence only. W8 owns the change in the worker adapter, together with the stale `docs/ai/system-context.md`, `docs/ai/testing-context.md` and `docs/ai/release-validation-*.md`. The other option, closing the item with no change, was rejected because a required `builtin` keeps relay tied to jarvis-cd's packages, which RL39 removes.
- **jarvis-util is dropped (coordinator).** The worker bootstrap no longer clones, requires or lists jarvis-util. `grc-iit/jarvis-util` is private and archived, so a fresh bootstrap stopped at `git clone` on a host without GitHub credentials, and jarvis-cd 1.8.1, the pinned version, does not import it. An older receipt that lists it still validates. W8 made the change (deec1d0, d56458b) and proved it with a fresh bootstrap on fx-ssh with no credentials: outcome `full`, rc 0.
- **jarvis-cd is a dev dependency, not a runtime one (coordinator).** `jarvis-cd @ git+https://github.com/grc-iit/jarvis-cd@v1.8.1` is in the `dev` group, so it is in `uv.lock` and a `uv sync` keeps it. Before, the shared Ares venv got it from `uv pip install` in `setup.sh`, and a `uv sync` removed it, so sites pointing at `.venv/bin/jarvis` exited 69. Relay itself never imports JARVIS: it runs the site's `jarvis` command. The lock adds numpy, pandas and podman-compose with it, for development only. The shared venv was restored with jarvis-cd 1.8.1. With `jarvis` now on a clone's PATH, W8's targeted modules still pass (437 of 440; the 3 errors are develop's uv-pin setup errors).
- **The files contract is one table for every adapter (polish wave, W7's proposal widened).** `docs/files.md` is the contract and `clio_relay.jobs.file_rules` holds the shared rules.
  - `FileAccess` gains `delete(path, *, recursive=False)` and `write(..., offset=0, final=True)`. A write with `offset > 0` or `final=False` is one chunk into `<path>.relay-part`. Each chunk must start where the part ends, or it refuses `invalid_arguments` with `resume_at`. The `final` chunk moves the part into place, and `overwrite` is checked on the first chunk and on that move. `stat("<path>.relay-part")` gives the resume point; downloads resume with `read(offset, limit)`.
  - `delete`: a missing path refuses `path_not_found`, a non-empty directory needs `recursive`, a symlink is removed and never followed, and the work directory, the home directory and `/` refuse `invalid_arguments`. There is no new refusal reason.
  - A relative path is under relay's work directory and may not leave it, on every adapter.
  - The `files` tool gains op `delete` and the `final` and `recursive` arguments. The 4 MiB inline limit stays, and is the chunk size.
  - Who has what: local, fixture and worker take everything (the worker's site side is the local implementation, and chunks travel as a separate `write-chunk` route, so a site that predates them refuses instead of writing one chunk as the whole file). Tapis deletes; it refuses chunks. ssh, globus and iri refuse delete and chunks with `verb_not_implemented` until their owners add them. Those stubs, the escape check in ssh, globus and iri, and Tapis's delete are compat edits in W7's, W10's and W11's files, for them to review. The kit gains `test_files_delete`, `test_files_chunked_write` and `test_files_relative_paths_stay_inside`; each passes on a `verb_not_implemented` refusal.
- **Deadline tests that fail under load are removed, not rerun (the person's standing rule, coordinator).** The 7fc87fe gate ran beside a second gate on Ares, and three tests failed:
  - `tests/test_bootstrap_fast_path.py::test_candidate_verifier_accepts_pinned_uv_metadata_subset_only` builds a wheel inside a 60 s subprocess deadline;
  - `tests/test_cli.py::test_cli_session_teardown_waits_for_worker_acknowledged_cancellation[leased]` and `[running]` race the old session teardown's 30 s cancel deadline through a patched `time.sleep`.
  All three pass alone, so they test timing, not behaviour. Each covers an old-system path (the bootstrap candidate verifier, gateway session teardown) that the shrink removes. They are deleted, and no other test lost coverage the new design needs.
- **Anyone can bring a machine or an adapter from the repo alone (user-directed acceptance bar).** The user: "make sure we have a clean maintainable repository to which other people can bring new machines, new interfaces to acces".
  - **One contract module.** `clio_relay.adapters.base` is all an adapter imports. Its docstring is the contract: declarations, verbs, sign-in and secrets, errors, policy, the optional hooks and registration. It re-exports the job, file and endpoint types an adapter returns, and `__all__` lists them. A contributor reads nothing else.
  - **`SiteFiles` gives every rule in `docs/files.md` for six primitives** (`adapters/site_files.py`: `kind`, `describe`, `children`, `get`, `put` of a whole file, and `remove` of one entry, over the site's own API). Path resolution, the escape check, delete's refusals, chunks and `.relay-part` live in the base, so an adapter cannot get them subtly wrong. It is designed around W11's backend limits (coordinator): globus and iri cannot append, iri cannot remove a tree, and iri caps one upload at 5 MiB. So chunks collect in a local staging part (`context.state_dir / "parts"`) and go up in one `upload` with the final chunk, never as a read-modify-write of a remote part, which costs O(n²) bytes; `stat("<path>.relay-part")` and `resume_at` answer from the staged part. A recursive delete checks "not empty" itself and walks the tree deepest first (`remove_tree`, which a site with a tree delete overrides). `max_upload_bytes` and `upload_hint` refuse an oversized file before any byte moves. ssh, Tapis, globus and iri can adopt it to close their files gaps.
  - **Secrets go through `AdapterContext.secret(name)`**, which reads relay's keyring store. An adapter never imports the identity package. The sky adapter now uses it.
  - **Registration is the `clio_relay.adapters` entry point**, with nothing in core to edit. A machine on an existing adapter is a `sites.yaml` entry, or a catalog file (`catalog/sites/<id>.yaml` plus an optional skill) checked by `tests/relay_next/test_catalog.py`.
  - **`clio_relay` ships `py.typed`**, so an adapter in its own package type-checks against it in strict mode.
  - **The kit gains four tests**: `test_errors_are_typed`, `test_cancel_after_finish`, `test_exec_cancelled` and `test_open_and_guarded`. They cover the typed errors, cancellation of a finished job and of a running exec, and the guard on an open and a guarded site. Every built-in adapter runs them.
  - **The template is `examples/adapter-template/`**: `relay_acme`, a REST facility adapter on `SiteFiles`, with an in-repo fake facility, that passes the kit (18 tests) and strict pyright.
  - **The docs are `docs/adding-an-adapter.md`, `CONTRIBUTING.md` and a README section.** The command line gains `describe [SITE]` and `site catalog --schema` for the walkthrough.
  - **Stale docs are deleted; history stays here.** Gone: `docs/ai/*`, `docs/one-link-control-plane.md`, `docs/runtime-capability-boundary.md`, `docs/design/core-queue-split-2026-08.md`, and the `examples/ares-*` pipelines of the old queue, which nothing referenced. `docs/architecture.md` is rewritten for the new design, and AGENTS.md points to CONTRIBUTING, the architecture page, the contract and this ledger.
- **Hook entry points fall back when their module is missing.** `hook_factory` uses the stub when a hook's own module is not installed. Any other import error is raised. Why: an entry point can be registered before its workstream merges, and the guard stub refuses guarded sites, so the fallback is safe.
- **The serve flow follows run (W9's spec).** `RelayService.start_serve` runs the guard check and price on `spec.job`, creates a `kind="serve"` record with the spend and sign-in asks, and calls `serve_hook.start`. `refresh` calls `serve_hook.status` and `cancel` calls `serve_hook.stop`. `record.result["endpoint"]` holds the endpoint, `record.result["message"]` the latest `JobStatus.message`, and `record.outputs` the stop artifacts. `serve` is task-optional like `run`. A running serve task is `working`, and its status message names each port as `name: host:port`. `wait` also returns once the service is ready.
- **Secret URLs reach the UI only.** The record, `describe` and the task status keep an endpoint's ports without their `url`. The serve result carries `_meta["ai.iowarp.relay/service"]` from `ServeHook.ui_data(record)`. Why: RL22. A test caught that the endpoint first reached the record with its URLs.
- **The stub serve hook refuses up front.** With no serve runtime installed, `serve` refuses with `verb_not_implemented` and writes no record. `ServeHook.attach(server, changes)` is called in `RelayServer.build`, and the stub's `attach` and `ui_data` do nothing. `local` lists `serve` and imports `clio_relay.serve` lazily; without it, `local.serve` refuses the same way.
- **Under tasks, `run(cancel=)` and `run(resume=)` return a plain result.** Only a new run or serve becomes a task. The same holds for `serve(service=)` and `serve(stop=)`.
- **Ask pages mount on the HTTP server (W6).** `MountsPages` is an optional Protocol (`mount(server)`). `create_server` calls it after `build()` for HTTP when the ask provider implements it. W9's `attach` follows the same pattern. `HookContext.settings["public_base_url"]` holds the server's public origin.
- **`serve` over HTTP.** The command line takes `--grant-file PROVIDER=PATH` (repeatable). It did not exist at M1, though the published interfaces listed it. It also takes `--tls-cert` and `--tls-key`, given together, which make the origin https for RL25a. Uvicorn's access log is off, because page addresses carry single-use tokens.
- **The job name is recorded before submit.** A run's SUBMITTING move stores `result["job_name"]` with the note "job name X" (RL25a).
- **The asks command group is W6's.** `clio-relay-next asks` mounts `clio_relay.cli_asks.app`. The built-in `list` and `answer` were removed when W6 merged.
- **Bounded commands leave nothing behind.** `run_bounded` reads both pipes to their end after a timeout kill. When its caller is cancelled, it kills the process group before it re-raises. This fixed a leaked subprocess transport ("Event loop is closed") from the local conformance tests, which was already present at M1. The relay_next suite now runs with `PytestUnraisableExceptionWarning` as an error.
- **W11 merged.** `jaimecernuda-relay-rework-w11` at b41c756 is merged as aa9b7b1. Its adapter pages are in the docs index in `README.md`.
- **Routing skips an adapter whose settings do not build (RL5, coordinator decision).** An alternative that fails at build time with `invalid_config`, such as Aurora with no endpoint or iri with no `resource_id`, is skipped, and routing moves on to the next one. The skip is part of the route, as `skipped: [{site, adapter, reason, message}]`. It shows in `describe`'s routes, in an operation's `result["skipped"]` and in an `exec` result, so the person sees why it was not used. When every candidate fails, the call refuses with `invalid_config`, and the refusal lists each adapter's reason. Any other refusal at build time is still returned as it is. Routing builds each candidate through the service's adapter cache (`RelayService.route` and `site_routes`).
- **`exec` has no hard time limit (coordinator decision, the person's rule).** `timeout_s` is optional and has no default or ceiling. Without it the command runs until it ends. After 30 s with no output on either stream, relay reports it as quiet, then reports again every 30 s while it stays quiet (until retake 1u the waits doubled up to 600 s, which left a quiet command silent for up to ten minutes between reports; a hung ssh channel close then read as a plain "Running"). Each report is a progress notification on the call (not a log message, since logging is deprecated at `2026-07-28`): "no output for N s; the command is still running; relay checks again in M s; cancel the call to stop it", and for `exec_read` also "it only reads, so running it again is safe". The result carries `quiet: {reports, longest_s}` when there were reports, for a client that sent no progress token. Cancelling the call kills the command's process group. A caller may still pass `timeout_s`, and then the command is killed at that limit as before. Every adapter's `exec` must accept `timeout_s=None` and an optional `on_stall`.
- **The conformance kit no longer skips.** For a verb the adapter does not implement, each kit test asserts the typed `verb_not_implemented` refusal and passes, instead of skipping. A new kit test runs `exec` with no time limit.
- **Wave 1 merges.** Each was merged into `jaimecernuda-relay-rework-w1`, gated on Ares and then pushed to the integration branch:
  - W11 at 785dcba, then at b8c9cf4 (merge f8a02d2), which moves globus and sky onto W6's identity API. Then at 745308c (merge 3f2d571), which adds regression tests for my duration compat edits in globus, iri and sky. The `credentials.service` setting is gone, and a config that still sets it is refused with `invalid_config`.
  - W7 at c30e348 (merge 82a2302), then its follow-up at cbf2e39 (merge 9490a62). Its exec replaces my compat stopgap c5ea18b.   - Gated together at 2e2da25 on Ares, with the size ratchet's rewrapped comment line in 2f5e8ff: core 488, asks 79, guard 86, relay_tapis 50, relay_ssh 108 and w9 421 passed, 0 skipped; the cutover wheel checks pass; pyright 4535; size floors src 82.29 and tests 77.97.
- **Relay never imposes a walltime or a duration (coordinator decision, the person's rule).** `JobAttributes.duration` is `timedelta | None` with no default, for run and serve specs alike. When it is unset, the site's `defaults.duration` applies if the site sets one. Otherwise no walltime is sent, and the scheduler's own default applies. `to_psij` passes `duration=None` explicitly, because psij's own default is 10 minutes. `JobSpec.node_hours()` returns `None` when the duration is unknown, and `Charge.walltime_s` and `Charge.node_hours` may be `None`, meaning unknown. The globus, iri, tapis and sky adapters send no walltime when the spec has none. These are compat edits in W10's and W11's files, for their owners to review. Sky prices such a job with no amount.
- **An unknown cost always asks (coordinator decision).** `SpendPolicy.needs_approval(None)` is true unless the site's `ask` is `never`. The approval message says plainly: "sets no walltime, so its duration and cost are unknown: the scheduler's default limit applies".
- **No new job state for a job the scheduler forgot (coordinator decision).** A job the scheduler no longer lists, with no exit record of its own, stays open as running with `native_state: "unknown"` and a message that says cancel ends it. It is never recorded as failed. W7's ssh adapter and relay's `local` adapter both follow this.
- **Local batch jobs are named and leave a record (W6's crash finding).** psij 0.9.11's templates read `job.name`, which psij's `Job` lacks, so they never name a job. Slurm then names it `submit.sh`, and `find()` cannot reconcile a job submitted just before a crash. `LocalAdapter.name_submit` puts the name on the submit line: Slurm `--job-name=`, PBS `-N`, LSF `-J`. `mark_script` has the job script write its scheduler id to `scheduler_id` in the job directory, and `find()` reads that when the scheduler has forgotten the job. The job's exit code comes from psij's `<native_id>.ec` file. Ares has `sacct` off and `MinJobAge` 300 s, so the scheduler forgets a finished job after 5 minutes.
- **psij 0.9.11 bugs that relay works around.** `Job` has no `name`, as above. The pbspro template writes `-N="x"`, which qsub refuses. Paths are made absolute against the local machine, not the site. W7's ssh adapter renders its own scripts for the last two.
- **A resumed run must match its approval.** `run(resume=op, site=, spec=)` with a changed site or spec refuses with `approval_mismatch`, as a task's resume round does. `RelayService.same_request(record, site, spec)` holds the check.
- **A sign-in finished elsewhere closes its ask.** `advance()` resolves a pending URL ask as accepted when the adapter's `sign_in_needed()` returns `None`. This covers a device-flow sign-in finished at the provider when no one answered relay's page.
- **`describe` with no site gives each site's status (LQ2).** Each listed site has `status`: `{"state": "ready"}`, `{"state": "needs_sign_in", "provider", "detail"}` or `{"state": "unavailable", "reason", "detail"}`. It asks the adapter that serves `describe` whether a sign-in is needed. That reads local state and runs nothing remote. `src/clio_relay/surface/describing.py` holds both reports.
- **Adapters may set a site up when it is added (W10's request).** The optional protocol is `clio_relay.adapters.base.SiteAdded`, with `async on_site_added(site: SiteEntry) -> dict | None`. `RelayService.site_added(site_id)` calls it on the adapter in use and on every alternative that implements it. The command line calls it after `site add`, `add-from-catalog`, `edit` and `switch`, and prints the results under `set_up`. A typed refusal, such as no sign-in yet, is reported, and the site stays saved, so the method must be safe to run again. An adapter whose settings do not build is left out here; `describe` shows why.
- **Hooks see the config directory.** `HookContext.settings["config_dir"]` (W6's request).
- **Relay keeps watching a service (W9's requests).** While a service is not ready, its task status message is "the service is starting: <the hook's latest message>" (W9's polish request: never "running" before the port is bound; a finished service shows its final state instead). A TLS refusal or a hung start is therefore visible on `tasks/get`. When the server starts, it observes each open service once, in the background, so a service that ended while relay was down is finished and its forwards close. Any error from `ServeHook.status` is a failed look, not the service's end: the service stays open, and its message says "relay could not observe the service: <error>". The hook must keep secrets out of its exception text, as it does out of `JobStatus.message`.
- **Site skills are resources (RL34).** `relay://skills/{name}` serves each skill: the catalog's `catalog/skills/<name>.md`, or the person's own `<config_dir>/skills/<name>.md`, which replaces a shipped one of the same name. `relay://sites/{site}/skill` serves the skill a site links with its `skill` field, which is a skill name or the path of a Markdown file (a relative path is read from the config directory). Both are `text/markdown` for the assistant, listed by a FastMCP provider (`surface/skills.py`, `SkillResources`) that reads the disk on each request, so a skill linked from the command line shows without a restart. A site with no skill, or with a link relay cannot read, has no site skill resource. `describe` gives each site `skill`: `None`, `{"linked", "uri"}`, or `{"linked", "uri": None, "detail"}` when the link is broken. The server looks at the sites file and skills every 5 s and announces a changed list or an edited skill on the change bus. The server instructions name the URI, and `site add --skill` links one.
- **Package reviews are approvals, and the guard prepares what runs (W13's requests R1 to R3, RL24 and RL45).**
  - `check_run`'s decision may carry `data["asks"]`, a list of reviews. Each has a `message` and a `binding` (`kind: "package_review"`, `package`, `version`, `package_hash`, `source`). Relay turns each into an approve ask bound to the site and the spec digest, placed after the sign-in ask and before the spend ask. `advance` checks it like any approval (`surface/guarding.py`, `review_asks`).
  - `GuardHook.prepare(site, spec, *, adapter, service=False) -> JobSpec` returns the spec the adapter submits. Relay calls it in `submit` just before `adapter.run`, and with `service=True` on a service's job just before `ServeHook.start`. It never changes `record.spec`, so the approved digest stays the one the person saw. A refusal from it fails the operation. `DefaultGuard.prepare` returns the spec unchanged.
  - **A service package starts and stays up (RL45, coordinator decision R6 and R7).** W9's live run showed a service package must run `jarvis ppl start` and stop on TERM, not `ppl run`. So `prepare` takes `service: bool = False`, and `_start_service` passes `True`. `PackageRef.service: bool = False` (`jobs/spec.py`) carries it to the adapter. For a `runs_packages` adapter, W13's `prepare` sets it on the submitted copy only; `record.spec` and its digest are unchanged. W13 implements the runner side. Because a spec's digest is its full JSON dump, the new field changes the digest of every package spec. An operation opened before this change keeps its stored digest, so answering its ask still works. Only a resume that passes the package spec again recomputes the digest and refuses with `approval_mismatch`.
  - `BaseAdapter.runs_packages` (default `False`) says the adapter runs a reviewed package rather than the agent's command. Tapis sets it.
  - The entry point `guard = "clio_relay.guard:create_guard"` is registered. It falls back to `DefaultGuard` while `clio_relay.guard` is not installed.
  - The docs index links `docs/guarded-sites.md`, which W13 writes.
- **Outputs carry metadata, and a running job reports its latest step (W9's requests, RL40 and RL43).**
  - `OutputRef.metadata: dict[str, Any]` (default empty) is JSON the adapter or package attaches; relay passes it on unchanged. Two keys have a set meaning, named by constants in `jobs/handles.py`: `view` (`VIEW_METADATA`) holds the output's view descriptor (path or path pattern, format, ParaView reader and its configuration, whether it is still being written), and `provenance` (`PROVENANCE_METADATA`) holds the package version and hash and its input digests. W9's `serve.provenance` goes there.
  - `JobStatus.progress: JobProgress | None` is the latest step an adapter observed: `step`, `total`, `message`, `outputs` (what the step wrote, each with its descriptor) and `data` (adapter-owned JSON). On each observation relay stores it in the operation's `result["progress"]`, so `describe`, the operation's structured result and the final task result carry it in full.
  - The task's `statusMessage` names it: "relay operation is running: step 3 of 10; wrote snapshot 3; views: snapshots" (`surface/progress.py`). The full descriptor is not put in `tasks/get` while the task is working, because the tasks extension's `GetTaskResult` has no `_meta` (its schema forbids extra properties). A client reads the step from the operation instead. This keeps relay a plain MCP server: no client needs a relay-only field to follow a run.
- **Transient inputs never outlive their job (coordinator decision, closing W7's and W9's secret gaps, RL16).** `InputRef.transient: bool = False`. At the final state, whatever the state, each copy of a transient input goes: the adapter drops the site's blob and the job directory's copy, and relay drops its own content-store copy (`surface/transient.py`, `drop_transient`, called wherever a record becomes final). Relay keeps a blob that another open operation still lists in its `input_digests`, so two runs sharing one secret do not break each other. The `local` adapter shares relay's store and unlinks the job copy before it lists outputs, so a transient input never appears as an output. A later run that needs the same bytes stages them again, since `stage` is idempotent. A run opened between the check and the drop can find the blob gone and refuse with `input_missing`; restaging fixes it. Like `PackageRef.service`, the new field enters every spec's digest: stored operations keep their digest, and only a resume that re-sends an old spec refuses with `approval_mismatch`. W9 marks `secrets.env` transient; W7 does the ssh side.
- **Every stored output carries provenance, and view descriptors are checked (W9's RL43 request).** `RelayService._apply` passes each status through `surface/outputs.py`'s `marked_status` before it stores it. That applies to run and serve alike, and to the final outputs and the progress step's outputs. Each output gets W9's `relay.provenance.v1` block under `metadata["provenance"]` (`serve.provenance.with_provenance`), unless it brought its own block. A `metadata["view"]` descriptor is checked with `serve.descriptor.problems`. One that fails the schema is taken off the output, so no client opens a broken view, and its reasons go under `metadata["view_problems"]`.
- **On a guarded site, files delete and replace ask the person first (coordinator policy, W7 part 2).** Relay checks this in the surface, before any adapter, so it holds for every adapter. `surface/files_approval.py` has `approval_action(site, access, op, path, ...)`: on a guarded site, any `delete` asks, and a `write` asks when `overwrite` is set, the target exists (`stat`; `path_not_found` means a new file, which never asks), and it is the first chunk (offset 0) or the last (`final`). The ask is an approve ask with `operation_id=None` and the binding `{"kind": "files", "site", "op": "delete"|"replace", "path", "recursive"}`. Data and offsets stay out of the binding, so one yes covers every chunk of one upload. A pending ask with the same digest is reused rather than asked twice. After the delete or the last chunk, relay sets the ask's `completed_at`, so the yes is used once. A no refuses with `policy_refused` and leaves the file. The `files` tool asks in the call: a modern client with form elicitation gets `input_required` (its retry carries the answer, accepted only for an ask bound to the same site and path), and a legacy one gets `elicit_form`. Any other client gets `needs_approval` with the ask and its page, then calls `files` again with the same arguments. The kit's `test_guarded_files_ask_first` runs `approval_action` against each adapter's real `FileAccess`. `tests/relay_next/test_files_guarded.py` covers the tool end to end. To keep `server.py` under the 800-line cap, the elicitation helpers moved to `surface/elicit.py`, and the files tool's flow moved to `files_verb.files_tool`.
- **A site's guide is readable from tools alone (W19's live bug).** Many MCP clients never read resources, so `describe(site=...)` now returns the guide as `skill.text`, with `skill.title`. The tool result shows the guide as its own text block after the JSON. `describe()` with no site gives each linked skill `read_with: "describe(site='<id>')"`. A `files` call on a `relay://` path refuses with `invalid_arguments`, pointing to `describe(site=...)`, so the agent never gets `path_not_found` for a guide. The server instructions and the `describe` tool's description say so. The kit tests adapters, not the surface, so the check lives in `tests/relay_next/test_skills.py`. The plugin's `use-relay` skill (W14) should point to `describe(site=...)` first.
- **Shrink round 2: the old queue's layers are gone (user-directed).** `spool`, `storage_*`, `models*`, `identifiers`, `job_identity`, `remote_values`, `filesystem_paths`, `dev_mode`, `command_evidence`, `pytest_platform_partition` and the cluster file registry with its Windows ACL code are deleted, with their tests. Nothing in the new relay read them. What their callers did read now lives in three small modules:
  - `cluster_config.py`: `FrpTransportConfig`, `IdentityAnchor`, `ClusterDefinition` (name, ssh_host, frp_transport) and an in-memory `ClusterRegistry` with `require`;
  - `config.py`: `TransportMode` and `RelaySettings.from_env().frpc_bin`;
  - `session_api.py`: the owner-session and generation headers.

  The `release_platform` marker now deselects in `tests/conftest.py`, without the evidence JSON the plugin printed. src went from 1.62 MB to 1.25 MB. Pyright strict dropped from 234 errors to 0 (one psij typing gap is marked in `jobs/spec.py`), and the ratchet baseline is 0. `docs/design/relay-rework-shrink-ledger.md` lists every remaining module with its size, owner and reason.
- **SiteFiles checks before it asks the site (W10's and W11's findings).** `delete` refuses the work directory, home and `/` before it calls `kind`, since a facility may refuse to stat `/` at all. A final chunk refused with `path_exists` is checked before it joins the local part, so a resend with `overwrite` lands at the same offset.
- **`files` speed through MCP (W7's 2 to 3 s per 4 MiB chunk).** Measured on an idle compute node, a 4 MiB chunk costs relay's MCP layer 0.1 to 0.3 s. Over stdio with the plain `mcp` client, a write takes about 0.2 s and a read about 0.3 s. In memory through FastMCP, a write takes 0.17 s and a read 0.11 s. Of that, request parsing is 0.015 s, the response dump 0.010 s, base64 encoding 0.015 s and decoding 0.075 s, and no faster decoder exists in Python 3.12. W7's figure came from two other causes. The Ares login node ran at a load of 22 to 40, which slows CPU-bound work several times over. The `mcp` Python client's stdout reader also splits `buffer + chunk` again for every 64 KiB it reads, which is quadratic in the line length: 0.14 s on an idle node and about 0.9 s under load. That reader is upstream in the SDK client, not in relay. Nothing in relay needed cutting.
- **HTTP takes a full `files` chunk.** The MCP SDK's Streamable HTTP manager refuses bodies over 4 MiB with a 413, FastMCP does not expose the limit, and a 4 MiB chunk is 5.6 MB in base64. `surface/http_limits.py` raises relay's own limit to 8 MiB (twice `CHUNK_BYTES`) and fails loudly if the SDK stops limiting where relay expects. Tests: a 4 MiB write and read over HTTP, and a body over the limit still answered with 413.
- **No pipe outlives its loop.** A core run failed once on an unraisable `_UnixSubprocessTransport` that the garbage collector closed after its loop was gone. `run_bounded` now closes the process transport in its `finally`, on the caller's loop, after a cancel or a timeout kill. A test runs a cancelled call under `asyncio.run` and collects garbage afterwards with an unraisable hook in place. Core passed twice afterwards on Ares with unraisable warnings as errors.
- **Contributor proof: a fresh agent wrote an adapter from the docs alone.** A subagent with no prior context was allowed to read only the README, CONTRIBUTING, `docs/` without `docs/design/`, the template, and the docstrings of `adapters/base.py` and the kit. It built `relay-kiln`, an adapter for a pretend facility with a pasted API key, `describe`, `exec` and `files`, a 2 MiB upload cap and no append, plus an HTTP fake of it. Result: the kit at 19 passed with 0 failed and 0 skipped (24 with its own tests), ruff and pyright strict clean, and `clio-relay adapters` listing `{"kiln": ["describe", "exec", "files"]}` from the entry point. It took about 25 minutes, and the adapter is 222 lines. What it got stuck on, and what changed:
  - `exec` had no written contract: the result's fields, time limit against refusal, the per-stream output bound, `on_stall`'s arguments and cancelling. `adding-an-adapter.md` gained an "Exec" section, and `base.py` now exports `STALL_S` and says the same in brief.
  - The template's copy step failed, because `[tool.uv.sources]` points at `../..`, and the doc's test count was stale. The doc now says to repoint or drop the table, and gives no count.
  - The `SiteFiles` primitives had no signatures, and the two kind vocabularies (`Kind` against `FileEntry.kind`) were undocumented. Both are now in a table, along with when a primitive may raise `path_not_found`, and the upload cap (the whole file, checked at each chunk; `upload_hint` is appended to relay's message).
  - Test secrets needed `clio_relay.identity.store.TokenStore` and `CLIO_RELAY_KEYRING=memory`, which are outside the contract. The kit gained `use_memory_keyring(monkeypatch, secrets)`, and the template uses it.
  - Kit tests for verbs left out pass after checking the refusal, which looks like more coverage than it is. The doc and the kit's docstring now say so.
  - The kit snippet had no imports and no `facility_url` fixture; the doc now shows a whole module.
  - The template shipped `clio-relay[testing]` as a runtime dependency, and the fake's `fastapi` and `uvicorn` came only through relay. These now sit in a `dev` dependency group, and the template sets `asyncio_default_fixture_loop_scope`.
  - The doc told contributors to skip real-site tests, against CONTRIBUTING's no-skip rule. It now says what CONTRIBUTING says.
  - Smaller items: the `clio-relay adapters` comment, a local-path `--with`, `acme-dev` for `myfacility`, `scheduler="none"`, and the "(RL6)" and "(RL7)" tags in docstrings.
  - Contract points left as they are, for whoever extends it next: the config type is declared twice (the generic parameter and `Config`); `max_upload_bytes` is a class attribute, so a per-site cap needs a subclass; `AdapterContext.secret` reads the global keyring rather than an injected store; and the kit has no "not applicable" outcome.
  - Recheck at 0b3fda0e, same agent and the same reading rules: 9 of the 11 points fixed and 2 partly. The template has no `exec` to copy, and pytest still shows tests for verbs left out as plain passes. Following "Start from the template" literally gave 19 passed, and kiln moved to `use_memory_keyring` and the documented `on_stall` arguments and passed 24.
  - The last two points, closed. The kit now collects tests only for the verbs in `implements`: a subclass names its adapter class in `adapter`, a test tagged with the verbs it needs is not collected when none of them is implemented, and each verb left out gets one `test_<verb>_is_left_out` that checks the typed refusal. Deselecting reads more plainly than a "not applicable" result, and it keeps the repository's rule that a skip is a failure. A `Test*` subclass without `adapter` fails at import with a message saying what to set. Delete and chunked writes are now required of `files` (every adapter has both), so their silent `verb_not_implemented` escapes are gone, along with the unused `whole_writes_only` and `no_delete` helpers. The template's `AcmeAdapter` implements `exec` over a command API in the fake (start, poll for exit and output sizes, read the first bytes of a stream, stop), with the time limit, output bound, stall reports and cancel from the Exec section. The kit's target and polling helpers moved to `testing/conformance/target.py` so the kit stays under 500 lines. Evidence on Ares at 82119a0f: ruff, format and pyright 0 (core and template); the template kit 19 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped, now with exec tests and `test_serve_is_left_out`; the core suite 546/0/0; the ratchets OK with the src floor raised to 92.81.
  - Second recheck at 82119a0f, same agent: both points fixed. It copied the template's `exec` almost line for line into a rebuilt kiln (about 5 minutes). The template copy gave 19 passed with `test_serve_is_left_out`; kiln's kit gave 14 passed with `test_run_is_left_out` and `test_serve_is_left_out` and none of the job tests. Its two new snags, both fixed: the template's `addopts = "-q"` cancelled `pytest -v`, so it is gone, and the guide now says why the adapter is named twice (the class for collection, the registered name for building a site).
- **R1: an agent finds a package by name (W17's demo gap).** A demo prompt may not carry a package's SHA-256. Relay now keeps a package catalog at `<state>/packages/<sha256>.json`, one entry per archive staged with `python -m clio_relay.packages stage <repo_dir> <repository>.<package>`. The command builds the archive, puts it in the content store, and records the name, version, digest, source (repository, tag, commit, path), the contract's kind and summary, and the parameters. `list` prints the catalog. The version comes from `--version`, else the contract's version, else the source tag; with none, it refuses. The parameters come from the package's `_configure_menu`, read statically, refined by the contract's `parameters` schema. `describe(site=...)` returns the newest version of each as `packages`, with whether that site has reviewed it. `PackageRef.version` and `sha256` are optional now: `run` resolves a name (and a version, if given) to the newest staged archive in `apply_defaults`, before the digest and the guard, so the review and the approval still pin one archive. A newly staged version after a review asks again (`approval_mismatch`), which is intended. jarvis_site's old `archive --stage` CLI is gone; refusals point at the new command. The site's own JARVIS repository (RL42) is not scanned: what runs on a guarded site is what the relay host reviewed, so the relay host's catalog is the one list.
- **R2: live progress and outputs for any adapter's package job.** `surface/package_progress.py` reads the job's JARVIS records through the adapter's `files()`: `<job_dir>/.relay-jarvis/shared/*/executions/<newest>/{progress,artifacts}/*.jsonl`, the last 64 KiB of each. The newest progress line becomes `result.progress` (step, total, message), and the artifact lines become outputs with absolute paths, state, format and the `metadata.view` descriptor. It runs in `ServiceSurface.refresh` for every adapter that gives a `job_dir`, so ssh, worker and the rest share it; any failure leaves the adapter's status as it was. The execution found is cached on the record.
- **R3: a finished job is not shown as running.** On Ares the record was right; Slurm accounting is off there, so `sacct` tells nothing and the ssh adapter's own `final` file is what ends a job. What misled the agent was the surface: `_apply` dropped `status.message` for runs, `updated_at` moves only on a state change (it times the submit grace), and `refresh` and the stdout tail swallowed errors, so a failed look left the old answer standing with no sign. Now every look sets `result.observed_at` and `native_state`, the message is kept (or cleared when the adapter gives none), and a failed look keeps the state but says "relay could not observe the job: ..." with `observation_failed_at`. The text summary of a job that is not final says "relay last looked at <time>; step N of M; <note>", and the task status message uses the same note.
- **R1 to R3 live on Ares through MCP (protocol 2026-07-28, `clio-relay serve` over stdio, plain `mcp` client).** Guarded ssh site `ares` with spend asks and W17's `jarvis-site`. Staged `clio_jarvis.xcompact3d` 0.2.1 from clio-jarvis d1f17084 (sha256 ad76ecc3...). `describe(site="ares")` listed it with its summary and parameter schema. `run` with only `{"name": "clio_jarvis.xcompact3d", "parameters": {...}}` asked the review (naming the digest and source) and the spend question, and then submitted op_d1c51958e3818b6f36849a89 as Slurm job 27864 (Taylor-Green, 4 ranks, 400 steps, 10 snapshots). Every `describe(job=...)` while it ran gave `result.progress` from step 2 of 10 to 10 of 10, the `snapshots` output at `.../executions/jarvis_977d.../shared/pkg/run/data.bp5` with its ADIOS2VTXReader view (`growing: true`), and a fresh `observed_at`. The job's stdout last changed at 22:19:22.58; the next look, at 22:19:29, gave `completed` with exit 0, the snapshots `finalized` at 131,898,675 bytes, and `growing: false`.
- **The server instructions name the sites and packages (W17's dry run).** Asked to "run Xcompact3d on Ares", the agent searched its plugins, found nothing, and began building Xcompact3d on the login node, because nothing it had read tied the request to relay. The MCP `instructions` that `initialize` and `server/discover` return are now composed from the sites file and the package catalog (`surface/instructions.py`): the general text, then "Compute sites configured for relay: ares (ssh, slurm, guarded)", then "Packages staged to run on them: clio_jarvis.xcompact3d 0.2.1 (the first sentence of its summary)", then a line saying to use relay's `describe` and `run` instead of building or launching by hand. Each list stops at 12, with "and N more (describe lists them all)", and a summary at 100 characters. It is plain MCP, so any client that shows instructions to its model gets it; nothing is CLIO-specific. The lifespan computes it before the first client connects, and a task refreshes it every 5 s, so a site added or a package staged while relay runs reaches the next `initialize` or `discover` (Streamable HTTP builds each `initialize` result fresh). A sites file relay cannot read gives "No compute sites are configured yet" rather than failing the server. Live on Ares with the real ssh site and the staged xcompact3d: a plain `mcp` client got the site and package in `instructions` over stdio at 2025-11-25 and at 2026-07-28 and over Streamable HTTP; on a running HTTP server, `clio-relay site add lab` showed `lab (local, open)` to the next client within 6 s.
- **Slurm's `%j` in a job's paths (W18's run, Slurm 27873).** A `run` with `stdout_path: "hostname-%j.out"` wrote a file named literally `hostname-%j.out`. sbatch expands its filename patterns only in `--input`, `--output` and `--error`, and PSI/J's Slurm template points those at `/dev/null` and passes the paths to its launcher, which redirects with the shell. The ssh adapter now puts a placeholder in the launch command for a stdin, stdout or stderr path holding a pattern, and after rendering writes it back as a shell word the batch shell expands (`adapters/ssh/slurm_paths.py`): `%j` from `SLURM_JOB_ID`, `%x` `SLURM_JOB_NAME`, `%u` `id -un`, `%N` `SLURMD_NODENAME`, `%A` and `%a` the array ids (as sbatch, the job id and 4294967294 outside an array), `%%` a percent sign; padded forms such as `%3a` stay as they are. Paths without a pattern render exactly as before, and PBS and the detached mode are unchanged. Live on Ares through MCP (plain `mcp` client, `clio-relay serve` over stdio, open ssh site with `scheduler: slurm`): op_0db66ea83a5bf74c416fb1b9, Slurm 27874, `hostname` with `stdout_path: "hostname-%j.out"` and `stderr_path: "hostname-%j.err"` completed with exit 0; the job directory held `hostname-27874.out` (`ares-comp-10`) and `hostname-27874.err`, and `describe(job=)` listed both.
- **A package's contract sets its walltime and layout (W3's run, sess_e00dc1a7bb3c).** The spend ask for `clio_jarvis.xcompact3d` with `walltime: 1800` said the job "sets no walltime": relay pinned the package but never read the contract's `resources`, so the price, the ask and the Slurm script all lacked the walltime and the process layout. The catalog entry now keeps the contract's `resources` (`PackageEntry.resources`), and `apply_defaults` evaluates them against the run's parameters, with schema defaults for omitted ones (`packages.contract_value`, as clio-jarvis evaluates `{param}` and `div`, `mul`, `add`, `ceil_div`). The job's own fields win, then the contract, then the site's defaults. Walltime and queue fill one by one; nodes, processes and processes per node fill only when the job sets none of them. A value that resolves to nothing, an empty queue or a non-whole count is left out. An entry staged before this is read again from its archive in the content store on first use, and saved. Generic: no package names in relay.
- **A deleted review stayed withdrawn only until the next look (W17's take 4, relay c06334e7).** W17 moved `state/guard/reviews/ares` aside and the next xcompact3d run on guarded ares went straight to the spend ask. `Guard.reviewed` harvests accepted `package_review` asks when no review file is found, and `harvest` read every accepted ask in `state/asks`, so the old answers (ask_5f495792 for 0.2.1, ask_83cb60d7 for 0.2.0) rewrote both files at 05:33. That broke the documented rule that deleting the file withdraws the review. `ReviewStore.harvest` now records only asks whose operation is still open (not final): that is the step between the answer and `prepare`, the only place the harvest is needed, and a finished run's yes is never read again. - **JARVIS submits package jobs on ssh Slurm, not relay (user-directed).** The user: relay calling `sbatch` itself "breaks the fundamental semantics of all the safeguards inside of jarvis." The ssh adapter now hands a package job on Slurm to `jarvis ppl submit` (jarvis-cd 1.8.1), so JARVIS makes the sealed execution directory (input, runtime, shared, private), the typed `.jarvis-execution.json` record and the allocation hostfile, creates the log directories before the submit, and binds the interpreter and `JARVIS_ROOT`. The old package path is gone: `jarvis_site.RUNNER` (the hostfile built from `SLURM_JOB_NODELIST`, `jarvis hostfile set`, `ppl create`/`append`, `ppl run`, the service hold loop) and `jarvis_site.package_job` are deleted, and ssh renders no PSI/J script for a package. The spend ask and the guard's review and refusals run before the submit, as before.
  - **One shared module, `clio_relay.jarvis_submit`, owned by W1 and used by ssh directly and by W10's Tapis FORK jobs** (W10's design, "Shared with W1"). It holds the paths (`ARCHIVE_DIR`, `JARVIS_DIR`, `pipeline_id`, `jarvis_root`, `execution_dir`, `record_path`, the site files `relay.pipeline.yaml`, `relay-jarvis.sh`, `relay.submission.json`, `relay.refusal.json`, `relay.exitcode`, `relay.jarvis`), the pipeline rendering (`slurm_time`, `scheduler_block`, `pipeline_document`, `render_pipeline`), the login-host `SITE_SCRIPT` and its arguments (`site_arguments`, `submit_command`), the exit codes (64 usage, 65 digest, 66 missing, 69 no jarvis), the execution command lines (`execution_argv` and `execution_shell` for `execution get`, `progress`, `artifacts` and `service-runtimes`), the parsers (`parse_handle`, `parse_document`, `record_status`, `artifact_outputs`) and the refusals (`missing_jarvis`, `cancel_refusal`, `stop_refusal`). `direct_job` lowers a package for adapters that neither run packages nor have a scheduler: `SITE_SCRIPT run`, which is `jarvis ppl run yaml`.
  - **The pipeline.** `name: relay_<operation id>`, then `scheduler:` with `name: slurm`, `job_name`, `nodes`, `ntasks_per_node`, `time` (the resolved walltime as Slurm `[D-]HH:MM:SS`, quoted by YAML when it would read as a number), `partition`, `account`, and `output`/`error` only when the job sets `stdout_path`/`stderr_path` (else JARVIS defaults them to `<execution>/stdout.log` and `stderr.log` and registers them as artifacts). Then `pkgs:` with the package as `pkg` and its parameters. JARVIS's pipeline YAML has no inline `env`, and it captures the environment at submit, so the job's environment goes as `env K=V` before the script. The execution id is relay's operation id.
  - **Status, outputs and cancel read JARVIS.** `status` runs `jarvis execution get` in the job's JARVIS root and maps the record's state (`record_status`); once it is terminal, outputs come from `jarvis execution artifacts`. Relay keeps no job tracking of its own for these jobs. The ssh `jarvis` setting (default `jarvis`) names the command; it matches Tapis's field. The guard's `SiteGuard.jarvis` now applies only to `direct_job` lowering for adapters without `runs_packages`; ssh sets `runs_packages = True`.
  - **Proof, rendered locally (no tests, nothing on a cluster, as directed).** A 4-node by 32-rank `clio_jarvis.xcompact3d` 0.2.2 run (`process_count` 128, `processes_per_node` 32, `duration` 1800, queue `compute`, account `lab`, operation `op_x`) gives `relay.pipeline.yaml`: `name: relay_op_x`, `scheduler: {name: slurm, job_name: relay-op_x, nodes: 4, ntasks_per_node: 32, time: 00:30:00, partition: compute, account: lab}`, `pkgs: [{pkg_type: clio_jarvis.xcompact3d, pkg_name: pkg, case: tgv, nx: 257, ny: 257, nz: 257}]`; the submit, from the job directory, is `bash relay-jarvis.sh submit <sha256> clio_jarvis.xcompact3d jarvis op_x`, which runs `JARVIS_ROOT=<job>/.relay-jarvis jarvis ppl submit <job>/relay.pipeline.yaml --execution-id op_x +json`. Status is `jarvis execution get op_x --pipeline-id relay_op_x +json`. ruff, format and pyright (Linux platform) are clean.
  - **What JARVIS 1.8.1 cannot express, so relay refuses it (the user decides; no jarvis-cd change, no workaround).** (1) No execution cancel: `cancel` of a running package job is `not_cancellable`, `needs: "jarvis execution cancel"`; relay runs no `scancel`. (2) No execution stop: a package service on ssh (pvserver, RL45) is `adapter_unavailable`, `needs: "jarvis execution stop"`, and `submit` has no place for relay's connector (frpc, `ready.json`, idle release). (3) Cleanup is Python API only, so execution roots stay on the site. (4) Slurm only on ssh: PBS, LSF and Cobalt package jobs are `adapter_unavailable`, `needs: "a JARVIS <scheduler> scheduler"`, which takes back Polaris's RL45 and LQ-L13 path. (5) The multi-node hostfile for a direct run (no scheduler) is gone; JARVIS's `ppl run` uses its default. (6) A package job's `directory` and `stdin_path` are refused (`invalid_spec`); inputs land in relay's job directory, not JARVIS's sealed one, so package parameters need absolute paths. (7) A direct-run service (guard lowering on adapters without `runs_packages`) runs `ppl run` and ends on `TERM` with no clean `ppl stop`. The RL45 service tail in the W13 and W10 bullets below describes the deleted runner.
    - **Integrated at the coordinator's request; what comes next.** A jarvis-cd session is adding `execution cancel`, `execution stop` (TERM trap, then `ppl stop`, then finalize), a find-first Spack installer and `ppl install [--check]` on grc-iit/jarvis-cd `jaimecernuda-execution-lifecycle`. When that lands, gaps 1 and 2 are wired to those commands; until then their typed refusals stay. Gaps 3 to 7 keep their refusals for now. Which of them the fluid demo (Xcompact3d plus pvserver on Ares, V1 to V4, over ssh and worker) hits:
      - **3, no cleanup command: not hit.** The demo lists the run's artifacts after it ends, and leaving the execution root keeps them readable. Disk on the site grows with each run.
      - **4, Slurm only on ssh: not hit.** Ares is Slurm.
      - **5, no hostfile for a direct run: hit, on V2 and V4 (worker).** The worker adapter runs packages through the on-site `local` adapter, which has no `runs_packages`. So the guard lowers the package to `direct_job` (`jarvis ppl run`), and `local` submits it with PSI/J `sbatch`. That is relay running `ppl run` inside an allocation, with no hostfile: the run is single-node, and the demo's rule that every job goes through `jarvis ppl submit` is broken. `local` (and so `worker`) needs the same `jarvis ppl submit` path as ssh through `jarvis_submit`. This change converted ssh only.
      - **6, `directory` and `stdin_path` refused, inputs in relay's job directory: hit, on every take.** The STL goes up through relay. As a run input it lands in `<job dir>`, not in JARVIS's sealed execution, so the package parameter must name its absolute path. Uploading it with `files` to a fixed absolute site path, then passing that path, works today.
      - **7, a lowered service has no clean `ppl stop`: hit, on V2 and V4.** pvserver over worker would go through `direct_job` and end on `TERM` only. On ssh, a package service is refused outright (gap 2). Both are resolved by `execution stop`, together with the `local` conversion above.
- **Local and worker submit packages through JARVIS too; the guard never lowers a package (coordinator decision, item 5 and item 6).** Gaps 5 and 7 are closed: no adapter runs `ppl run` inside an allocation any more.
  - `LocalAdapter` and `WorkerAdapter` now have `runs_packages = True` and a `jarvis` setting (default `jarvis`). On Slurm, `local` writes `relay.pipeline.yaml` with its `scheduler:` key and `relay-jarvis.sh`, runs `jarvis ppl submit` through `jarvis_submit.submit_command`, keeps JARVIS's handle in `relay.submission.json`, and reads `status` and outputs from `jarvis execution get` and `artifacts`, exactly as ssh does, with the same refusals (exit 69 `missing_jarvis`, `cancel_refusal`, `stop_refusal`, `scheduler_refusal` for PBS, LSF and Cobalt). `find` reads `relay.submission.json` first, so a retried `run` never submits twice. With no scheduler, `local` runs `jarvis_submit.direct_job` (JARVIS's direct run) under its supervisor on the machine itself, which is not an allocation. `worker` stages the archive and hands the package to the on-site `local`, so worker gets the same path; its `job_dir` hook lets `describe(job=...)` show package progress.
  - The guard's `prepare` no longer turns a package into a command job. It hands the reviewed package unchanged to an adapter that runs packages, and otherwise refuses with `jarvis_submit.not_run_here` (`adapter_unavailable`, `needs: "jarvis ppl submit through the <adapter> adapter"`). The surface makes the same check in `_open` right after building the spec, so on globus, iri and sky a package is refused before any review or spend ask. Relay's DefaultGuard (no guard installed) passes the spec through, and the adapter runs it the same way.
  - Package services on `local` and `worker` are refused with `stop_refusal`, as on ssh, until `jarvis execution stop` lands.
  - Item 6: `pipeline_document(..., job_dir=)` turns a parameter whose value is a run input's name into that input's absolute path in the job directory (`jarvis_submit.input_paths`); the package archive is never one of them. It applies at render time only, so the spec, its digest and its approval do not change. ssh and local pass their job directory; `job_dir` is optional, so W10's Tapis calls keep working until they pass it.
  - `WORKER_PROTOCOL` is 3: the worker's site config now always carries `jarvis`, which an older worker's `LocalConfig` (extra forbidden) would reject, so the next sign-in replaces the placed worker.
  - The fixture adapter simulates package jobs (`runs_packages = True`), so the conformance kit and the surface tests can open a package run against it.
- **Relay follows jarvis-cd's execution lifecycle, installs and pipelines (coordinator, jarvis-cd `jaimecernuda-execution-lifecycle` at 811dde80).** Branch `jaimecernuda-relay-rework-jarvis-lifecycle`, off 805e09f9. No tests, nothing on a cluster; ruff, format and pyright (Linux) are clean. The pin is `jarvis_submit.JARVIS_PIN` = `811dde8072dc2267080dc013e69110a9670c1baa` (8adbe5db, then f3cb10a1, then 811dde80, as the coordinator moved it).
  - **Gaps 1 and 2 are closed.** `cancel` runs `jarvis execution cancel <id> --pipeline-id relay_<op> +json`, and the new adapter verb `stop` runs `jarvis execution stop` (a pending job falls back to cancel in JARVIS). Stopping a package service goes through `stop` on ssh, local and worker; the `cancel_refusal` and `stop_refusal` refusals are deleted. `record_status` reads `metadata.termination` and `metadata.cancel_request` / `metadata.stop_request` into the new `JobStatus.control`, and its message says what was asked and how it ended. Relay still never runs `scancel`. `BaseAdapter` gains `stop`, `install` and `install_jarvis`, each `verb_not_implemented` by default.
  - **The install flow.** The site script runs the submit with `JARVIS_INSTALL_ON_LOAD=check`. Exit 3 means sbatch never ran: the adapter keeps JARVIS's `jarvis.install-report.v1` and refuses with `jarvis_submit.install_needed` (`needs: "jarvis ppl install"`, the report in `install_report`). The surface (`surface/installing.py`) turns that into asks on the existing ask provider: first one approval bound to the site, the spec digest and the report's digest (`report_digest`: installers and issues, no paths), saying what is reused and what is built; then one `choose` ask per answerable issue, with its candidates. An issue no answer fixes fails the run with `install_blocked`. Once all are answered, a `submitting` fence starts `adapter.install`, which runs `jarvis ppl install +submit --execution-id <op>-install --answers relay.answers.json` (answers are `{issue_id: {constraint, answered_by, ask_id}}`). The operation follows the install job with no time limit: `describe(job=...)` tails `<execution>/install/install.log` through `files` and shows `metadata.install` progress, including `waiting_env_lock` as the install's state while another install holds the env. On `completed` relay submits the run itself; another final state fails or cancels it, with the log's tail. At most three install rounds (`MAX_ROUNDS`). `cancel` during the install cancels the install job.
  - **JARVIS on the site.** When the site's `jarvis` setting points to nothing (exit 69), `missing_jarvis` is installable: relay asks once, then `adapter.install_jarvis()` runs `jarvis_submit.BOOTSTRAP_SCRIPT`, which makes a venv from the site's `python3` (3.11 or newer) under `<CLIO site home>/jarvis-cd/` and installs jarvis-cd from the pinned commit's GitHub archive, falling back to `pip install --user`, and links `<home>/jarvis-cd/jarvis`. The site script then uses that link when the setting finds nothing (`CLIO_RELAY_JARVIS_FALLBACK`). It needs the site to reach github.com. An adapter without `install` or `install_jarvis` (Tapis today) gets the plain refusal, with no ask. A bootstrap or install-job start that a crash interrupts fails the run after six hours (`START_GRACE`). Superseded: relay now runs only its own pinned jarvis, with no PATH lookup and no `--user` fallback (see "Relay runs only its own pinned jarvis"), and the bootstrap finds a Python 3.11 or newer itself (see "A clean site, end to end").
  - **Pipelines: `JobSpec.packages` (coordinator decision a).** An ordered list of `PackageRef`; `package` stays the one-entry shorthand, and `JobSpec.steps()` is the one way to read either. Each entry is guard-reviewed and digest-pinned on its own (one review ask per unreviewed archive); all share one `scheduler:` block. `host_slice` (a Python slice of the allocation's hosts, such as `"0:12"`) and `start_mode` pass through as package parameters. With no layout in the job, `node_count` is the largest slice end; the walltime is the longest contract duration and the queue the first one. Two steps from one repository must come from the same commit (`pipeline_conflict`). The spend ask lists the steps and which nodes each gets, and `describe` shows `steps` (package, version, sha256, nodes, service). Tapis runs one step only and refuses more (`verb_not_implemented`).
  - **Decisions and the Spack env are kept per set of archives.** The job's JARVIS root and pipeline are per operation, so `site_environment(spec, home)` keys both on `steps_digest` (the sorted package digests): `<home>/jarvis-cd/pipelines/<digest>/decisions.yaml` as `JARVIS_SPACK_DECISIONS` and `<digest>/spack-env` as `JARVIS_SPACK_ENV_DIR`. The site script exports both before `ppl submit` or `ppl install`, so the job inherits them; a repeat run reuses the `spack.lock` instead of concretizing again, and JARVIS's flock on `.jarvis-env.lock` serializes installs into it.
  - **Proof, rendered locally.** pvserver on nodes 0:12 and Xcompact3d on 12:16, 16 nodes by 32 ranks, 30 minutes, operation `op_x`: `pkgs:` is `builtin.paraview` (`host_slice: 0:12`, `start_mode: background`) then `clio_jarvis.xcompact3d` (`host_slice: '12:16'`, quoted because YAML 1.1 reads it as base 60), under `scheduler: {nodes: 16, ntasks_per_node: 32, time: 00:30:00, ...}` with the connector in `pre_cmds`. The submit is `env CLIO_RELAY_JARVIS_FALLBACK=~/.clio-relay/jarvis-cd/jarvis CLIO_RELAY_JARVIS_DECISIONS=.../pipelines/b1da1a17.../decisions.yaml CLIO_RELAY_JARVIS_SPACK_ENV=.../spack-env bash relay-jarvis.sh submit jarvis op_x builtin.paraview@<sha>,clio_jarvis.xcompact3d@<sha>`, and the install is the same with `install jarvis op_x-install ... '<answers>'`.
  - **Interfaces W10 shares.** The site script's arguments are `mode jarvis execution steps [answers] [pipeline]`; `submit_command`, `install_command` and `site_environment` take `home=`; `execution_argv` / `execution_shell` take `pipeline=` and the verbs `cancel` and `stop`; `bootstrap_command` and `bootstrap_result`; `scheduler_block(job_dir=)`; `with_archive` and `archive_inputs` cover every step.
  - **Limits and notes.** The serve connector ran on the allocation's first node in cd948939; the site-facts change below moves it to the service step's first slice host. A port with `secret_env:` protection is refused for a package service. A staged package named `builtin.paraview` shadows JARVIS's own builtin in the job's root. sbatch gets no `--export`, so Slurm's default (ALL) carries the exported environment. `WORKER_PROTOCOL` is 4 (new `/stop`, `/install` and `/install-jarvis` routes), so the next sign-in replaces a placed worker. Of gaps 3 to 7: 3 (no cleanup) and 4 (Slurm only) stay refused; 5 and 7 were closed in 805e09f9; 6 was closed by input paths.
- **Site facts, layout limits, the resolve pin, the CLIO Spack and jarvis-cd 96283b3f (design §8; branch `jaimecernuda-relay-rework-site-facts` off cd948939).** No tests and nothing on a cluster, as directed; ruff, format and pyright (Linux platform) are clean. Tapis items stay paused.
  - **The serve connector runs on the service step's first host (coordinator check on cd948939).** `jarvis_submit.service_step(spec)` is the first step marked `service`, and `slice_start` reads its `host_slice` start (a negative start counts from the end; none means 0). Service steps that start on different hosts are refused (`invalid_spec`), since one connector serves one host. With start 0 the connector is a background process in the batch script, as before. Otherwise `connector_command` resolves host `start` from `scontrol show hostnames "$SLURM_JOB_NODELIST"`, the same list JARVIS slices, and runs `srun --overlap --nodes=1 --ntasks=1 --nodelist=<host>` around `connect.sh`, a job step in JARVIS's own allocation started from `pre_cmds`. `connect.sh`'s liveness check (`BATCH=$PPID`) then watches slurmstepd, which lives as long as the step, and `ready.json` names the slice host, so the ssh forward and frpc's `127.0.0.1` both reach pvserver's rank 0. A missing host fails the connector with a message, not the job. Proof (local render), Xcompact3d on `0:12` and pvserver on `12:16` of 16 nodes: `(h=$(scontrol show hostnames "$SLURM_JOB_NODELIST" | awk -v s=12 '...'); ...; exec srun --overlap --nodes=1 --ntasks=1 --nodelist="$h" sh -c 'cd <job> && exec sh serve/connect.sh op_demo 900 -') </dev/null >><job>/serve/connect.log 2>&1 &`. `GuardPolicy.prepare` now marks every step a service only when the caller marked none, so a sim step before pvserver stays a plain step. W10's Tapis package-service mode shares this.
  - **Per-partition `describe` on Slurm.** `SiteInfo.partitions` is a list of `PartitionInfo` (name, default, nodes, cpus_per_node, sockets, cores_per_socket, threads_per_core, physical_cores, memory_mb, max_time and max_time_s, allocated, idle, other, gres, features), and `SiteInfo.mpi` holds the site's MPI facts (Slurm's `MpiDefault`, `MpiParams`, `srun --mpi=list` plugins, `SelectType`, `SelectTypeParameters`, `TaskPlugin`, the `mpiexec` on the path with its version, flavour and prefix, and Open MPI's plm/ras/pmix components). `adapters/slurm_facts.py` holds the script (each read under `timeout 20`) and its parsers; rows of one partition merge (counts summed, per-node figures the smallest, features intersected). The ssh `describe` runs it on Slurm and `queues` falls back to the partition names. JARVIS's launcher picks its own MPI from Spack (`JARVIS_MPI_*`); relay only reports the site's. PBS, LSF and Cobalt report no partitions yet.
  - **Guard layout limits.** `guard/limits.py` checks a run against its partition (the named queue, else the default): nodes, `ranks_per_node` times threads per rank against physical cores (else CPUs), memory against 90% of a node's, and walltime against `max_time`. An unknown queue is `invalid_spec` with the partitions; a layout that does not fit is a refusal with `field`, the partition's `limits` and `next_step: resize`. The guard reads `describe` through a freshly built adapter, cached for 600 s per site with a 60 s timeout; any failure, or a site with no partitions, skips the check. It runs before the warm and open shortcuts.
  - **`resolve` requires a pin.** With one staged archive of a name (and version), relay pins it. With several, `run` refuses `invalid_spec`, `field: package`, with `candidates` (version, sha256, staged_at, newest first) and `next_step: pin_package`, instead of picking the newest. Both `version` and `sha256` given skip the catalog lookup.
  - **`Charge` carries the layout.** `JobSpec.sizing` (`rationale`, up to four `alternatives`, each a `Layout` of nodes, ranks_per_node, threads_per_rank, walltime_s, memory_mb, queue, note) is the agent's reason for the layout. `Charge.with_layout(spec)` adds threads per rank, memory per node, each step's nodes (`ChargeStep`, from `charge_steps`) and the sizing; the surface applies it to every price, so the spend binding's digest covers the layout. The spend ask shows threads and memory on the nodes line, then "Why" and "Alternatives".
  - **The CLIO Spack.** `site_environment` adds `CLIO_RELAY_SPACK_ROOT=<home>/spack` and `CLIO_RELAY_SPACK_CONFIG=<home>/spack-config`; the site script exports `JARVIS_SPACK_ROOT` only when `share/spack/setup-env.sh` is there (JARVIS errors otherwise) and `JARVIS_SPACK_CONFIG` only when that directory is there. The `spack-config` name is assumed until clio-kit's `spack_prepare_site` confirms it.
  - **jarvis-cd pinned to 96283b3f; exit 4 is a refusal.** A plain `ppl install` and a default-policy install-on-load refuse to build outside an allocation with exit 4 and `refused.kind: build_outside_allocation`. Every submit keeps `JARVIS_INSTALL_ON_LOAD=check`, and relay builds only with `ppl install +submit`. `jarvis_submit.build_refused` reads the last JSON line's `refused` and returns `policy_refused` with kind, what, packages, `exit_code: 4` and `retry: false`; ssh (submit and install) and local (so worker too) map exit 4 to it. An install record's `refused` phase shows as its state. Relay never passes `+allow_local`, and the site script unsets `JARVIS_ALLOW_LOCAL_BUILD`.
  - **Login-node audit (user rule: only light, short work on a login node).** Allowed and bounded: `jarvis ppl submit` under `check` (`spack spec`/`find`), `execution get|artifacts|cancel|stop`, the describe reads (`timeout 20`), the bootstrap venv, and `files` (tar and cat for the call). Builds, installs, pvserver and the connector run in JARVIS-submitted jobs. `exec` is stopped when its call ends; on an open site it runs whatever the agent asks for that long. Violations, which need a user decision: (1) the worker adapter's `clio-relay worker site-serve` daemon, started with `setsid nohup` on the site host and long-lived; (2) serve's hop proxy (`serve/_site/hop_proxy.py`), started with `setsid` on the login node for sites whose compute nodes cannot reach the rendezvous, which outlives its command until `HOP_IDLE_EXIT_S` of idle. Conditional: ssh's no-scheduler direct mode (`supervise.sh` and `child.sh` under `setsid`, and `jarvis_submit.direct_job`) runs detached on the ssh host, which is a login node only when `scheduler: none` is forced or detection fails on a batch site.
  - **Login-node hardening (coordinator's decision while the user is away; the user reviews it).** Worker site-serve and the hop proxy stay on the login node, hardened. `login_load.py` holds the shared pieces. `clio_light` runs a command at `nice -n 19` and `ionice -c3` with `MALLOC_ARENA_MAX=2`. It uses `systemd-run --user --scope -p CPUQuota=25% -p MemoryMax=512M` only when `loginctl` shows the user lingering and a probe scope works, because without linger `user@.service` ends with the last session and takes every `--user` scope with it. Otherwise it uses `ulimit -S -v` (2 GiB, above the memory cap because it counts address space) and `ulimit -S -t` (7200 CPU seconds a process). `LoginLimits` (`login_limits` on the worker config and on serve's site settings) changes them.
    - Site-serve: one per user and site through an `flock` on `run/site.lock` (a second exits naming the holder). It exits after `idle_exit_s` (1800 by default, 0 for never) with no HTTP request; relay has no keepalive, and exec polls are long polls, so they count. It writes `run/stopped.json` (`reason` idle or stopped) and removes `ready.json`. frpc gets `PR_SET_PDEATHSIG`, so it dies with site-serve. After an idle exit the link fails and `_connection` refuses `needs_sign_in`, which places the worker again. `WORKER_PROTOCOL` is 5.
    - Hop: one per user and site in `<hop_dir>/<site>` (`~/.clio-relay/hop`), with modes `start`, `lease` and `release`. The daemon holds `run.lock` for its life; `start` holds `start.lock` and reuses a running hop that lets the same target through. Each serve job holds `leases/<op>.json`: `start_for` leases it after the submit, `_handle` adds the job directory when it first learns it, and `_finish` releases it. A lease ends on `serve/stopped.json`, a `serve/heartbeat` older than 180 s (`connect.sh` and `launch.sh` touch it about every 30 s), a job that left the queue before it started (only one squeue showed by its `relay-<op>` name when leased, after 120 s), no job directory after 900 s, or 24 h queued. The hop exits when no lease is live, and on its idle exit only when every live lease has started.
    - Direct mode: `auto` never falls back to a no-scheduler run on a batch host. ssh's `_detect` adds `BATCH_HINT_SH` (PATH, a login shell, `slurm.conf`, PBS and LSF files, `$SLURM_CONF`); a missing `@@scheduler` answer is `unreachable`, an unknown name `invalid_config`, and hints with no scheduler are `invalid_config` with `batch_hints` and `next_step: set_scheduler`. `local` (so worker on the site) does the same through `batch_hints()`. Only an explicit `scheduler: none` runs jobs on that host.
    - Risks: a site-serve child (`jarvis ppl submit`, `spack spec`) inherits the caps, and `MemoryMax=512M` covers the whole scope, so a large concretization can be killed (raise `login_limits.memory_mb`); the 7200 s CPU cap could end a very long-lived hop; after an idle exit the next call asks for a sign-in; a serve job canceled while queued, after relay went away and never saw it in the queue, keeps the hop up to 24 h.
- **Both frp modes on the worker link (user's finish line: three videos, ssh, frp brokered, frp NAT bypass).** The worker adapter's `link_mode` picks the control link: `brokered_tcp` (frp `stcp`, through the rendezvous, the default) or `udp_rendezvous` (frp `xtcp`, a UDP hole punch with `stun_server`). Pick it on the Compute page: Protocol `worker` in the site's row, then the "link mode" choice in the worker's add or edit form, which renders the worker config schema; or set `link_mode` in `sites.yaml`. A change takes effect at the next sign-in. `udp_rendezvous` is strict: a punch that fails is `punch_failed`, never a silent fall back to `stcp`. Serve's own `direct` (xtcp) keeps its reported fall back to `stcp`, since a viewer link is not the control link.
- **`shutdown` (user-directed: "relay processes die with clio on ask").** `clio_relay.shutdown`, the `shutdown` tool and `clio-relay shutdown [--site S | --all] +json`, exactly the coordinator's contract: `{"all": true}` or `{"site": ...}`, optional `timeout_s` (15, at most 120), result `relay.shutdown.v1` with `stopped`, `still_running` and `failures`, kinds site_serve, hop_proxy, connector, tunnel, frpc, local_server and job. Sites run in parallel, each under the timeout. Desktop ports and the login hop stop first, through serve's `shutdown_site` over the site's exec adapter.
