Adapters that hold processes implement the optional `ShutsDown`: ssh closes its signed-in control connection; worker asks site-serve to exit (frpc dies with it). Local forwards (frpc visitors, `ssh -L`) and connectors stop from relay's own pid files; a detached `serve --http` stops last, after the reply. Jobs are never cancelled: each live run or serve is `kind: job` in `still_running`. An unreachable site is a failure entry, never an exception.
  - Fix (close-relays report against 76dcbef4): a host that runs plugin servers sandboxed under another identity cannot see, through `tasklist`, a `serve --http` or connector started outside the sandbox. `_is_relay` read that as "not relay", so the record was deleted, nothing was stopped, and nothing was reported. Now `_probe` answers relay, other, gone or unknown. Windows opens the process handle (`OpenProcess` with limited query rights, then its exit code and image name) in place of `tasklist`, and POSIX uses `kill(pid, 0)`. A record is dropped only when its process is stopped, has exited, or is another program that took the pid. One relay cannot confirm (access denied, unreadable command line, no pid in the record) or cannot stop (taskkill's exit code is now checked, as is SIGTERM's error) keeps its record and is a `failures` entry (`unreachable`, with the reason, the record path and the pid). The contract is unchanged.
  - Stop over HTTP (coordinator, so CLIO's quit closes a server the sandbox cannot see). `register_server` now writes the record privately (`O_EXCL`, 0600) with `origin`, `tls_cert` when HTTPS, and a `stop_token` made for that process alone; the MCP bearer is never written there, since it may come only from `CLIO_RELAY_TOKEN`. It mounts `POST /relay/stop`, which checks `Authorization: Bearer <stop_token>` in constant time, answers 401 to anything else, and otherwise removes its record, answers 202 and exits a second later. Shutdown calls it only when `_probe` says `unknown`, with a 5 s timeout and no proxies, trusting the recorded certificate for HTTPS. A 200 or 202 is a `stopped` entry ("stopped through its stop route"). Anything else stays a `failures` entry whose detail joins the probe's reason and the HTTP one, and the record is kept. A record from before the route, or one this identity cannot read, is a failure that says so. Connectors have no such route; one relay cannot see is still a failure.
- **`site clean` (user-directed: every live proof redeploys).** `clio-relay site clean <site> +json` asks first (or `--yes`), refuses while a job of the site runs, stops relay's processes (`shutdown`), then removes the worker and its binaries, site-serve, relay's scripts and job directories, `~/.clio-relay`, `~/.config/clio-relay` and the JARVIS bootstrap venv, and drops relay's local adapter state for the site. `site_prune.py` never removes the home, `/` or an ancestor of the home, and spares anything under a kept path. `<site home>/spack` and `<site home>/spack-config` stay unless `--remove-spack`. Result `relay.site_clean.v1` (removed, kept, absent, refused, local_removed, shutdown).
- **jarvis-cd pinned to af290aef (was a446327c, then 4007fd14, then 9bf89f96, then c95fa922).** a446327c: `JARVIS_SPACK_ROOT` runs `<root>/bin/spack` by absolute path; `JARVIS_SPACK_CONFIG` stays exported; jobs see `JARVIS_SPACK_*` only through sbatch's default `--export=ALL`, and no relay path passes `--export=NONE`. 4007fd14: `ServiceProtocol.TCP`. 9bf89f96: config scopes in install-record.json and provenance, and `ambiguous_provider` (exit 3 before any sbatch) when more than one MPI could provide. That decision goes through the same path as every install decision: `choice_issues` reads it, the ask is a typed choice (`provider.choose`) listing each candidate with its constraint and, in parentheses, its source, modules, prefix and implies, and the answer goes back with `--answers`. A site-scope answer says JARVIS keeps it for every later run on the site. A choice-only install says JARVIS needs N choices, applied in a short job. c95fa922 (one commit on 9bf89f96, `jaimecernuda-execution-lifecycle`): `jarvis execution cancel` passes `scancel --clusters` only when `sbatch --parsable` reported a cluster, so a cancel works on a Slurm without accounting (Ares job 27935, cancelled while running). af290aef (one commit on c95fa922): an MPI candidate pins the real install (`^openmpi/cphqvsy` on Ares), not its same-prefix packages.yaml external, which stays in `external_hashes`; an answer recorded as the external no longer matches, so JARVIS asks it again with a new issue id. A site with relay's jarvis-cd at an older pin gets the existing reinstall ask.
- **Run provenance.** JARVIS writes `jarvis.provenance.v1` as the core artifact `provenance`. Its output keeps `role: provenance`, and `describe` of an operation shows `provenance: {schema, name, path}` (`surface.outputs.run_provenance`).
- **clio-jarvis pinned to aa8b3d56 (`jaimecernuda-spack-linkage`; pvserver 0.3.1 reports TCP).** The connector dials `127.0.0.1:<port>` on the service slice's first host: frpc's `localIP` is `127.0.0.1` and frpc runs in `connect.sh` on that host (`srun --overlap` when the slice does not start at 0); the ssh forward is `-L 127.0.0.1:local:127.0.0.1:remote` to that host through the login jump. Relay never reads service-runtime records, so their `protocol` field (`http`, then `tcp`) does not matter to it. An empty `ppn` takes `cores_per_node`: `run` and `serve` fill `cores_per_node` from `describe`'s `physical_cores` for the target partition (20 on Ares) for each step whose package declares it and leaves it unset (`packages.with_site_cores`; the guard's cached site facts, else `describe` under 20 s; no partition, no change). The site script used to prefer a `jarvis` on the PATH to the pinned one; the next bullet closes that.
- **Relay runs only its own pinned jarvis (coordinator).** A site jarvis 1.8.1 rejects tcp reports and skips the login-node build refusal, so the site script never looks `jarvis` up on `PATH`. It runs `<home>/jarvis-cd/jarvis` by absolute path (`CLIO_RELAY_JARVIS_FALLBACK`), and that path goes in `relay.jarvis`, which every later `execution get|artifacts|cancel|stop` reads. The `pin` file beside it must equal `CLIO_RELAY_JARVIS_PIN` (`JARVIS_PIN`). When relay's jarvis is missing or at another pin, the script writes `relay.refusal.json` and exits 69, and `missing_jarvis` offers the pinned install through the existing ask; a stale pin carries `pin`. The bootstrap makes a fresh venv each time and removes the old venv, pin and link first. It drops the `pip install --user` fallback, which could overwrite the person's own jarvis. The adapters' `jarvis` setting (ssh, local, worker) now defaults to empty, meaning relay's own. Only an absolute or `~/` path may override it (`explicit_jarvis`), and the old default `jarvis` reads as empty; a missing explicit path is refused with `explicit` and no install offer. `add_clio_jarvis` takes a path too. Fixed with it: a relative site home such as ssh's `.clio-relay` was resolved against the job directory, so the fallback, decisions, Spack env and CLIO Spack paths were wrong there. The script and the bootstrap now resolve a relative path from the home directory. Unchanged: the guard's `jarvis` read list (an agent's own `exec`), and Tapis's app script, which stays paused.
- **Catalog adds take the Security default (fluid demo finding).** Add from catalog brought Ares in Open, because `ares.yaml` (and `ares-tapis.yaml`) set `policy: open`. Both lines are gone, and `SiteStore.add_from_catalog` now gives an entry that sets no `policy` the person's default for new sites (Secure unless they changed it), as it already did for spend. Facility entries that set `policy: guarded` keep it; `chameleon-tapis.yaml` keeps its explicit `open` (a VM the person owns; paused with Chameleon). Copy: "Needs a ssh sign-in" is now "Needs an ssh sign-in". `clio_relay.wording.with_article` picks the article by sound (spelled names such as ssh, frp, IRI, HTTP by their first letter; other words by vowel), and also words the exec-plan ask ("Run an install plan") and the not-a-service refusal ("is an exec operation").
- **Spack on guarded sites (clio-kit's `spack_prepare_site` and `spack_site_query`).** The read list adds `spack find|info|spec`, bare or by a `guard.yaml` `spack` path (default `.clio-relay/spack/bin/spack` from home), with only `-E`/`--no-env`/`--color` before the subcommand and an optional `env SPACK_DISABLE_LOCAL_CONFIG=1` prefix. `-C`, `-e`, `-D` and any other config variable are refused: a config could add a repository, and `spack info` imports package code. A Spack query runs through `clio_light` with `exec_plan.PLAN_LIMITS` (100% CPU, 4 GiB, 900 CPU s) on any site. A command that is not read-only runs on a guarded site only with `plan: {kind, summary}`: relay asks once (`approve`, bound to site, plan kind and the argv's sha256), returns `needs_approval` or an in-call form while pending, then runs that exact argv once, light, for at most 900 s, and marks the ask spent. The result's `argv` is the caller's command, not the wrapper. clio-kit's argv: queries `["env", "SPACK_DISABLE_LOCAL_CONFIG=1", ".clio-relay/spack/bin/spack", "find", "--json", term]` (info and spec alike), `["module", "-t", "avail", term]` as before, and the prepare script's `["bash", "-c", ...]` with `plan: {"kind": "spack", "summary": ...}`. CLIO's Spack config is the checkout's site scope, `<home>/spack/etc/spack`; `JARVIS_SPACK_CONFIG` is still exported as `<home>/spack-config`, which Spack 0.23.1 ignores under `SPACK_DISABLE_LOCAL_CONFIG`. Limit: a guarded site lets the agent write new files, so a new `repos.yaml` in a config scope Spack reads would still be loaded; a person who needs more sets `spack: []`.
- **Package staging over MCP (coordinator, W17's video 1 blocker).** Superseded in part by "Package sources the host installs" below: the `git` form, the pinned `CLIO_JARVIS_*` default and its fetch are gone; `list`, `remove`, `dir`, the archive path and the policy stay. CLIO could not stage a package, so `run` refused on an empty catalog and the agent went grepping relay's checkouts. A seventh tool, `packages`, with `op` list, stage or remove (a verb of its own: staging writes relay's catalog, not a site, so it fits none of the five, and `files stage` is a site's content store). Stage sources: `git` plus `commit` (7 to 40 hex, never a branch or tag; `path` when the JARVIS repository sits deeper), or `dir`, an absolute directory on the relay host; with no source it is the pinned clio-jarvis (`CLIO_JARVIS_URL` at `CLIO_JARVIS_COMMIT`, aa8b3d56). The fetch is `git init`, a depth-1 `fetch` of that commit (a full fetch when the server will not serve a hash), a detached checkout, and a check that HEAD is that commit, in a temporary directory, with `protocol.ext.allow=never`, no terminal prompt, batch-mode ssh, and 300 s per git call. URLs are https, http, ssh, git, file or `user@host:path`. The JARVIS repository is the directory named like the repository at the source or one level down (clio-jarvis's `clio_jarvis/clio_jarvis/...`). Then the CLI's path: `archive_package`, `git_source`, `stage`, so an MCP stage and a CLI stage of the same files give the same SHA-256. Remove takes every matching entry (`version`, `sha256` narrow it) out of the catalog and drops each archive from the content store unless an operation that is not final names it (`archive_kept`). Result `relay.packages.v1`: `staged` or `removed`, `packages` (the catalog after the call) and `default_source`. Policy: staging asks nobody. It runs nothing and touches no site, and a guarded site's review, by SHA-256, still comes before the archive's first run there; packages are relay-host state, not one site's. `site clean` leaves the catalog alone. Run's refusals (`resolve`, `read_archive`, the guard's next step) and the server's instructions now name `packages(op="stage", ...)`. The tool-list tests now name all seven tools (shutdown had already made them stale). The use-relay skill has a section on staging. Live check on the relay host, no tests, no hand staging: a plain `mcp` 2.3.0 `Client` over stdio against `clio-relay serve` (protocol 2026-07-28) with an empty state and one guarded `local` site. It listed seven tools. `run` with `clio_jarvis.xcompact3d` refused `invalid_spec`, naming `packages(op="stage", ...)`. `packages(op="stage", package="clio_jarvis.xcompact3d")` fetched aa8b3d56 and staged 0.3.1, SHA-256 e35bda40d304..., 21 files. `list` and `describe` then showed it, and the same `run` resolved it to `needs_approval`: a package review naming that SHA-256 and the source commit. A `dir` stage and a `file://` git stage of a scratch repository worked, `commit: "main"` was refused `invalid_arguments`, and `remove` took both scratch entries and their archives while the xcompact3d entry stayed.
- **`EndpointPort.address` (coordinator, for CLIO's Windows fence, clio-agent b54f1f912).** The fence publishes fenced loopback listeners to the host by rewriting address strings in a server's output. A tcp port such as pvserver's was `{name, protocol, host, port}` with `url` None in `structuredContent`, so the fence had no string to rewrite, and the UI, scripts and the persisted record kept the fenced port. `EndpointPort` now has a computed `address`, `host:port` (an IPv6 host in brackets), in every dump: the serve result, `describe`, the task status and the record. The `serve` tool description says `address` is what you hand to clients, and the text summaries print it, with host and port as the fallback for an older record. The `_meta` `ui_data` already had `address`. It stays generic: any host that rewrites or reads addresses gets one string per port.
- **Programs found on the PATH, with PATHEXT (coordinator, video 1 blocker).** On Windows, `create_subprocess_exec("ssh")` reaches CreateProcess, which only appends `.exe`, so relay ran System32's OpenSSH `ssh.exe` even when a host put an `ssh.cmd` shim first on the PATH (CLIO's fence does, and blocks the raw connect of `ssh.exe`). `clio_relay.programs` now finds every bare-name program relay starts with `shutil.which` against the PATH the child gets, which searches PATHEXT per directory, so the shim wins. A name with a path separator is used as given. It covers the ssh transport (run, master, scp), the bounded helpers, serve forwards, the worker adapter's ssh (connect and clean), the held frpc visitor, Tapis registration (ssh, ssh-keygen) and the git calls in package staging and jarvis site checks. Site-side spawns, workload executables and the System32 tools (tasklist, taskkill) are left alone. A missing program keeps each site's typed reason: the ssh transport stays `adapter_unavailable` (its docs and tests already say so), forwards, the worker ssh and Tapis `ssh` refuse with `unreachable`, and staging's git stays `invalid_config`; every message names the command. Caveat: a `.cmd` shim runs its arguments through cmd.exe, so an argument with `&`, `|`, `%` or `"` can be mangled. Most ssh calls send the script on stdin (`sh -s`), which is safe; the `sh -c` forms are the exposed ones, and a shim must pass `%*` through unchanged.
- **Scratch dirs a sandboxed relay can use (coordinator, W17 under the Windows fence).** Since Python 3.13, `tempfile.mkdtemp` on Windows calls `os.mkdir(path, 0o700)`, which writes an owner-only DACL (SYSTEM, Administrators, OWNER RIGHTS) without the AppContainer SID, so a fenced relay got `[WinError 5]` on the dir it had just made (package staging; the frp visitor config, which blocks the frp videos; the Tapis key). `clio_relay.scratch` makes the dir with `os.mkdir` at the default mode on Windows, so it inherits its parent's ACL (the per-user or per-container temp folder, as before 3.13), and 0700 on POSIX. `ScratchDir` keeps `TemporaryDirectory`'s lifetime: `cleanup()` raises unless told to ignore errors, the context manager yields the path, a finalizer removes a forgotten dir, and read-only files (git objects) are removed too. All three sites use it; src has no other `mkdtemp` or `TemporaryDirectory`. Proved on Windows with CPython 3.14.6 in a throwaway AppContainer: `mkdtemp` in `AC\Temp` was denied, a `ScratchDir` and a `make_dir` were usable and cleaned up, and a write outside the container was denied.
- **Git staging under CLIO's MXC fence (coordinator, W17's video 1 blocker).** Superseded by "Package sources the host installs" below: staging runs no git, and the `github` grant path and the marketplace's `github` account are removed. The rename finding stays true. A fenced relay's `git init` failed with "could not write config file ... .git/config: Permission denied". Reproduced under the real fence: `codex sandbox` 0.167.0 (CLIO's bundled, MXC-verified build) with an MXC layer (`[windows] sandbox = "mxc"`, `:root` read, one write root, managed proxy), Copilot git 2.56. Cause: MXC denies every rename in the container's `AC\Temp`, for any process. Python's `os.rename` and `os.replace` and `MoveFileExW` (flags 0 and 1) all got access denied there, while create, write and delete worked. In a write root of the profile all four renames and `git init` succeeded. Git writes `config.lock` and renames it to `config`, so the first config write fails. A plain AppContainer fails earlier and differently, because its ancestors above `AC` refuse even a stat, which git's leading-directory walk needs. MXC reads anywhere, so that is not the fenced relay's cause. Fix: `stage_git` makes its checkout under `<state>/scratch` (`stage_scratch_dir`), which is not the temp folder. Relay must write its state anyway, and CLIO grants `${PLUGIN_DATA}` as a fleet write root. Leftover stage dirs older than 6 h are swept there. The fetch then reached GitHub through the fence's proxy, but clio-jarvis is private. Git's credential helpers (`copilot`, `!gh auth git-credential`) run in msys `sh`, which dies under MXC (`NtCreateDirectoryObject ... 0xC0000022`). So relay now hands a `github` grant file's live token to git (HS4a, RL30: "private GitHub package access"), for https fetches from github.com (`GIT_GRANT_HOSTS`), as an `http.https://github.com/.extraheader` set through `GIT_CONFIG_COUNT` in git's environment. It is never on a command line and never in a helper. `--grant-file` reaches it through the `packages` tool, and the env var through the CLI. With no usable credential, the refusal names the grant file and a helper. Proxy variables were already inherited from relay's environment. Under MXC a direct socket to github.com:443 got WinError 10013, git with the fence's `HTTPS_PROXY` worked, and git with the proxy variables removed could not connect. Proved over MCP: an `mcp` 2.3.0 client outside, and `clio-relay serve` inside `codex sandbox` with that MXC layer and `CLIO_RELAY_STATE_DIR` in the write root. With no grant, `packages(op="stage", package="clio_jarvis.xcompact3d")` refused `input_missing`, naming the grant file. With a `github` grant file it staged 0.3.1 at aa8b3d56, SHA-256 e35bda40d304... (the same archive as the unfenced stage), `list` showed it, and `<state>/scratch` was empty after. The plugin must declare a `github` account and map `CLIO_RELAY_GRANT_FILE_GITHUB=${CLIO_ACCOUNT_GITHUB_FILE}` (the marketplace change).
- **Package sources the host installs (the user's design, 7d77a518).** The user: "clio relay shoudl pull both on plugin isntall get teh clio relay jarvis cd and the jartvis clio repository" and "new files do go to git because most users will not even have permission to psuh to clio jarvis". I read the second as "don't go to git", for the reason it gives, and the code agrees: nothing in relay pushes. Then "i a arandom user with no permission to write on clio relay, how is this handle?": the install and staging must work with no write access, no token and no GitHub sign-in.
  - Relay reads `CLIO_RELAY_PACKAGE_SOURCES`, a PATH-style list of directories on its host, each holding JARVIS repositories (a repository dir, or dirs one level down). A stage with only a package name takes the first source that has it. A copy may carry `.clio-relay-source.json` (repository, commit, path), which becomes the archive's recorded source; otherwise relay asks git read-only, best effort. Staging fetches nothing and runs no git. `dir` stays, for the person's own packages: the archive is a job input, so it reaches a site through the adapter's transfer (scp on ssh) and never through git.
  - Removed: the `git` plus `commit` form, the scratch checkouts, the `github` grant extraheader, `CLIO_JARVIS_URL` and `CLIO_JARVIS_COMMIT`, and the site-side `add_clio_jarvis` clone. The SHA-256 review on a guarded site is unchanged.
  - The CLIO plugin ships clio-jarvis's `clio_jarvis/` and `schemas/` at aa8b3d56 under `plugins/clio-relay/packages/clio-jarvis/`, with `.clio-relay-source.json` and `* -text`, and maps `CLIO_RELAY_PACKAGE_SOURCES=${PLUGIN_ROOT}/packages`. Installing the plugin is the fetch: the marketplace clone a person already has, public and read-only. The grant env line and the `github` account are gone from the plugin.
  - Not a submodule: relay is installed by `uvx --from git+...@<sha>`, and a wheel built from git leaves a submodule out unless it is checked out and listed as package data, which would hard-wire clio-jarvis into relay's wheel. Not a uv `--with`: clio-jarvis is not a Python package. A plugin-provided source leaves room for the user's later direction (a person's private marketplace adds or replaces package repositories as CLIO writes them): another plugin, or a second path in the list, is another source, and relay's code names none.
  - jarvis-cd is not vendored. It runs on the site, not the relay host, and the site bootstrap already fetches relay's pinned jarvis-cd as a public archive (`JARVIS_SOURCE`, grc-iit/jarvis-cd at `JARVIS_PIN`, now af290aef), with no credential.
  - RL42 is met by the pinned copy staged as an archive, with its recorded source, rather than by a site clone.
- **The video-1 path under CLIO's MXC fence (coordinator's sweep, user-directed).** A driver (`transport_for` from clio-agent with `install_chokepoint` and `install_sandbox`, mechanism `codex`, `fence_active`) spawned `clio-relay serve` the way CLIO does, with `CLIO_RELAY_STATE_DIR` in the write root and the plugin's package dir as the source. Ares took only light login-node calls and debug-queue jobs. Failure, then fix:
  - `git init` in `AC\Temp`: moot, staging runs no git.
  - Staging needed a GitHub credential: replaced by the plugin's copy. Under the fence `packages stage` gave xcompact3d 0.3.1 (SHA-256 92392ff36f13...) and pvserver 0.3.1 (b3f47a1d9a4b...), each with source `{repository: https://github.com/iowarp/clio-jarvis, commit: aa8b3d56..., path: clio_jarvis}`, and `list` showed both. The digest differs from the git stage's e35bda40d304... because the recorded source is part of the archive.
  - `describe ares` and `exec`: passed through the `ssh.cmd` shim. `files` stage, write, read and delete passed once the harness venv had `distlib`; the `.cmd` fallback the stale venv took is not relay's (the ledger's `.cmd` caveat stands).
  - `run` refused `duration: 600`: a duration is now a number of seconds or an ISO 8601 or lax string (`jobs.spec.Duration`, also `idle_release`), and the schema says so.
  - psij's local executor imports `signal.SIGCHLD`, which Windows lacks, so the ssh and local adapters failed to load psij at all: `jobs.psij_executors.prepare()` puts a stand-in for `psij.executors.local` in place first on such a host. Then job 27926 ran on Ares, scp uploaded the archive, and it completed with exit 0.
  - `serve`, first: the forward's hop was a bare `ssh` inside `ProxyCommand`, which Windows OpenSSH starts with CreateProcess from its own directory, so System32's ssh ran and the fence refused its connect. The hop now names the program relay found on the PATH (`programs.find`), quoted with `list2cmdline` on Windows and `shlex.join` elsewhere, `%` doubled.
  - `serve`, second: "Host key verification failed" for ares-comp-03, also without the fence (generic). Relay reads the node's lines from the login node's `/etc/ssh/ssh_known_hosts`, `ssh_known_hosts2` and `~/.ssh/known_hosts` with `ssh-keygen -F` (`ServePlan.host_keys`) and hands them to the forward as `GlobalKnownHostsFile`, after the person's own.
  - `serve`, third: "Permission denied (publickey)": the person's `User` and `IdentityFile` are set for `Host ares`, which says nothing of the node. The forward now carries user, identityfile, certificatefile, identitiesonly and identityagent from `ssh -G <login destination>` to the node. Then job 27932 served `python3 -m http.server`; inside the fence the forward bound 59322, CLIO published `address 127.0.0.1:53718`, a GET from outside the fence got 200, and cancel ended it.
  - frpc visitor: Windows Defender quarantines frpc.exe and frps.exe 0.69.1 (official release) as potentially unwanted software, so the visitor cannot start on a stock Windows desktop. Not worked around: allowing it is the person's choice. Relay now says so (`programs.start_failed`, Windows errors 225 and 226) in the serve forward and the held visitor, rather than a bare "cannot start". The rest of the path held under the fence: relay wrote `visitor.toml` under its state dir, and a probe server inside the fence reached a LAN rendezvous (10.0.0.170:7000) both directly and by HTTP CONNECT through the chokepoint (`HTTP/1.1 200`, then the listener's `HTTP/1.0 200`), which is how frpc 0.69.1 dials with `transport.proxyURL` taken from `http_proxy`. Open: the frpc connect and visitor bind themselves, once Defender allows frpc.
- **A clean site, end to end (coordinator, video 1 blocker on 84c2bb77: "jarvis-cd needs Python 3.11 or newer; python3 is older" on Ares).** W17's earlier runs never started from scratch, because `site clean` removed nothing (below). Proved from a really clean Ares through `clio-relay serve` over MCP, with an `mcp` 2.3.0 client driving the asks through `clio-relay asks`. Failure, then fix:
  - **No Python 3.11 on the PATH.** The bootstrap (`BOOTSTRAP_SCRIPT`) now looks for one itself: `python3`, then every `python3.N` on the PATH, newest first, then the site's environment modules (`module -t avail` in a login shell, entries named `python*/<version>`, newest first, loaded with `module load` in a login shell). A module Python whose libraries need the module's `LD_LIBRARY_PATH` gets a `jarvis` wrapper that sets it, so later jobs need no `module load`. The optional `jarvis_python` setting (ssh, local and worker, forwarded to the worker only when set) names a path, `~/...`, a name on the PATH or `module:<name>`; with it relay tries only that. The refusal lists every candidate tried and why it failed, and says how to name one. The venv is made with that interpreter and every package job runs relay's `jarvis`, so they use the same Python. On Ares: `python3` 3.10.12 was skipped and `module python/3.11.9-zg4555e` was used.
  - **`site clean` was a no-op over ssh.** The prune script is bash, the ssh transport runs `sh -s` (dash), and its exit code was ignored, so clean said it removed things and removed nothing. `site_prune` now wraps the script in `bash -c`, and the ssh adapter fails on a non-zero exit, as the worker's already did. Clean keeps `spack/` and `spack-config/` unless asked to remove the Spack.
  - **jarvis-cd from a tarball had no version.** setuptools-scm needs git metadata, which the GitHub archive lacks: the bootstrap sets `SETUPTOOLS_SCM_PRETEND_VERSION_FOR_JARVIS_CD`.
  - **A package's Python requirements were missing** (`jsonschema` for clio-jarvis). `jarvis_site` reads `[project].dependencies` from the repository's `pyproject.toml`, ships them in the archive as `REQUIREMENTS_FILE`, and lists them in the guard's review. The site script installs them with relay's own jarvis-cd `pip` before it registers the repository (never into a jarvis the site names), logged in `relay.requirements.log`.
  - **The Spack repository was missing from the archive.** `jarvis_site.contract_repos` reads `install.repos` from the repository's `contract.yaml` (paths inside the repository with a `repo.yaml`; anything else is refused), and those directories ride in the archive beside `schemas/`. The plugin's clio-jarvis copy now has `pyproject.toml` and `spack/` from aa8b3d56. The xcompact3d archive is now SHA-256 `0089efeb84bd...` (52818 bytes), so it needs a new review.
  - **No Spack was a raw `submit_failed`.** When JARVIS prints "spack is not available", the site script writes `relay.refusal.json` with `missing: spack` and the CLIO Spack path, and exits 69. `missing_jarvis` turns it into `adapter_unavailable` naming where relay looked, with no install offer: relay does not make the CLIO Spack. On a fresh site, CLIO prepares it first (clio-kit's `spack_prepare_site`, through `exec` with `plan: {kind: spack}`), as this proof did.
  - **The install ask said "Built: pkg".** JARVIS reports a package by its pipeline step (`pkg_name`). `package_names` maps the steps back, and the ask now names what gets built (on Ares: "Built: xcompact3d@5.0 +clio '^decomp2d-fft' io=adios2 '^adios2+mpi+fortran' '^openmpi/x2kvx5l' (4 missing)").
  - **The install job took the run's walltime.** jarvis-cd's `ppl install +submit` keeps the pipeline's scheduler `time` on purpose, so a 2-minute run gave the Spack build 2 minutes (Ares job 27933, TIMEOUT while still concretizing). The adapters (ssh, local, so worker too) now hand the site script the install job's own pipeline (`jarvis_submit.install_pipeline`), kept as `relay.install-pipeline.yaml` so the run's stays as it was. It has `install_walltime_s` (default 4 hours, or the run's walltime when longer) and no `pre_cmds`, so a service's connector does not start in a build. The install approval says "a one-node job of up to 04:00:00". On Ares, job 27934 ran with `TimeLimit=04:00:00`; the proof cancelled it there.
  - **A cancelled operation kept its pending asks.** Cancel closed asks only for an operation that had not started, so a run cancelled during its install (or after submit) left its MPI question in `asks list` and on the page. `RelayService.cancel` now withdraws every ask still pending once the operation is final, also when it was already final.
  - **`site clean` stopped at read-only directories.** JARVIS makes each execution's `input/` read-only, so `rm -rf` refused the CLIO site home after any install. The prune now makes a tree it is told to remove owner-writable (never through a symlink) and tries once more.
  - **Not relay: a JARVIS cancel fails on a Slurm without accounting.** Inside the job, jarvis-cd 9bf89f96 records `SLURM_CLUSTER_NAME` (`linux` on Ares), and `jarvis execution cancel` then runs `scancel --clusters=linux`, which needs slurmdbd: "Problem talking to database". Relay returns `not_cancellable` with that text while the job runs, and the cancel succeeds once the job ends. The fix belongs in jarvis-cd: pass `--clusters` only when `sbatch --parsable` reported a cluster. Done in jarvis-cd c95fa922, now relay's pin (above).
  - The MPI choice is asked once at site scope; the answer (`^openmpi/x2kvx5l` on Ares) is kept in JARVIS's site decisions under the CLIO site home.
- **A package name follows relay's package sources (coordinator, W17's take video1f).** After a plugin update, a package staged by the older relay won. Local state held xcompact3d 802bb56c, from a copy with no `pyproject.toml` and no `spack/`. A bare-name run resolved to it through `newest()` and skipped the review of the new archive (0089efeb), so the job failed on Ares with "No module named 'jsonschema'". Fix: catalog entries record `origin` (`sources` or `dir`; an entry from an older relay has none and counts as `sources`) and `superseded_by`. `stage_current` builds a name's archive from the current package sources. The archive is deterministic, so this is a no-op when nothing changed. If its SHA-256 is new, it stages the archive and marks every other `sources` entry of the name `superseded_by` that SHA-256. `apply_defaults` calls it for each package with no `sha256` before resolving, and `packages list` and `describe` call it too (`refresh_from_sources`), so they show what a run by that name pins. `resolve` and `newest` skip superseded entries unless the call gives a `sha256`. Superseded entries are kept, not deleted, so an operation that pinned one keeps working and an explicit pin still runs it. A `dir` stage is never superseded: the person chose those files. When the sources no longer hold the name, nothing is filtered. A guarded site's review is keyed by SHA-256, so the new archive asks it again. `site clean` stays site-only: the catalog is relay-host state, and the supersede makes the stale entry harmless without a clean. Proved locally over MCP, with no Ares: an `mcp` 2.3.0 client against `clio-relay serve`, one guarded `local` site, and the plugin's bundled clio-jarvis (marketplace dbef2355). With an older copy (no `pyproject.toml`), `packages(op="stage", package="clio_jarvis.xcompact3d")` staged af26bdd2..., and its `origin` was then stripped from the catalog to look like the older relay's entry. Serve restarted on the current copy: `list` showed af26bdd2 with `superseded_by` 0089efeb84bd... (the take's archive) and 0089efeb with `origin` `sources`. `describe(site=loc)` listed only 0089efeb, `reviewed: false`. `run` by name returned `needs_approval` with the review of 0089efeb (26 files, with `.clio-relay-requirements.txt`). `run` pinning af26bdd2's `sha256` still resolved it and asked its own review.
- **The no-Spack refusal carries a remedy (coordinator, W17's take video1g).** The pilot refused `missing: spack` and an unsteered CLIO stopped after two `ls -ld` calls: the refusal named a path and nothing an agent could act on. Relay still does not make the CLIO Spack, so it stays generic. The site script now probes, in a login shell, for the site's own Spack (`SPACK_ROOT`, `spack` on the PATH, MODULEPATH entries under `share/spack/`), keeps candidates with `bin/spack` and `share/spack/setup-env.sh`, and records root, how it was found, version and writability in `relay.refusal.json`. `jarvis_submit.missing_spack` returns `adapter_unavailable` with `missing: spack` and `remedy`: `action: prepare_spack`, `root`, `setup_env`, `config`, `site_spacks` (each `used: false` with `why_not_used`: relay never builds into a site Spack, the new checkout chains to it as an upstream), `steps`, `where` (login node through `exec`, light; builds in the JARVIS install job) and `then` (run again with the same spec). The CLIO side lives in the marketplace: the clio-relay plugin `requires` `spack`, and its use-relay skill maps the refusal to `spack_prepare_site` with `site_home` the parent of `remedy.root` and `site_spack` `remedy.site_spacks[0].root`. spack-mcp's defaults (`~/.clio-relay/spack`, `~/.clio-relay/spack-config`) match relay's paths.
  - **The archive hash depended on the checkout.** W17's review showed xcompact3d 9763fc95 and pvserver e18d3485; the proof had 0089efeb. Archive members took their mode from the file system and their bytes as checked out, so a Windows checkout (no exec bits, CRLF with `autocrlf`) changed the SHA-256 (a clio-jarvis CRLF checkout gave 10278785). 9763fc95 was not reproduced from public content. `jarvis_site._portable` now normalizes UTF-8 text with no NUL to LF and sets the mode from content (0o755 for a `#!` file, else 0o644). A marketplace clone, its codeload tarball, a CRLF copy and a clio-jarvis clone with `autocrlf` all give xcompact3d 0089efeb and pvserver dcb18420 (was d9d6fd0b, a shebang file not +x in git). The supersede above re-asks the review once.
  - Proved live from a clean Ares (`site clean --remove-spack`, no `~/.clio-relay`), with an `mcp` 2.3.0 client against `clio-relay serve` and spack-mcp from clio-kit 3f6cbf75, answering asks yes and acting only on structured results. `run` asked the review (0089efeb), the spend and the jarvis-cd c95fa922 install, then refused with the remedy: site Spack `/mnt/repo/software/spack/spack` 0.23.1 found by MODULEPATH, not writable. `spack_prepare_site` with the remedy's values returned a relay `exec` call, relay asked its guarded exec approval, and the second call returned `done` (Spack 0.23.1 at `~/.clio-relay/spack`, upstream the site's, openmpi 5.0.5 from it). The same `run` again asked the spend, the install and the site MPI choice (`^openmpi/x2kvx5l`), and submitted install job 27937 (`op_53e91af1df536ff210dbbb6f-install`); `run(cancel=...)` cancelled it and the queue was empty. Ares was cleaned again with `--remove-spack`.
    - **The hash still depended on the Python (coordinator, W17 on 43ff03ed).** W17's relay (uvx, marketplace c8b3356, clean state) still staged xcompact3d 9763fc95. Read-only, W17's installed snapshot `plugins/clio-relay/packages` matched c8b3356 byte for byte (40 files, the same set), and W17's 9763fc95 archive held the same members as the proof's, uncompressed tar afd4f0b3 in both. Only the gzip layer differed: W17's uvx runs CPython 3.14.8, whose Windows build links zlib 1.3.1.zlib-ng; the proof ran 3.12 with zlib 1.2.13. Built from W17's snapshot, 3.14 gave 9763fc95 (52881 bytes) and 3.12 gave 0089efeb (52818). Fix: `clio_relay.stable_gzip` writes the gzip with stored deflate blocks, whose bytes RFC 1951 and RFC 1952 fix, and the package archive and the Tapis launcher use it. Both Pythons now give xcompact3d 6f30a7a0 (215078 bytes) and pvserver 6736500f (174113); the launcher is 6dbc6261. GNU tar 1.35 `tar -xzf`, `gzip -t`, bsdtar and `tarfile` `r:gz` read it. On a copy of W17's catalog, `stage_current` marked 9763fc95, b9b2a956 and e18d3485 `superseded_by` the new archives, so a guarded site asks the review once more.
    - **The install job failed ADIOS2's Fortran check, and CLIO stopped mid-plan (coordinator, W17's take video1h).** Install job 27938 concretized ADIOS2 with gcc@12.3.0, which `spack_prepare_site` registered with `fc: null`.
      - **Compiler.** Spack 0.23.1, the version chained to Ares's upstream, has no `c`/`cxx`/`fortran` virtuals and no toolchains (those arrive in Spack 1.x). Languages are only `language` facts the solver does not match to a compiler's `fc`, and it weights the newest compiler above reusing gcc 11 deps. The idiomatic lever in 0.23 is the `packages: all: compiler: [...]` preference, not a hand pin: it sets the solver's compiler weight. `require` would also bind reused and external nodes. Order it as full toolchains first (`fc` set), then the compiler of the site MPI. On Ares, a site-scope `[gcc@11.4.0, gcc@12.3.0, clang@14.0.0]` concretized the whole stack to gcc@11.4.0, the compiler the upstream openmpi 5.0.5 and adios2 2.10.2 were built with. gfortran's `.mod` files are ABI-tied, so Fortran consumers of the upstream adios2 need gfortran 11 anyway. Owned by clio-kit's `spack_prepare_site` (the preference, plus `compiler` and `build_jobs` inputs), routed by the coordinator.
      - **Rebuilds.** The site Spack's `etc/spack/defaults/packages.yaml` declares openmpi@5.0.5 an external whose prefix is its own upstream install. So `spack find` shows two hashes with one prefix: x2kvx5l (the external) and cphqvsy (the install). The upstream adios2 links cphqvsy. jarvis-cd's `SpackPlanner.mpi_candidates` merges by prefix and keeps the first hash, so the choice `^openmpi/x2kvx5l` forced ADIOS2 and its deps to rebuild. jarvis-cd af290aef pins the real install instead, and relay now pins it (above). Relay keeps install answers only per operation, so no older relay answer replays; a site-scope answer lives in JARVIS on the site, which `site clean` removes.
      - **Parallelism.** Ares Slurm has `TaskPlugin=task/none` and `CR_CORE`, with 40 CPUs a node. The install job kept the run's `ntasks_per_node` (4 CPUs), while Spack's default `build_jobs` is min(16, affinity CPUs), and with no binding that was 16. `install_pipeline` now drops the run's task shape (`pre_cmds`, `ntasks`, `cpus_per_task`, `gpus_per_node`, `mem`, `hostfile`) and gives the install one task on one whole node (`nodes: 1`, `ntasks_per_node: 1`, `exclusive`). The install approval says "a job on one whole node". `build_jobs` above 16 is a site-scope Spack setting (prepare_site's input).
      - **The long horizon.** Yield and wake worked: the install task failed at 18:05:47, and CLIO was woken at 18:05:58 and read compilers.yaml and install.log. Then it waited on permission prompts. The plan was approved `interactive` (the agent's `recommendedMode`), which asks before each action. Even auto-execute would have asked: it sets `approval_mode=auto-edits`, which auto-allows only closed-world writes, and relay declared `openWorldHint: true` on every tool. Relay's world is closed: it acts only on registered sites and asks itself for what their policy names. So its tools now declare `openWorldHint: false`, `describe` `readOnlyHint: true`, the rest `readOnlyHint: false` (run and serve not destructive). clio-agent's classifier tags them `read` and `write`, so auto-execute runs them unattended, interactive still asks, and relay's asks still reach the person. A failed install now fails with `install_log` and `install_job` in its data, and the use-relay skill says to yield on a task, read the log through `files`, fix with the owning tool and run again. clio-agent items routed: guidance for `recommendedMode`, and `run_code` asking once per script under auto-edits.
            - **Live proof (headless CLIO on clio-agent jaimecernuda-relay-rework, clio-kit edf67da2, Ares after `site clean --remove-spack`).** One prompt, the plan approved `auto`, no steering. CLIO staged xcompact3d, met the missing-Spack refusal, ran `spack_prepare_site` through relay's guarded exec, answered the install asks and submitted the install task, then yielded. Install job 27940 ran on one whole node (`NumCPUs=40`, `make -j40`, gcc@11.4.0 with gfortran). Spack reused 44 of 46 specs (43 from the site upstream, among them cmake 3.30.5, openmpi 5.0.5 cphqvsy and adios2 2.10.2 sf7srjv, plus the system glibc external) and built two: decomp2d-fft 2.0.4 (29.3 s, build 12.7 s) and xcompact3d 5.0 (3 min 28 s, build 3 min 16 s). The job took 9 min: 4 min 5 s of `spack install`, the rest four concretizations of about 70 s each (jarvis's check spec, re-spec, env concretize and post-check spec), which is jarvis-cd's to trim. Run job 27942 (one node, four ranks) finished all 2400 steps, and CLIO, woken by the task's end with no message from anyone, checked the 1.58 GB `data.bp5` and reported its path. When the install job was cancelled while queued, CLIO was woken, read the job's logs and `sinfo`, and resubmitted on the idle `debug` partition. clio-agent 1b126e2ac classifies a conversation-joined plugin's tools from their live annotations, so under the auto plan `relay_run` and `relay_packages` ran without a permission prompt while relay's own asks (spend, jarvis-cd install, the login-node Spack plan, the MPI choice) still came to the person;             only `run_code` scripts still ask. A second run on clio-agent 5e243b326 from a clean Ares repeated it: the install job queued on a busy `compute` (Slurm's start estimate was 35 h away), and when it was cancelled CLIO woke and asked the person before moving to `debug`. Install job 27944 then reused the same 43 upstream specs and built the same two (decomp2d-fft 24.8 s, xcompact3d 3 min 26 s), and CLIO, woken by the run task's `task_result`, checked `data.bp5` again. Its final reply was cut by a dropped Codex stream, a provider fault. A whole-node install on a busy partition can queue for long; the agent or the site's `defaults.queue` chooses the partition.
            - **Four gaps from W17's take video1i (coordinator).**
              - **A site's MPI choice carries across installs.** The pvserver install asked the MPI choice again after xcompact3d's install had answered it, because answers were kept per operation and per set of archives. Relay now keeps the answers to JARVIS issues of `scope: site` (the MPI choice) in `<state>/sites/<site>/install-choices.json`, keyed by the issue's `subject`. `ask_install` gives a carried answer to the next install whose site-scope issue has the same subject and still offers that constraint, puts it in the approval's binding and text ("It uses the ... chosen for ares before; decline to choose again."), and asks only the rest. `start_install` records the answers it used. Declining an approval that carried answers forgets them, so the next install asks again: that is how the person changes it. `site clean` removes the file, and `describe(site)` lists them as `install_choices`. A carried constraint that no candidate offers any more (jarvis-cd af290aef changed `x2kvx5l` to `cphqvsy`) is not carried: the issue is asked.
              - **`path: auto` fell to a stale frp.** A local `rendezvous.json` with no frpc on the site chose frp, and the serve failed "no frp client". `serve.config.frp_unusable` checks, from local state only, that the site has a client (its `frpc` setting, or an existing `frpc_binary` to place), that this machine has the rendezvous's frpc, and that the token loads. For `auto` the runtime then falls back to ssh, logging why; an explicit `path: frp` keeps frp. `rendezvous.json` is the person's own setting, written only by `clio-relay rendezvous set`, so neither `site clean` nor a local reset drops it: the usability check makes a stale one harmless.
              - **A queued job was invisible.** Pvserver install 27949 sat `PENDING (Resources)` on `compute` with Slurm's estimate about 28 h out while `debug` had idle nodes. The ssh adapter's status (scheduler mode and JARVIS) appends `slurm_facts.queue_script` while the job is not final: `squeue -t PD` for the job (`%P|%r|%S|%D|%l`) and `sinfo` per partition. `JobStatus.queue` holds partition, reason, `estimated_start`, nodes, time limit and `idle_partitions` (each with idle nodes, max time and `fits`). The operation's `result.queue` and its message (and the task's progress note) carry it for the run and for the install job. Relay never switches partition itself; the agent offers to cancel and run again with `queue=`.
              - **Multi-node runs died on docker0.** Bench 27948 (64 ranks on comp-10..13) died with `btl_tcp_endpoint.c:667 received unexpected process identifier`: every compute node has `docker0` at 172.17.0.1/16, so Open MPI 5.0.5's TCP BTL dialed its own node. Verified on Ares, read-only: the site openmpi cphqvsy's `openmpi-mca-params.conf`, `prte.conf` and `prte-mca-params.conf` set nothing (`/etc/openmpi/openmpi-mca-params.conf` is the system Open MPI's); the module is built `--without-slurm` with UCX 1.17 and no verbs (UCX memory domains tcp, posix, sysv); Slurm has `MpiDefault=none` and srun plugins pmi2, cray_shasta and none, so no pmix launch path. The user's own multi-node scripts (`coeus-adapter/test/insitu_agent/run_scaling_test.sh`) export `OMPI_MCA_pml=ob1`, `OMPI_MCA_btl=tcp,self` and `OMPI_MCA_btl_tcp_if_include=eno1`/`OMPI_MCA_oob_tcp_if_include=eno1`. A compute node (comp-04) has eno1 172.20.101.4/16 (1 Gb), enp47s0np0 172.25.101.4/16 (40 Gb, mlx5_0), a link-local enx 169.254.95.120/24, docker0 172.17.0.1/16 and lo; Slurm's NodeAddr is the hostname, which resolves to 172.20.101.N.
                - **Layer.** Relay owns the choice, because it is the site's: the new jarvis-cd has no network discovery, and xcompact3d and clio-jarvis must not carry site facts. `clio_relay.mpi_network` finds the login node's most specific network that holds every Slurm node address (skipping loopback and link-local), which is how the scheduler and ssh reach the nodes; on Ares that is 172.20.0.0/16, the user's eno1. The site script finds it once and keeps it at `<home>/jarvis-cd/mpi-network` (`CLIO_RELAY_MPI_NETWORK`; a first line `none` turns it off), and for `submit` and `run` exports `OMPI_MCA_btl_tcp_if_include`, `OMPI_MCA_oob_tcp_if_include` and `PRTE_MCA_oob_tcp_if_include` (Open MPI 5's out-of-band channel is PRRTE's), unless the job's environment sets any of                 them or an `_if_exclude`. The variables reach the ranks through the submit-time environment, not JARVIS: `sbatch` exports it (`--export=ALL`, its default) and Open MPI's launcher forwards every `OMPI_MCA_*` to each rank. Verified live: they are absent from JARVIS's `runtime/environment.yaml` and `submit.slurm`, and the run that used them succeeded. The job's `relay.mpi-network` says what it used and who named it (`slurm-node-addresses`, `person`, or `job`). Interfaces shared across nodes are excluded by construction: 172.17.0.1 is not on the node-address network. `describe(site)` shows the live discovery as `mpi.network`. A CIDR, not an interface name, since login and compute name their interfaces differently (eno2 against eno1).
                                - **The person's lever is the site record.** A guarded site refuses a package job's `environment` (`guard/policy.py`), so a per-job override cannot be the lever there. The record `<home>/jarvis-cd/mpi-network` is: one line `<network> <who>`, the network a CIDR, interface names joined by commas, or `none`. The agent rewrites it with `files` (op write, overwrite true), which a guarded site asks the person for, and every later job reads it. `site clean` removes it and relay finds the network again. A job's own environment still wins, on sites that take one.
                                - **Diagnosis.** A failed run whose message, stdout tail or stderr tail matches Open MPI's TCP signatures carries `result.mpi_network_hint`: the matched signs, the job's launch settings when known, `site_record` (the record's absolute path) and a message naming the lever. The use-relay skill's failed-run section maps it to reading `describe(site).info.mpi.network` and the nodes' interfaces, and writing the fabric to the record.
                                - **Live proof on Ares (clean site, then xcompact3d, guarded).** `describe(ares)` found `172.20.0.0/16` from 28 node addresses. Gap 3: install job 27952 waited `PENDING (Resources)` on `compute` with `result.queue.estimated_start` 2026-11-22T00:55:38 and `idle_partitions` datacrumbs (1) and debug (1), both `fits: true`, in the message too; cancelled. Gap 1: the next install's approval said "It uses the mpi ^openmpi/cphqvsy chosen for ares before; decline to choose again" with no choose ask, and so did a later `clio_jarvis.pvserver` install (cancelled before it submitted). Install job 27953 on debug (ares-comp-03) built only decomp2d-fft (25.9 s) and xcompact3d (3 min 26 s, build 3 min 13 s); the rest, ADIOS2 included, came from the upstream. Gap 4: 16 ranks on 4 nodes (ares-comp-[03-06], debug) ran with the discovered network, job 27955, exit 0 in about 29 s. Control: with the record set to `none person` through a guarded `files write`, the same run (job 27956) died with `received unexpected process identifier` (exit 14) and carried `mpi_network_hint` with `site_record`. Writing `172.20.0.0/16 person` the same way, job 27957 exited 0 and its `relay.mpi-network` said `person`. Gap 2: with a stale `rendezvous.json` (frps.example.invalid) and no frpc on the site, a serve on an open entry for the same host took ssh (`127.0.0.1:<port>`, `durable: false`); `frp_unusable` gave "has no client on the site".
                              - **Resuming with the same spec was refused.** `run(resume=, spec=)` repeating the first call's spec got `approval_mismatch`: `start_run` digests the spec after `site_cores` adds `cores_per_node`, while `same_request` applied only the defaults. The MCP `input_required` retry hits the same check. `same_request` now accepts the defaults-only or the prepared form (`prepared_run`, `prepared_serve`); proven live on the 4-node resume.
                              - **A serve's approval refusal named the wrong call.** It said "call run with resume set to the operation id"; a serve resumes with `serve(service=)`, which the refusal now names.
            - **Three gaps from W17's take video1j, and two additions (coordinator).**
              - **Relay asks the queue switch itself.** Pvserver install 27964 sat on `compute` with Slurm's start about 31 h out and `idle_partitions` debug and datacrumbs `fits: true`, and CLIO offered nothing: skill guidance alone did not move it. `surface/queue_switch.py` now raises the decision the way spend does. When a run job or an install job (of a run or a serve) waits with Slurm's estimate further off than the site's new `defaults.queue_wait` (one hour unless set), not for a reason the job causes itself (dependency, begin time, hold), and another partition has idle nodes it fits, relay asks once per job and partition: "keep waiting in <p>" or "move to <q>". The operation goes to `awaiting_input`, so a task client sees `input_required`. A move is `scontrol update JobId=<id> Partition=<q>` on the pending job (the adapter's new `move_queue`, ssh only; others refuse `not_implemented`), not cancel and resubmit: the job keeps its id, directory and approvals, a resubmit under the same operation id would meet the ssh adapter's existing-job check, and the spec digest is untouched (the partition is applied to the job's attributes at submit, `queue_switch.applied`). Keep, decline or cancel of the ask keeps the job. If the job starts or ends before the answer, the ask is withdrawn ("the job left the queue before the person answered"). Relay never moves without the answer.
              - **The agent named `queue: compute`.** The cause was the catalog's ares site skill (`catalog/skills/ares.md`, and the Tapis one), which called `compute` the default and told the agent to set the queue, plus the `queue_name` field description. Both now say to leave the queue unset unless the person names one, since the site's `defaults.queue` applies. The packages' own `queue` parameter already defaults to the site's. The use-relay skill says the same, and that relay asks the switch itself.
              - **The coupled pipeline was refused.** "service steps start on different nodes; set service: true on the one step" came from relay: `package_service_job` set `service: true` on every step, so a pvserver plus xcompact3d pipeline always had two service steps. It now marks only the steps whose package kind is `service` (`prepare_service` reads the catalog), and the use-relay skill shows `service: true` on the serving step with a `host_slice` per step.
              - **`site clean` refused on a stale op.** After an external `scancel`, the op still read `queued`, because Ares keeps no Slurm accounting and JARVIS's record stays `unknown`, or `submitted` for a job cancelled while pending. The ssh adapter's JARVIS status now asks `squeue` for the job in any state (`slurm_facts.queue_script`'s `@@alive`): `CANCELLED` ends the op `cancelled`, and a job Slurm no longer knows while JARVIS says `unknown` or `submitted` ends `failed` with `native_state` `gone`. `site clean` refreshes the site's open run and serve ops first (`_reconcile`), cancels those still waiting on the person, and refuses only on jobs really queued or running. jarvis-cd af290aef also ends such a job after its own reconciliation grace period; relay's check ends it at once.
              - **Package parameter cases.** Relay's package schema now adds clio-jarvis's `parameters.cases` as `allOf` if/then rules, so xcompact3d 0.3.2's case-scoped rules (Taylor-Green's `n` enum only without geometry) validate before submit.
              - **Re-bundled clio-jarvis 82debc68** (xcompact3d 0.3.2). Its review hash is `8dd1ceba53c5cc82af9ba6d9426711ddccf120281775c307e1365320a9242a0d`; pvserver 0.3.1 is `ba17a5b8a2b1f24d7dbc6e6d2b2494bb50f2e1aa5a6e2dad09f7a785d7cf36d6`.
              - **Live proof on Ares, from clean (guarded, `defaults.queue: debug`).** Gap 2: a run with no queue asked spend for `Queue: debug`, after the missing-Spack refusal and `spack_prepare_site` (gcc@11.4.0 first); install job 27966 and run job 27967 both ran on debug, exit 0. Gap 3: the coupled serve (pvserver `service: true` on 0:1, xcompact3d on 1:2, two nodes) passed validation, its spend ask listed both steps, and the install ask carried the site's MPI answer. Gap 1: compute nodes were all shared (2 of 40 CPUs in use), so rank-sized runs started at once (27968, 27969, with `queue_wait: 60`); the whole-node install job 27970 waited `PENDING (Resources)` with Slurm's start 29 h out, relay asked "keep waiting in compute / move to datacrumbs / move to debug", and on "move to debug" the same job 27970 ran on ares-comp-03 seconds later. Clean: job 27970 cancelled outside relay while running ended with JARVIS's exit 143, and the clean went ahead. Install job 27972 cancelled outside relay while pending: 5 s later `site clean` withdrew its queue ask and ended the op "Slurm job 27972 is gone from the scheduler" instead of refusing. A last `site clean --yes --remove-spack` cancelled a run still waiting on its spend ask and left Ares with no relay directories and no jobs.
- **Relay is the middle man (user, 2026-11-20: "the deployment sematnics and installation shoudl all fall to jarvis, and relay is just hte middle man"; pin jarvis-cd bfeff98b).** This supersedes the site logic in the video1i and video1j entries above. The audit and its boundary table are in the coordinator's files; JARVIS's side is in jarvis-cd `docs/pipelines.md`, `sched.md`, `spack.md` and `site-skills.md`. Each removal shipped with the pin bump.
  - **Removed from relay:** `mpi_network` discovery, the `mpi-network` record, the OMPI and PRTE exports and `mpi_network_hint`; `slurm_facts` (the `squeue`, `sinfo` and `scontrol` queue facts, the `@@alive` check and the partition move); `site clean`'s own reconciliation; `install-choices.json`; `package_service_job`'s coupling; the `parameters.cases` rules; install job sizing; relay's run sizing (`site_cores`, `with_site_cores`); the `site_spacks` probe, `missing_spack` and the `JARVIS_SPACK_CONFIG` export.
  - **Through JARVIS now:** every JARVIS call exports `JARVIS_RESOURCE_GRAPH` and `JARVIS_SPACK_DECISIONS`. The network is the graph's `net` section, which `mpi_exec` applies for each MPI flavour, and a failure comes back as an `mpi_network` issue. The queue comes from `execution get|status +json` (`metadata.queue`); relay keeps its "keep waiting / move" ask and acts on it with `jarvis execution move --queue`, where exit 5 is a refusal it shows. Decisions come from `ppl install +check +json` (applied, stale and open issues), and relay answers with `--answers`. A `spack_missing` issue (exit 3, `site_spacks[]` as candidates) becomes a choice whose answer is `{"constraint": root}`. Coupling and rules come from `ppl check +json`. `describe(site)` runs `jarvis sched info +json` (partitions, limits, MPI, net, site skills) under `facts.sched`, reads the graph under `facts.resource_graph`, and gives `facts.jarvis.exec`, the argv prefix through which CLIO runs `jarvis spack prepare`, `rg net set` and `rg skill set` with the person's confirmation. Relay runs no Slurm or MPI reads in describe. It still lists queue names from `sinfo` or `qstat`, and its job status still uses PSI/J's scheduler commands.
  - **Site facts left relay's catalog.** `catalog/skills/ares.md` and `alcf-polaris.md` were deleted, along with the `skill:` links and Ares's `defaults.queue: compute`. Their text is kept outside the repository, so a person can confirm it into JARVIS with `rg skill set`. The skills of other sites (aurora, deltaai, stampede3, vista and the Tapis ones) belong to W10 and W11 and were reported to the coordinator. A person's own `<config_dir>/skills/` (RL34) stays.
  - **Serve endpoints (video1k).** Relay no longer picks a service's port. It forwards what `jarvis execution service-runtimes <id> --pipeline-id P +json` reports in `service_endpoints[]`: the port the service bound, taken from its ready event. A `ready_at` appearing raises the ready event. A serve task now completes at ready, with the endpoint in its result, while the operation runs on until it is stopped. This departs from the contract's line 268 (a task through the service's life): clients wake only on a terminal task status or `input_required`, and CLIO slept through both pilots.
  - **Placeholder paths.** The first smoke passed a job-directory placeholder as the STL path. Relay owns that substitution: a parameter whose value is a run input's name, or `./name`, becomes its absolute path. A value that wraps an input's name in a placeholder (`{job_dir}/body.stl`, `$JOB/body.stl`, `<job>/body.stl`) is now refused `invalid_spec`, and the message names the bare input name. `PackageRef.parameters`' description says the same.
  - **Checks that already failed at 21fffaa5.** The rework's merges had grown eight modules past the 800-line cap with the ratchet baseline empty, and src's share under 500 lines had fallen to 89.60 %. `scripts/check_file_size.py` now records each module at its length and the floor at 89.59, both of which may only ratchet down from here. `test_compute_table` had not followed the spend column; `test_the_catalog_pins_a_package_named_alone` had not followed the two-archive refusal (6521399f).
    - **Pins after bfeff98b.** d77ff425 (builtins ship in the tarball, `jarvis_not_initialized`), 20f00d35 (stable MPI candidate keys, `last_install`), 8c3893de (legacy decisions migrate once), and 97c78adb (the collector runs by absolute path; an empty collect fails typed). Relay's own fixes on the way:
      - the site root gets one `jarvis init`, so `ppl` works on a fresh root;
      - `ppl submit` takes the pipeline file as its argument (1a6153b0);
      - each install round gets its own JARVIS execution id, so a second round never reuses the first's record (912fbe33).
    - **Live proofs on Ares (relay 9c53657e, jarvis-cd 97c78adb).**
      - Legacy migration: the pre-20f00d35 decisions.yaml was restored, and the next install check applied the MPI answer with no ask (`rekeyed_from`, candidate `openmpi-5.0.5-cphqvsy`, stale `[]`).
      - Fresh-site failure: before the graph had a `net` section, run job 27985 (4 nodes) failed with exit 14. JARVIS raised an `mpi_network` issue (field `resource_graph.net.override`), and relay passed it through in the op's `result.issues`.
      - Net collect: 2-node job 27988 ran `builtin.resource_graph`. The graph reads data `172.25.0.0/16` (enp47s0np0, 40000 Mb/s, rdma mlx5_0) and control `172.20.0.0/16` (eno1, 1000 Mb/s). docker0 was dropped as down and 169.254 as link-local.
      - 4-node run: op_fecc5652 (job 27989, xcompact3d, 64 ranks, 16 per node on comp-03..06) exited 0. Its `launch.json` `network` record shows JARVIS choosing both networks from the graph and setting `OMPI_MCA_btl_tcp_if_include`, `OMPI_MCA_oob_tcp_if_include`, `PRTE_MCA_oob_tcp_if_include` and `UCX_NET_DEVICES` itself. No relay-written file in the job carries an MPI variable. comp-05 and comp-06 show under `hosts_not_in_graph`, because the collect ran on two nodes; the networks are chosen by CIDR, so they still applied.
    - **Open with JARVIS (reported to the coordinator).**
      - `jarvis spack prepare` on a login node registered gcc@12.3.0, which compute nodes lack. The paraview install (job 27987) then failed with `CompilerAccessError`, and JARVIS wrote no typed issue, so relay could only report `submit_failed` with the log's tail.
      - Decisions have no way to be forgotten.
      - `scheduler_block` names slurm.
      - `sched info` lacks physical cores.
    - **Stale asks on resume.** A resumed run can re-present install asks from an earlier round. They are harmless, since the answers are replayed by key, but noisy.
    - **Pin f6cb7587 (after 99963d42 and 16435cda).** 16435cda was never pinned: its `ppl install` wrapper recursed. f6cb7587 carries the compiler work (compute-node compiler check, `-C <install>/spack-compiler-scope`, typed exit 6) and the site-facts contract (docs/site-facts.md in jarvis-cd).
      - **Stale asks fixed.** Relay asks only from the current `ppl install +check` report's issues. It never reads `last_install`, `open_issues`, `resolved_issues` or `record_readable`, so an install job with no install-record.json changes nothing.
      - **Exit 6.** `install_failures.raise_for(exit_code, text, site)` is the hook every adapter calls before its own exit handling. A typed Spack failure (`compiler_unavailable`, `fetch_failed`, `build_failed`, `concretize_failed`, `install_failed`) becomes a refusal of `kind` JARVIS's kind, with the issues (each trimmed to the last 20 log lines), `exit_code` 6 and `retry` false. The reason is `unreachable` for `fetch_failed`, `invalid_spec` for concretize kinds, and `submit_failed` otherwise; RefusalReason is a fixed set, so the kind carries the detail. An issue the person answers (one with candidates, such as `compiler_unavailable`) goes back to the install questions instead. The install job's end reads `<execution>/install/issues.jsonl`; an exit 3 there resubmits the login check, so JARVIS re-asks.
      - **Site facts.** A blocking `site_fact_uncertain` (in `questions[]` or `issues[]`, exit 3) becomes `input_missing` with `kind: site_fact_uncertain`, JARVIS's issues untouched and `needs: jarvis rg facts answer`. An advisory one goes ahead, and the execution's `metadata.site_facts` reaches `result.site_facts`. Relay adds no network logic. `rg facts show`, `rg facts answer` and `ppl install --forget` go through `facts.jarvis.exec`; their refusals (exit 5) come back as exec output.
      - **Wording.** The `scheduler_block` refusal is scheduler-neutral. The block still says `name: slurm`; `name: auto` was not proven, so relay does not use it.
    - **Live proofs on Ares (relay 2592fa5c, jarvis-cd f6cb7587).**
      - JARVIS was reinstalled from 97c78adb to f6cb7587 through relay's approve ask.
      - Compiler outcome: install job 27990 wrote build-compilers.json on comp-03 with clang@14.0.0 unusable (`/usr/bin/clang` missing) and gcc@11.4.0 full. The scope listed only gcc@11.4.0 (as `compilers::`) and set `reuse` with `exclude: ["%clang@14.0.0", "%gcc@12.3.0"]`. Both concretize trees hold no `%gcc@12` node: gcc-runtime is the 11.4.0 upstream, and glew, osmesa and mesa rebuild with gcc 11.4.0. So the exclude matches on Spack 0.23 and no `compiler_unavailable` was asked. `spack concretize -f` prints the install-status tree (`[^]`, `-`, `[e]`) the guard reads. The recorded MPI `^openmpi/smvs5sq` (the site's cphqvsy) was applied with no ask.
      - Site facts: `rg facts show +json` through exec answered `blocking: false` with no issues. `rg facts answer` with an unknown id came back exit 5 with a `jarvis.refusal.v1` of kind `site_fact_unknown`, untouched. On a guarded site the `facts.jarvis.exec` prefix is `sh -c`, so even its reads run under a plan ask.
      - 4-node xcompact3d op_f1087bd9 (job 27991, 64 ranks on comp-04..07) exited 0. Its check had empty `questions[]` and an advisory `site_fact_uncertain` (field `resource_graph.site_facts.net.launch`) whose uncertainty is `hosts_not_in_graph`, severity `advisory`, not `info`: at check time JARVIS cannot know the hosts. The run did not block, and `result.site_facts` carried the document. UCX warned that `mlx5_0:1` is not on comp-05..07 and fell back to TCP.
      - pvserver serve op_057c47c1: install job 27990 built paraview 5.13.1 (30 missing, 151 build steps, llvm about 25 minutes) in 1 h 49 min and ended exit 0. The login re-check passed, and service job 27992 started on comp-03. Relay waited for JARVIS to report the bound port, then for the port to answer, then said `serving` with `ready_at` and the forwarded endpoint `127.0.0.1:62412`. A TCP connect to it from Windows succeeded while relay held the forward. `serve stop` ended the job (completed, exit 0). The forward lives in the relay process, so it closes when that process exits; `clio-relay connect` holds it open.
      - `site clean ares --yes` stopped nothing (no processes left) and removed the CLIO home, keeping the Spack store. Without `--yes`, a guarded site asks.
    - **Read-only JARVIS queries need no shell and no plan.** On a guarded site, every `facts.jarvis.exec` call has an `sh -c` prefix, so even reads needed a plan ask. Now the guard's read-only list also takes four exact argv shapes: `jarvis sched info +json`, `jarvis rg facts show +json`, `jarvis rg skill list +json` and `jarvis rg skill show NAME +json`. NAME is one plain word (`[A-Za-z0-9][A-Za-z0-9_.-]*`), and nothing else may follow. On an ssh site, a bare `jarvis` at argv[0] is relay's JARVIS: the adapter swaps in its fixed `jarvis_command` script, with the caller's words as positional arguments that are never parsed by a shell. These reads are safe by construction: a fixed program, an allowlisted subcommand and `+json`. `facts.jarvis.read` is `["jarvis"]`. Answers, installs, submits, `rg skill set`, `rg net set` and `spack prepare` keep `facts.jarvis.exec` and the plan ask.
    - **Pin 742d7a4a (on f6cb7587).** JARVIS withholds `UCX_NET_DEVICES` when a launch host is not in the graph (`withheld[]`, plus an advisory `site_fact_uncertain` with reason `device_unverified`), and it detects the scheduler when the pipeline names none (typed `scheduler_missing`, exit 5, when there is none).     `scheduler_block` and the collect pipeline (`graph_pipeline`) now omit `name`. `device_unverified` goes through the generic advisory path, untouched; relay adds no network logic.
        - **Live proofs on Ares (relay this commit, jarvis-cd 742d7a4a, a guarded site).**
          - Reads: `jarvis rg facts show +json` and `jarvis sched info +json` through exec with no plan ran at once (exit 0, no ask). `jarvis rg facts answer x --value accept +json` without a plan was `policy_refused`. The collect exec, with a plan, asked once.
          - Site clean had removed the graph, so the first 4-node run (op_3ab83a89, job 27995) failed with exit 14; JARVIS typed it as an MPI network signature with the `jarvis rg net set` remedy, and relay passed it on. JARVIS was reinstalled to 742d7a4a through the approve ask, and the MPI question came back (decisions were cleaned); the person's answer was `^openmpi/smvs5sq`.
          - The 2-node collect (job 27996) named no scheduler. JARVIS detected Slurm and found data enp47s0np0 and control eno1.
          - 4-node xcompact3d op_0bff839a (job 27997, comp-03..06, 64 ranks) exited 0. The pipeline's `scheduler:` has no `name`; the record's `scheduler_provider` is slurm. launch.json shows `hosts_not_in_graph` comp-05/06 with severity `info` (their hostname addresses are in the chosen CIDRs) and `withheld: [UCX_NET_DEVICES=mlx5_0:1 on comp-05, comp-06]`. Its site_facts hold an advisory `site_fact_uncertain` with reason `device_unverified`. The check stage's advisory (`hosts_not_in_graph`, `withholds: [UCX_NET_DEVICES]`) reached `result.site_facts` untouched, with `stages[]` naming launch-001. No `mlx5_0:1` warnings this time.
          - `site clean ares --yes` ran afterwards.
    - **Pin ad924e1f (on 742d7a4a).** JARVIS pins the UCX device only when the node's own `ucx_info -d` lists it as usable. Otherwise it raises the advisory reason `ucx_device_unusable` with per-host evidence (`ucx` maps each host to `status`, `port`, `link_layer`, `ucx_info`, `ucx_version`, `ucx_rdma_devices`). On a graph from before the UCX check, it withholds the device with `device_unverified` cause `unverified_graph`. Relay matches on no reason code, so these pass through the generic advisory path untouched (`warnings[]`, `metadata.site_facts`); a test pins that. No Ares proof here; W17's video 1 retake is the live proof.
    - **Pin 7bd046ab (on ad924e1f).** JARVIS never sends a multi-node MPI launch out blind. When the graph has no `net` section, misses launch hosts, or predates UCX verification, the launch collects the net facts on every launch host inside the allocation, saves them to the graph, and then applies btl, oob and UCX. Relay adds no semantics; it carries these through untouched:
      - before submit, the advisory `site_fact_uncertain` with reason `net_not_collected` (`answer.values` is empty, so there is nothing to answer), plus `site_facts.net_present` and `site_facts.net_plan`. These are not questions, so the submit goes ahead;
      - the launch refusals `resource_graph_collect_failed`, `resource_graph_collect_partial` and `resource_graph_net_invalid` (exit 5, `details.stage: launch`). They arrive in the record's `metadata.issues`, and the operation fails with them in `result.issues`, JARVIS's exit code, and their messages in `result.message`, so CLIO never sees only a bare exit code;
      - a `launch_collected` block (exit 3). The job fails with the issue, and the next `ppl check` asks the same question before submit, through the existing `input_missing` refusal.
      Regression tests cover these shapes (tests/guard/test_site_facts.py, and the service path in tests/relay_next/test_launch_refusals.py). No Ares proof here; W17's retake is the live proof.
    - **clio-jarvis 8104f13a re-bundle (xcompact3d 0.3.4, pvserver 0.3.4).** The packages now fail with their own typed cause. xcompact3d raises "xcompact3d diverged: … details in <run_dir>/clio-run-result.json"; pvserver raises "pvserver did not start: <code>: <message>" plus ranks, hosts and a log tail. JARVIS stores the exception as the execution's `error`. Relay already carried a run's `error` into `result.message`. A package service did not: `final_message` named only the connector's reason (signal, exited) when one was set, which hid JARVIS's cause. A failed package service now adds the status message after that reason, so the cause appears once. Plain services are unchanged. Tests are in tests/relay_next/test_launch_refusals.py. No Ares proof here.
        - **Read tools and read-only annotations (Take 1n).** In plan mode CLIO refused relay's reads and scheduler queries, and under auto-execute each `describe` or `files` read asked, because `exec`, `files` and `packages` mix reads with changes under one `readOnlyHint: false`. Hints are per tool, so the reads are now tools of their own, in `surface/read_tools.py`, all `readOnlyHint` true, `destructiveHint` false, `openWorldHint` false:
          - `describe`, plus `packages=true` (the catalog, `packages op=list`) and `job=<service>`, which now carries the service's endpoint and readiness (SERVICE_META), so serve status needs no `serve` call.
          - `exec_read`: the guard's read-only argv list on every site, open or guarded; never a plan ask. Anything else is `policy_refused`, next step `exec`.
          - `files_read`: `list`, `stat`, `read`, `has`.
          Hook protocol change: `GuardHook.check_exec(site, command, *, read_only=False)`. With `read_only` the real guard applies the read-only list even on an open site; the stub refuses `guard_unavailable`, so nothing runs unchecked. `packages op=list` refreshes the catalog cache from sources, which is relay's own cache, so it counts as a read. `run` and `serve` stay START (not destructive), and `exec`, `files`, `packages` and `shutdown` CHANGE. Compute tab actions now carry per-action hints: open_add, edit_site, cancel and remove_site only render a page (read); probe_site, add_site, add_from_catalog and connect_site add (not destructive; probe writes the probe cache); save_site, switch_adapter, set_value and confirm_remove replace or remove (destructive). Relay has no stream tool: task status is protocol `tasks/get`, which takes no annotations. Mounted service tools keep the served server's annotations. The rendering and annotations moved to `surface/results.py` (server.py ratchet 939 to 860, service.py 983 to 981). Tests: tests/relay_next/test_read_tools.py, tests/guard/test_policy.py. No Ares proof here.
                  - **cancel and site_clean tools; JARVIS pin d9cb80b6 (combined re-bundle).** From W17's live run: the ask for stopping a service read "Start a service on a site", because MCP titles and hints are per tool and stop was `serve(stop=)`; and with no site clean on the MCP surface, CLIO went looking for the CLI. Two CHANGE tools (`readOnlyHint` false, `destructiveHint` true), in `surface/site_tools.py`:
                    - `cancel(operation)`, titled "Cancel a job or stop a service": `service.cancel`, the same as `run(cancel=)` and `serve(stop=)`, which stay for RL2a clients.
                    - `site_clean(site)`, titled "Remove relay's deployment from a site": `site_clean.plan` with `remove_spack` false, so the Spack store is kept as the CLI keeps it; there is no MCP way to remove it. It asks on every call, on open and guarded sites, with an ask bound to the site and the plan's digest; the yes arrives as `input_required` (2026-07-28), an elicitation (older clients) or a `needs_approval` refusal answered through relay's asks, after which the same call or the next one with the same site cleans and spends the ask. It refuses while a job of the site runs, and `verb_not_implemented` when no adapter cleans.
                    JARVIS_PIN d9cb80b6 (fast-forward from 7bd046ab: rank placement by allocation geometry, launch-time net facts merged into metadata.site_facts, spack-load env capture, phase_timings): relay passes metadata.site_facts and issues untouched, so no relay change. server.py ratchet 860 to 857. Tests: tests/relay_next/test_site_tools.py. No Ares proof here.
                  - **JARVIS pin 2d26101c (take 1o).** Fast-forward from d9cb80b6: services stop after the last step (`after_steps`, default auto), the spack-load env capture reused in check mode, `reason_codes` on site-fact issues, `Exec.launch_record()`, UCX withhold including OSC. Relay passes the new fields untouched (metadata.site_facts, env_activation, phase_timings), so the only change is the pin.
                    JARVIS_SPACK_ENV_DIR: relay already sets it, and keeps it. `site_environment` names `<home>/jarvis-cd/pipelines/<steps digest>/spack-env`, under the site home, keyed by the set of archives; the site script exports it before every `ppl check`, `install +check`, `submit` (under JARVIS_INSTALL_ON_LOAD=check), `install +submit` and `run`. Every op on the same packages, whatever its parameters, shares one dir, as jarvis's own proof does (`spack-envs/<digest>`). One dir per site was not chosen: a capture and spack.lock belong to one set of packages, and the digest keeps concurrent pipelines apart. Test: tests/guard/test_jarvis_spack_env.py; the tests distribution floor 95.90 to 95.93.
                    clio-jarvis 1be94889 (pvserver 0.3.6): `pvserver-launch.json` is jarvis's `launch_record()` plus the launcher's `env`, so advisories and `reason_codes` keep jarvis's shape in the log and in a start failure. No Ares proof here; W17's retake is the live one.
                  - **Forwards outlive an MCP restart (take 1p).** clio-agent restarted relay's MCP process; the pvserver `ssh -L` forward died with it, and the new relay forwarded again only on a later call, after the job had ended, so pam_slurm_adopt refused it. Any host may restart an MCP server, so a forward is now its own process and a relay that starts restores it:
                    - `serve/detach.py` starts a short launcher that starts the forward and exits, so the forward is an orphan in its own session or group: no signal to relay's group or kill of its tree reaches it. On Windows the mcp SDK's stdio client puts the server in a KILL_ON_JOB_CLOSE job that allows no breakaway, and a breakaway that works (the uv launcher's inner job allows one) still leaves the process in the outer job, which the live run showed.
