So on Windows the launcher is started by the WMI service (`Win32_Process.Create`), outside every job; a breakaway is the fallback, then an in-job start that the next relay restores. The argv and env go through a 0600 file the launcher removes first.
                    - On stop relay releases a forward rather than killing it. A relay that starts (server lifespan to `serve_hook.status` to `resume`) adopts the forward that still holds the ports, so there is never a second one, or starts it again at once. Stop, shutdown and site clean kill it by its recorded pid; a forward to a node ends with the job.
                    - The endpoint is `durable` unless the forward rides the interactive sign-in master (`ControlPath`), which closes with relay; it was false for every Windows ssh forward.
                    Proof (files/proof-forward-restart.txt, ares job 28033): with relay under the SDK's job, the host closed relay and the port still answered; a second relay adopted the same forward (one ssh holding 127.0.0.1:64526); after the forward was killed, a third relay restored it and the port answered within 1 s with no tool call. Tests: tests/serve/test_forward_restart.py.
                  - **An exec returns when its command exits (take 1p).** Two exec_read calls hung 23+ minutes after the command had finished: `run_process` ended only on EOF of stdout and stderr and ssh's exit, and an ssh whose session stays up (a background child holding the output, a jump host's proxy, keepalives) never gave either. ControlMaster is off for ares, and no forward rides an exec's connection. Now:
                    - every `SshTransport.ssh` script runs in a subshell followed by a line on stderr with a per-call random marker and the exit code. `run_process` strips that line, and once it arrives gives ssh `EXIT_GRACE_S` (5 s) to end, then stops it and returns the marker's code with `remote_exit` set. A call whose command reported its exit is never retried.
                    - the exec watchdog's `cat` (holding the session's stdin) is stopped with the watchdog, so nothing of relay's stays in the session.
                    - on Windows a stop kills ssh's tree (`taskkill /T`), so a wrapper's ssh and its ProxyCommand go too.
                    Proof (files/proof-exec-exit.txt, ares login node): `sh -c 'sleep 20 & echo command-exited; exit 3'` took 21.5 s before and 6.4 s through relay's exec path, with exit 3; `hostname` 1.0 s; exec_read through the MCP server 1.1 s. Tests: tests/relay_ssh/test_ssh_exit.py; the tests distribution floor 95.96 to 96.00.
                  - **Install ahead of a run: the `install` tool (off-camera rebuild).** CLIO, asked in plain words to install pvserver and Xcompact3d on Ares without running anything, searched source for 25 min: install happened only as the install task inside `run` or `serve`. Now `install(site, spec)` is a START task tool (`surface/install_tool.py`, `surface/install_op.py`), an operation of kind `install`:
                    - relay asks JARVIS `ppl install yaml <pipeline> +check +json` for the same pipeline a run would submit, through the site script's new `check` mode (adapter hook `install_check`: ssh, local and worker's `/install-check`). Exit 0 with `satisfied: true` completes the operation with `installed` (reuse and build per package) and "nothing ran".
                    - exit 3 takes the run's install path unchanged (`surface/installing.py`): the same approval and choices, `ppl install +submit` as a job on the compute nodes, then the check again. The ask says nothing runs after it (`install_wording.install_message(run=False)`).
                    - exit 69 (no relay jarvis-cd, as after site clean) is the run's bootstrap ask, then `install_jarvis`, then the check. So the install op makes JARVIS available on a site on its own.
                    - no spend ask and no charge (it is JARVIS's install job, sized by JARVIS's install walltime); guard check_run and package review still apply. A site whose scheduler is not Slurm refuses `adapter_unavailable` (`needs: a Slurm site`), since `+submit` needs one.
                    JARVIS needed nothing new: `ppl install +check` and `+submit` were there. The typed exits moved to `jarvis_exits.py` (shared by every adapter), the ask wording to `install_wording.py`. There is no CLI `run` verb, so none for install either. Ratchets: jarvis_submit 2075 to 2026, service 981 to 980, worker adapter 1080 to 1077, local 919 to 918, src distribution 89.94 to 90.16, tests 96.00 to 96.03. Tests: tests/relay_next/test_install_op.py, tests/guard/test_scripts.py.
                  - **Concurrent installs on one site (install-only rerun).** CLIO started `install` for xcompact3d and pvserver at once on a clean Ares. Both asked for jarvis-cd and both bootstrapped into `~/.clio-relay/jarvis-cd/venv` at once; one failed with `No module named 'pip._vendor.rich._emoji_codes'` (two pips writing one venv). Now:
                    - the bootstrap script (`jarvis_bootstrap.py`, moved out of `jarvis_submit.py`) takes `<home>/jarvis-cd/.relay.lock` (flock, or a `mkdir` lock where flock is missing) and reuses an install already at the pin (`"from":"reused"`). A second bootstrap waits for the first and does nothing. The site script's requirements pip install into that venv takes the same lock.
                    - the jarvis-cd approval is site-scoped: its binding is `{site, scope: "site", kind, jarvis pin}`, with no spec digest (`asks.core.approval_covers`). Under a cross-process lock on the asks store, an operation reuses one another live operation on the site holds, pending or accepted while that install is unfinished (`surface/install_share.py`). An answer moves on every holder (`holders`, also in the CLI); cancelling one holder leaves the ask to the others (`withdrawable`); a decline cancels all.
                    - the package installs are left to JARVIS and Spack, which already lock: JARVIS takes an exclusive flock on `<JARVIS_SPACK_ENV_DIR>/.jarvis-env.lock` around every env write (shared for check and load), the site-facts store locks, and Spack locks its DB and prefixes. Relay's env dir is per steps digest, so the same package set shares one env under that lock and different sets get different envs.
                    Left open: an operation that arrives just after another's jarvis-cd install finished asks again, and its bootstrap then reuses; one extra ask, no corruption. JARVIS writes decisions atomically but does not lock the read-modify-write, so two answers at the same instant could lose one, which means a re-ask (a JARVIS gap, reported). Ratchets: jarvis_submit 2026 to 1881, src distribution 90.16 to 90.27, tests 96.03 to 96.06. Tests: tests/relay_next/test_install_concurrency.py (with a POSIX test running two real bootstraps at once: one build and one reuse; with the lock stripped, two builds).
                  - **Shared JARVIS questions, and clean keeps the answers (off-camera install).** Concurrent installs each asked "Which MPI", a site-scope JARVIS question, and `site_clean` deleted `jarvis-cd/site/decisions.yaml`, so the next run asked it again. Now:
                    - a JARVIS issue with `scope: site` is bound `{site, scope: "site", kind: "jarvis_choice", issue_id, options}` (`install_share.choice_ask`). JARVIS's `issue_id` digests the question with its candidates, and the options are in the binding too, so only an identical question is shared. Under the asks lock, an operation reuses the choose ask another live operation on the site holds, pending or accepted; the one answer reaches each install's `answers` under its `issue_id`. Cancelling one holder leaves it to the others, a decline cancels all (the jarvis-cd ask's `holders` and `withdrawable`). A package-scope question, or one with other candidates, stays per operation.
                    - clean keeps the decisions file beside the Spack store (`site_prune.site_keeps`, used by the ssh and worker adapters). `site_clean(forget_decisions=true)` and `site clean --forget-decisions` drop it; `remove_spack` alone keeps it. The finer tool is JARVIS's `jarvis ppl install --forget <selector>` (all, an issue id, mpi/compiler/spack, a package, a constraint), already in the pin 2d26101c. The ask's text says when the answers go.
                    - the prune resolves a kept file through its directory, as it does kept directories, so a kept file inside a removed target survives a symlinked home (checked on Ares in a temp dir: decisions.yaml kept, the graph and venv removed).
                    The jarvis pin is unchanged (W17 is recording take 1q); jarvis-cd 465cc55f's decision-store lock comes with the next bump. Ratchet: tests 96.06 to 96.12. Tests: tests/relay_next/test_install_choices.py, tests/relay_next/test_site_clean_decisions.py.
                  - **Take 1q: serve ready on time, no needless install ask, cancel and clean.** Three gaps from take 1q:
                    - serve woke the agent only after the job ended. A task poll's `status()` waited on the service's lock while the follower ran a whole round of site calls, and readiness needed the connector's ready.json. Now a poll answers from the follower's latest look while that is fresh (`FRESH_S` 60, `FOLLOW_MAX_S` 5), and in ssh mode the node and port JARVIS reports ready make the service up without ready.json (`plan.runtime_ready_host`). The task completes at readiness with the endpoint (plain task completion, which CLIO already wakes on; no new signal), and the job keeps running until cancel or stop.
                    - an install ask came although the kept store had both packages: `+check` reported only `needs_decision` (which MPI) and relay read that as "build". Now a round with only questions asks just them, then runs the site script's `recheck` mode with the answers (`ppl install +check`): exit 7 (`EXIT_INSTALLED`, `software_installed`) completes the install op or resubmits the run, nothing asked or built; exit 3 takes the install path with its approval. Rounds are capped at 4.
                    - cancel and clean. The CLI has `clio-relay cancel OPERATION`, the cancel tool's call and view. `site clean` now refuses while relay's jobs run (chosen over cancelling them: clean stays a removal, cancel stays the person's own call), and asks the site's scheduler (adapter protocol `ListsLiveJobs`, `site_prune.jobs_script`: squeue, bjobs, flux or qstat, for the person's jobs named `relay-...` and the job ids relay's records hold in any state), not relay's records alone; the prune on the site checks again before it deletes. The refusal names each job, its operation and state, and says to cancel them with relay's cancel. `cancel` on an ended operation whose job the scheduler still lists cancels that job and notes it in the history (`site_clean.leftover_cancel`). Job 28048's 143 was W17's manual `scancel`; clean's empty cancelled list was relay's records showing no live job.
                    Splits for the ratchets: `jarvis_report.py` (the install report, out of jarvis_submit), `adapters/ssh/statuses.py`, `adapters/worker/batch_ssh.py`, `serve/runtime_util.py`. Ratchets: jarvis_submit 1881 to 1691, ssh adapter 1251 to 1207, worker adapter 1077 to 1053, serve runtime 948 to 947, service 980 to 979, src distribution 90.27 to 90.47, tests 96.12 to 96.21. Tests: tests/serve/test_runtime_ready.py, tests/serve/test_plan.py, tests/relay_next/test_install_recheck.py, tests/relay_next/test_site_clean_live.py, tests/guard/test_scripts.py (recheck; POSIX, also run by hand in Git Bash, as was the jobs and prune guard with a stand-in squeue).
                                      - **JARVIS pin 5a58ab82 (combined re-bundle after take 1q).** Fast-forward from 2d26101c, two commits: 465cc55f locks the install decision store's read-modify-write (a flock, msvcrt on Windows), which closes the "two answers at the same instant could lose one" gap left open above; 5a58ab82 probes the build host's compilers before the first build and types an autoconf, CMake or meson "cannot create executables" failure as `compiler_unavailable` with candidates (`JARVIS_COMPILER_PROBE=off` keeps the existence-only check). Relay relays both as JARVIS issues untouched, so the only change is the pin.
                                      - **Take 1r: files by path, one answer moves every holder, one review per job.** Three gaps from take 1r (relay 9791465e):
                                        - file bytes went through the agent: CLIO sent a 4.4 MB STL with `files` in 2 MB base64 chunks from run_code and read each back, which overflowed clio-agent's event store. Now `files` has `upload` (`local_path` to `path`) and `download` (`path` to `local_path`) (`surface/files_transfer.py`). Relay reads or writes the local path itself, streams it through the adapter's own file access in chunks (`transfer_chunk_bytes`: 32 MiB over ssh, where a chunk is one ssh call; 4 MiB elsewhere), checks the SHA-256 where the file landed and returns only `{path, local_path, size, sha256, verified}`. `verified` is `site` when the access hashes in place (optional protocol `SiteChecksum.sha256`: ssh runs `sha256sum` or `shasum -a 256`, local hashes in a thread), else `read_back` (upload) or `size` (download). A download goes to `<local_path>.relay-part` and moves into place only after the check. `local_path` is absolute or `~` on the relay host; a relative one refuses (relay's working directory is not the agent's). MCP roots were not used: `roots/list` is deprecated in the 2026-07-28 revision. A replacing upload on a guarded site takes the replace ask a write does. Relay opens the path with its own launch's permissions, so a host that sandboxes relay (clio-agent's MXC fence) must let that launch read the workspace; an unreachable path refuses `policy_refused` and says so. Proven on Ares through `SshFileAccess`: 4.4 MB up in three forced 2 MiB chunks and down, both `verified: site`, bytes equal, the scratch dir removed. The files tool registration moved to `surface/files_tool.py`.
                                        - MPI asked twice although relay shared the ask: both ops held `ask_8b2010479e0d31a3db15b5d2`, but an answer through tasks/update, an elicitation or a resumed round advanced only the calling op. The other holder moved only on its own tasks/get, which clio-agent does not send while a prompt shows, and both tasks listed the same ask in `input_requests`. Now each answer path calls `install_share.advance_holders`, which moves every holder at once. The modern protocol has no task status notification, so a host still sees the other task's new state on its next poll.
                                        - install asks: the two relay_install permissions are the host's tool permissions, not relay's. The package reviews are the guard's, one per unreviewed archive hash (real: new clio-jarvis 0.3.7 archives), and the jarvis-cd ask was real after site_clean and already shared. Now a job's unreviewed packages are one review ask (`guard.policy.reviews_message`, binding `{kind: package_review, packages: [...]}`; `reviews.harvest` records each, and a tampered item records none), and use-relay steers a plan's packages into one `install` with `packages`, for one review, one JARVIS check and one install job.
                                        Ratchets: server 857 to 815, src distribution 90.47 to 90.57, tests 96.21 to 96.24. Tests: tests/relay_next/test_files_by_path.py, tests/relay_ssh/test_ssh_files.py (POSIX), tests/relay_next/test_install_choices.py, tests/guard/test_policy.py.
                                      - **JARVIS pin 0fa2b312: no Spack on the login host.** W5 found that `+check` (report, check, recheck) concretized on the Ares login node for minutes. jarvis-cd 0fa2b312 (jaimecernuda-execution-lifecycle) makes the submitting host of a pipeline with a `scheduler:` block answer `ppl install +check` from the `install-check.json` record a job's check wrote, while its inputs match. With no matching record it says `install_unchecked` (answerable false, a `reason` such as `no_record`, `inputs_changed` or `answers_pending`). This supersedes the exit 7 recheck above:
                                        - a submit (`submit` mode) asks that light check first and stops with exit 3 and the report before `ppl check` or `ppl submit`, so the person is still asked before the run's job is submitted. `JARVIS_INSTALL_ON_LOAD=check` stays on `ppl submit`; JARVIS now makes that check in the allocation. `ppl submit`'s own exit 3 prints the report on stdout, which the script keeps as `relay.install-report.json`, instead of running `+check` again;
                                        - `install_unchecked` is neither a blocking issue nor a question. A report with only it asks nothing and runs `recheck`, now a job: `ppl install yaml <install pipeline> --answers A +check +submit` (walltime the shorter of the site's install walltime and 1 h). A round with only questions asks them, then runs the same check job with the answers. When the check job ends, relay submits again and the light check reads the new record. If that record still does not match, the op fails typed (`check_unrecorded`, `submit_failed`, with the reason) rather than looping. Missing software takes the approve-then-install path as before (`+submit`, no `+check`);
                                        - exit 7 (`EXIT_INSTALLED`, `software_installed`) is gone; rounds are capped at 6, since a fresh site can take a check job, a question and a check job with the answers. Install job outcomes are still read from the execution record's `metadata.install` and `issues.jsonl`. The shared jarvis-cd and site-scope asks are unchanged: concurrent ops on a site with no record each submit a check job, and a shared question still moves every holder.
                                        Ratchets: jarvis_submit 1691 to 1689 (the `jarvis_python` setting check moved to `jarvis_bootstrap.py`). Tests: tests/relay_next/test_install_recheck.py, tests/guard/test_scripts.py (POSIX).
                                      - **Take 1s gaps, and JARVIS pin 58cd5b1a (install placement).** Root causes and fixes:
                                        - production forward with `host_keys: []`: the plan read the node's keys in one exec that hung for 120 s, and its error was swallowed. Keys are now read in short tries (`serve/keys.py`, 20 s, twice) whenever the forward's host changes, and three failed rounds become a typed `host_key_unverified` service error. A forward whose ssh says "Host key verification failed" stops (`serve/forward_exit.classify_exit`), reads the keys once more, and otherwise reports the refusal instead of restarting;
                                        - a forward whose job ended (`pam_slurm_adopt`) stops, and the service finishes once the scheduler says the job ended;
                                        - readiness 7 to 10 minutes late: `ready_at` was stamped only after a follower round, and `port_bound` took any bind error (a Windows excluded port range) as a listener. `port_state` now counts only EADDRINUSE/10048, a foreign holder is adopted after two looks, and a task poll answers from the follower's look at once;
                                        - the resource_graph ask now says it submits a job (`ExecPlan.submits`, with nodes and walltime from the pipeline);
                                        - the jarvis-cd placement ask came from relay's bootstrap. On a guarded site it is folded into the package review (its binding carries the pin); other sites keep one shared site ask, since nothing else asks there first;
                                        - `site_clean` names the outputs relay's runs wrote outside its directories (from the operations' records), keeps them by default and deletes them only with `remove_outputs` (`--remove-outputs`); the result lists `outputs_kept`;
                                        - a service relay cannot bring to the desktop carries `service_error`, and its result is an error until it was ready.
                                        Install placement (jarvis-cd 58cd5b1a, `docs/scheduler.md` "Install placement"): relay only relays it. `install_placement` issues are site-scope questions with JARVIS's candidates, recommendation and evidence (the first 6 lines), and per-package options (`<choice> for <package> only`) that answer the override's issue id. An `.occasion` question is never shared and says it is for this run only. The `recheck` mode first runs `ppl install +check --answers` on the submitting host, so JARVIS records the answers and checks there when the site chose the login host; a check job goes only when JARVIS still says `install_unchecked`. A recheck that JARVIS answered here returns a `checked_here` handle, and the install resubmits at once (a still-unrecorded check fails typed). Decision: relay declares `JARVIS_INSTALL_PLACEMENT_BUILD=compute_job` as the caller default for builds and builds only through `ppl install +submit`, so a fresh site is not asked where to build; a site's recorded answer still wins. Login builds stay a question for the user. `SITE_SCRIPT` moved to `jarvis_site_script.py`.
                                        Ratchets: jarvis_submit 1689 to 1425, ssh adapter 1207 to 1203, local 918 to 916, serve runtime 947 to 942, src distribution 90.57 to 90.72, tests 96.24 to 96.35. Tests: tests/serve/test_forward_exit.py, tests/serve/test_runtime_ready.py, tests/relay_next/test_install_placement.py, tests/relay_next/test_clean_outputs.py, tests/relay_next/test_service_error.py, tests/guard/test_scripts.py (POSIX).
                                        Build placement offered like the check (user-directed: "this is usually fine if we controll the limits and ask the user", per cluster and per package). Reverses the earlier caller default: the site script exports no `JARVIS_INSTALL_PLACEMENT_BUILD` (it unsets both placement variables and `JARVIS_ALLOW_LOCAL_BUILD`), so a site that has not answered gets JARVIS's own recommendation in its `install_placement` question; Ares still gets JARVIS's compute recommendation until the person decides. The install mode routes on JARVIS's `placement.build.choice` from its `+check` report: `compute_job`, or a login host JARVIS now defers (`evidence.limits.defer`), goes through `ppl install +submit` as before; `login_limited` starts JARVIS's own `ppl install --answers` on the login host, detached under `<job dir>/relay.login-build/` with the supervisor files the adapters already follow (ssh: `pid`, `child_pid`, `exit_code`, `cancel`; local and worker: `state.json`), and returns a `clio-relay.login-build.v1` handle (`jarvis_exits.login_build`, native id `pid:N`). Relay sets no limits (JARVIS sets and applies them) and no time limit: the install is a background task with progress from the build log, and only the person's cancel stops it (site clean refuses while it runs, as with any live job of relay's). Exit 4 from a login build reads JARVIS's report and is `policy_refused`, never retried. A recheck never builds. `build_refused` moved from `jarvis_submit` to `jarvis_exits`; the install log readers moved from `surface/installing.py` to `surface/install_logs.py`. Ratchets: jarvis_submit 1425 to 1392, ssh adapter 1203 to 1201, src distribution 90.76%, tests 96.40%.
                                        `packages_read` (take 1s, coordinator): in plan mode CLIO's `packages op=list` was denied, since `packages` also stages and removes and so is `readOnlyHint: false`. Hints are per tool and relay uses only plain MCP annotations, so listing is now a read tool of its own, `packages_read` (no arguments; READ: `readOnlyHint` true, `destructiveHint` false, `openWorldHint` false), and `packages` keeps only `stage` and `remove` (CHANGE, `package` required). `packages op=list` is now a schema error rather than a second way to list, so a host never routes a read to the change tool. `describe(packages=true)` still lists the same catalog. Thirteen tools; the tool-list tests name `packages_read`, and test_read_tools checks its hints and that it matches describe.
                                        Take 1t gaps (W17, coordinator), and JARVIS pin acbf4308:
                                        1. Outputs outside relay's state (data loss): xcompact3d's run_dir defaulted to JARVIS's shared directory, `<remote_dir>/jobs/<op>/.relay-jarvis/shared`, so site clean deleted 11 GiB. Each remote adapter now has `outputs_dir` (default `clio-relay-runs` under home; the ssh config refuses one inside `remote_dir`). The adapter hands it to the site script as `CLIO_RELAY_OUTPUTS` in the job's environment (`site_outputs.with_outputs`, only on the command-building calls, so record digests do not change). The script unsets it, keeps JARVIS's shared directory at `<outputs_dir>/<op>`, and links it from the job root, so clean removes the link only (`launch.sh` follows links with `find -L`). `relay_state.refuse_relay_state` refuses, as `invalid_spec` and before any ask, a run or serve whose job names a path inside the adapter's clean targets or `~/.clio-relay` and `~/.config/clio-relay`, or a site whose `outputs_dir` is inside them (this covers the worker, whose site side is the local adapter, given `outputs_dir` through `_site_config`). Clean keeps every listed output unless `remove_outputs`, even one inside its targets (`_in_job_dir` excludes only the op's own job dir). describe shows `outputs_dir` (`site_outputs.dir_facts`). The local adapter takes `outputs_dir` unset by default (nothing cleans it directly).
                                        2. Placement asked twice: jarvis 58cd5b1a keyed a site answer per cost class (`install_placement.check.long` for paraview, `.check.short` for xcompact3d), so one round raised two site questions. That keying is jarvis's, so it was fixed there: jarvis-cd acbf4308 has one site decision per phase (`install_placement.check`, `.build`), which covers every package; older per-class answers still count. Relay changed only the pin, since it already shares a site-scope question by issue id.
                                        3. One ask per call: CLIO answers a plain confirm itself once the person has allowed the call (fence session, coordinator), so a plain confirm may carry nothing beyond the call's arguments. `hooks.approve_schema(plain=...)`: plain is one boolean, and typed is a titled `approve`/`decline` choice. `said_yes` reads both. Typed: spend, review, install, jarvis-cd install, site clean (tool and CLI). Plain: exec plans (resource_graph's submit) and files delete/replace, whose text lost the size fact. resource_graph's confirm is therefore the host permission plus nothing new.
                                        4. No MPI ask: Ares's `~/.clio-relay/jarvis-cd/site/decisions.yaml` kept the earlier MPI decision (openmpi-5.0.5) and the placement answers, because clean keeps decisions unless `forget_decisions`. Relay relayed nothing because JARVIS asked nothing. A "from clean" take uses `forget_decisions=true`. The clean question now says the answers stay and how to forget them.
                                        5. Pilot linger overlapped production: `surface/held_services.py`. When a run or serve opens on a site where another of relay's services is RUNNING, one choice ask joins the job's asks: end them when this job is submitted, or keep them (named with their package, nodes and start time). A decline or KEEP leaves them; END cancels them through `cancel` right after the new job moves to SUBMITTING, so the review lasts until then. Relay cannot tell a lingering service from an active one, so the ask covers any running service of the site.
                                        `tests/serve/test_forward_restart.py` fails now and then on Windows under load, on the base 49cf7e02 too (a pid race with the venv's python.exe stand-in). It is not from these changes.

                                        Take 1t proof on Ares, through the stdio MCP path: demo.hello on 1 compute node (slurm 28288, ares-comp-10) wrote `~/clio-relay-runs/<op>/.../hello-out.txt`, and the job root's `shared` was a link to it. The site clean kept the outputs and `decisions.yaml`. The proof found that the run's outputs were still named through the link (`~/.clio-relay/jobs/<op>/.relay-jarvis/shared/...`), so they dangled after a clean and the clean question did not name them. Fix: the site script writes the real shared directory to `relay.shared` (a site file, never an output) and prints a `clio-relay.shared.v1` line. `jarvis_submit.shared_dir` reads that line, and `handle_data(shared=)` puts the execution there (ssh submit and install, and the local adapter from the file). `package_progress._shared` reads the file through `files`. Serve's `launch.sh` names files under the link by their real path, and `serve.report.listed_artifacts` keeps an absolute name as is. `site_clean.run_outputs` lists a run's outputs in a directory named for its operation as that one directory, so the question names `~/clio-relay-runs/<op>` once. Tests: test_outputs_named.py, test_outputs_dir.py, test_clean_outputs.py.
                                                                                Job card, commit A (user-directed: one ask per submit, and it is the job's card; the card is the job everywhere in gact-tui). Contract agreed with fence (194ec97f) and W17 (db3592cf), `docs/mcp-tasks.md` "tools that confirm themselves" and "the job card". Keys are org-level so any iowarp server can adopt them: tool `_meta["io.iowarp/confirm"]={"version":1}` on run, serve and exec, and the host's request flag `params._meta["io.iowarp/confirm"]="server"`. A flagged call raises one typed form (`surface/confirm.py`: an `approve` choice, plus `running_services` when held services apply); spend and review fold into it, sign-in stays separate, no forms is `needs_approval`, a flagged call that asks nothing returns `"not_needed"`, cancel and stop get their own form, and flagged exec on an unguarded site asks for that command. The snapshot (`surface/job_card.py`, `io.iowarp.clio-relay/job`, full each time, `revision` per change, keyed by `operation_id`) rides the confirm, every task status `_meta`, `structuredContent.job` and `describe(site=...).jobs`. Fact: the `2026-07-28` schema gives a request embedded in `input_required` no `_meta`, and the SDK's per-version sieve drops it, so on that wire the snapshot rides the `InputRequiredResult`'s own `_meta` (`elicit.input_required`); older connections get it in the elicitation's `params._meta`. In commit A the plan is relay's own (`source: relay`, no couplings or script); commit B fills it from JARVIS's `+plan`. Tests: tests/relay_next/test_job_card.py.
                                                                                Job card, commit B: the card is JARVIS's own plan (jarvis-cd 3b651c68, `+plan`, `--plan_sha256`, `components.jsonl`). `AdapterBase.plan_job(spec, operation_id=)` (default `None`) runs the site script's new `plan` mode, which stages, runs `jarvis ppl submit --execution-id E +plan +json` (no Spack, no job, no record) and exits 10 (`jarvis_plan.EXIT_PLANNED`) with the plan in `relay.plan.json`; ssh and local implement it. `surface/plan_card.py` puts the plan on the confirm (`source: jarvis`, components with package, nodes, ranks and `host_slice`, couplings, script) and `jarvis_plan.bound(spec, plan)` passes the yes to `run` through `CLIO_RELAY_PLAN`, which the site script captures and unsets before JARVIS starts, so the submit carries `--plan_sha256`. A JARVIS that cannot plan submits unbound and the card stays relay-sourced. Fact: jarvis-cd's ArgParse ignores an unknown `+flag` unless `strict_unknown_args` (checked at acbf4308), so an older JARVIS given `+plan` would submit for real. Decision: only relay's pinned JARVIS (the fallback whose `pin` file matched) is asked to plan; a JARVIS the site's `jarvis` setting names is never pin-checked, so it submits unbound, as before. JARVIS's `submission_plan_changed` refusal (exit 5) re-plans and shows a second card with the new plan, at most `MAX_ROUNDS` (2) times; it never submits what the person did not see. `_ask_inline` (legacy clients) now asks up to 3 rounds so that second card reaches them too. Decision: a package not yet reviewed gets no plan, because the review must come before JARVIS loads package Python; its card is relay-sourced and the submitted handle still carries the plan. Install jobs stay relay-sourced too (JARVIS plans them, but relay's install question is its own form), and `serve` is not pre-planned. Status reads `components.jsonl` (`jarvis_plan.component_states`, first terminal line per component wins) into `JobStatus.components`, so each component's state and hosts reach every carrier; JARVIS's roles are `batch`?`compute`, `service`?`service`, plus `install`. To keep the size ratchets, the PSI/J executor cache moved from both adapters into `jobs/psij_executors.batch_executor`. Ares proof through the stdio MCP path with the host's `io.iowarp/confirm: "server"` flag, a two-step demo.hello pipeline on 2 nodes: the first run had no JARVIS, so its card was relay-sourced and relay installed jarvis-cd 3b651c68 (slurm 28290). The next runs (slurm 28291, then 28292 after the pinned-only gate) asked exactly once, with `source: jarvis`, node ranges 0:1 and 1:2, and the script; JARVIS's record says `submission_plan.bound: true` with `script_sha256` equal to the card's (b9bc9f4b?, then 6f23bb63?). The running snapshot showed `hello` done while `hello_2` was starting, from `components.jsonl` (with hosts ares-comp-10 and -11). Tests: tests/relay_next/test_job_plan.py, tests/guard/test_site_plan.py (POSIX).
                                                                                Job card, W17's live run on Ares (slurm 28303, 28306, 28310). 1. `started_at` is the job's own start, not the operation's: jarvis-cd a28999b1 writes `metadata.scheduler_job` (`provider`, `job_id`, `started_at` in UTC, `source`) once from inside the allocation; `jarvis_record.scheduler_start` reads it only when its `job_id` is the handle's, `JobStatus.started_at` carries it, and the service keeps it in the result each poll. The card falls back to the first component line with hosts, then to the start of the latest running stretch (after an install job or a requeue). `install_progress` moved with it to the new `jarvis_record.py` (the size ratchet). 2. Components showed `pkg` (the pipeline id): fixed in JARVIS (jarvis-cd 3d1f3fb0, label option, then component_label(), then the package name); no relay change. 3. JARVIS's advisories (for example UCX to TCP, a `site_fact_uncertain` proposal JARVIS keeps out of exit 3 because TCP is correct, only slower) are now on the card as `advisories` (issue_id, kind, subject, fact, reason_codes, message, pick, consequence_if_wrong, remedy) and as "JARVIS notes:" lines in the confirm text. Before submit they come from the plan's `warnings`, after it from `metadata.site_facts.advisories` minus `confirmed` and `superseded_by_launch`. JARVIS offers to make them an exit-3 ask if the user wants one. 4. The site skill link `ares` failed: the decision above (site facts left relay's catalog) stands, so `catalog.RETIRED_SKILLS` names the deleted skills, and a site still linking one gets no skill link unless the person has their own `<config>/skills/<name>.md`. 5. demo.hello is not shipped: it was the take 1t proof package, and relay ships no packages; use JARVIS's builtin packages (gray_scott) or clio-jarvis. Relay's JARVIS pin is a28999b1. Tests: tests/relay_next/test_job_card_start.py, test_job_plan.py, test_w11_catalog.py. No Ares run for this change.
                                                                                Job card, a serve's confirm (retake 1u blocker, bee13a84). Every guarded `serve` failed before submission with `3 validation errors for JobSpec: job, ports, lifetime: Extra inputs are not permitted`: `plan_card.plan_first` ran `JobSpec.model_validate(record.spec)` before it looked at the record's kind, and a serve record holds a `ServiceSpec`. Fix: `plan_card` takes the job through `installing.job_of(record)` (run or serve, with the queue switch), marks a serve's steps as services by the rule `package_service_job` uses (every step when the caller marked none), and plans it with `guard.prepare(..., service=...)`. Decision: a service's plan is shown, not bound. `serve/runtime.start_for` submits `package_service_job(spec, plan, staged)`, which adds the connector, per-start secrets, staged files and env to the job, so no `+plan` of the job alone can match the script JARVIS would seal; the serve card therefore drops `plan_sha256` and `script` (`plan_card.UNBOUND`), keeps JARVIS's directives, components, couplings and advisories (still "JARVIS notes:" lines in the confirm text), and the text adds that relay makes the batch script when it starts the service. This replaces commit B's "`serve` is not pre-planned". Audit of every kind-specific path in surface, confirm, the card and transient: `plan_card` was the only one that validated a spec without its kind; `installing.job_of`, `job_card._job`, `transient.transient_digests`, `held_services`, `service._steps_view` and `_start_service` all branch on kind, and install records never take the confirm path. Proof (in-process, relay's real `start_run`/`start_serve` with `confirm=True`, the fixture adapter with a recording `plan_job`, `run` and serve hook; no test suite): the run card has `source: jarvis`, the script and `plan_sha256`, and the yes submits with `CLIO_RELAY_PLAN=sha256:5eed`; the serve card has `source: jarvis`, the component as `service`, `script: null`, no `plan_sha256`, the TCP advisory on the card and in the text, and the yes reaches the serve hook once, unbound. The same script on bee13a84 raises the reported error at `start_serve`.
                                                                                Retake 1u findings, after the design note. 1. exec and exec_read reported a quiet command at 30, 60, 120, 240 and 480 s and then not until 960 s, so an ssh channel that never closed (its cause is fence's) read as a silent "Running". The quiet reports now come every 30 s for as long as the command stays quiet (`_process.run_bounded` and `SshTransport.run_process`), with no time limit; a read-only call's report adds that running it again is safe, and the result carries `quiet` (`reports`, `longest_s`). Cancelling stays the host's (`notifications/cancelled`). 2. A service's card stayed "Running" after its job ended (slurm 28344), because a `serve` task completes at readiness and nothing looked at the job after that. `surface/job_watch.py` is now the one reconciler: `job_watch.watch` runs in the server's lifespan and every 30 s calls `job_watch.current`, which refreshes each open run, serve or install job that nobody observed in that time (the same `refresh` a `tasks/get` makes, so a polled job is not looked at twice), and when the card changes it announces `relay://jobs/{operation_id}` updated on the change bus. That resource template holds the newest card as JSON. A host that keeps a card after the task ends subscribes to it or rereads it; that is CLIO's side. 3. JARVIS's advisory message carried the agent's instruction ("Investigate with details.probes, propose a value, and have the person confirm it."). `jarvis_plan._advisory` now splits it: a sentence that names one of the issue's own `details.` fields goes to `agent_guidance`, along with the issue's `probes`, and `message` keeps the person's line. JARVIS's own `person_message` and `agent_guidance`, if it sends them, win. 4. Found in the proof: an ended job with no scheduler start lost `started_at`, because the card's fallback reset the running stretch on the final state too; it now resets only when the job waits again. 5. The production confirm said pilot op_01a7 "still runs on Ares" 11 min after slurm 28344 left squeue, the same cause as the stale card. The confirm's running-services question (`held_services.question`) and `describe(site)`'s jobs now first await `job_watch.current` for the site, so the confirm text, the Compute list and the card read one reconciled store; a look that fails says so in the confirm text ("though relay could not check it just now"). 6. A dead ssh transport surfaced as "ToolError: [exit 4294967295]", Windows's unsigned -1. `run_process` now reads exit codes as signed on every OS, ssh's own failure is 255 or -1 (`errors.SSH_FAILED`), and an `ssh()` session that ends without relay's exit marker and without a timeout is an `unreachable` refusal with `cause` `connection_lost`: "the ssh connection to <profile> was lost during <what>, before the command reported its end", adding that the command may have run in part. No test suite run. Proven on Ares in-process: exec `sleep 70` reported quiet at 30 and 60 s with `quiet` {reports 2, longest_s 60}; slurm 28352 on the handle path, with nobody polling, was reconciled to completed by the watcher and a legacy client got `notifications/resources/updated` for its `relay://jobs/` URI; JARVIS's own issues from slurm 27997 split as above; killing the local ssh.exe 10 s into exec `sleep 60` gave the `connection_lost` refusal, not a raw exit; serve op_d5baaa9a (slurm 28356, a 75 s tcp service) ended with nobody polling, and with the store still saying running, the confirm question for a new ares job came back empty and describe's card read ended.
                                                                                                                                                                Job card, a serve's yes is bound (follow-up to 0889629b). The path first: a serve start never runs sbatch from relay for a package job. `RelayService.submit` -> `_start_service` -> `serve_hook.start` -> `ServeRuntime.start` -> `adapter.serve` (ssh, local and worker call `serve.runtime.start_for`; others fall back on `verb_not_implemented`) -> `ServeRuntime.start_for` -> `adapter.run(package_service_job(spec, plan, staged))` -> `SshAdapter._submit`, where a package job on a Slurm site renders the JARVIS pipeline (`_render_jarvis`) and the site script's submit mode runs `jarvis ppl check`, then `jarvis ppl submit <pipeline> --execution-id E --plan_sha256 <sha> +json`; the connector starts from the pipeline's `scheduler.pre_cmds` inside JARVIS's allocation. A package service on another scheduler is refused; with no scheduler JARVIS runs it (`jarvis ppl run`). A plain-executable service (no package) is `wrapped_job` rendered by PSI/J, as a plain run is. Fact (jarvis-cd a28999b1): `plan_sha256` covers the script and `input_sha256`, which is pipeline.yaml (the scheduler block with `pre_cmds`, the steps and their parameters) and environment.yaml (only JARVIS's common variables when the pipeline names no `env`). The staged `serve/` files and the exported `CLIO_RELAY_*` variables are not sealed. So a start differed from any earlier plan only by the connector's arguments (operation, idle seconds, frpc), by the ports relay chose and filled into `${CLIO_RELAY_PORT_*}`, `${CLIO_RELAY_LOCAL_PORT_*}` and `${CLIO_RELAY_URL_*}` parameters, and by the path (frp or ssh). Decision: the serve runtime plans the job its start submits. `serve.planning.serve_plan` is the side-effect-free part of `start_for`, used by both (settings, path, ports; no secret, hop, save or stage). `plan_job(site, adapter, spec, record)` (new protocol `hooks.PlansServices`, separate from `ServeHook` so other hooks still satisfy it) returns `package_service_job(spec, plan, {})` and `serve.plan.port_choices(plan)`. `plan_card` plans that job, stores the choices under `record.result["service_choices"]`, and the card keeps `plan_sha256` and the script. The start reuses the kept ports (`plan_ports(choices=...)`, a desktop port only while it is still free), and `_start_service` binds the job (`CLIO_RELAY_PLAN`) and routes a refusal through `plan_card.refused`, so `submission_plan_changed` re-plans and shows a second card. Secrets stay by reference: the rendezvous token, the frp key and per-port secrets are only in the staged, transient `serve/secrets.env`, never in the sealed pipeline or the job's environment. A serve hook without `plan_job`, or a service that is not a package, keeps the unbound card with `SERVICE_NOTE`. Proof (in-process, no test suite; relay's real `start_serve`/`start_run` with `confirm=True`, the real `ServeRuntime` on the frp path, the fixture adapter as the site, and a stand-in JARVIS whose `plan_sha256` is the sha256 of the pipeline relay renders for the operation's job dir and which refuses a mismatched `CLIO_RELAY_PLAN` with `submission_plan_changed`): the serve card carries `plan_sha256` and the script, the sealed `pre_cmds` holds `exec sh serve/connect.sh <op> 600 <frpc>`, the yes submits with `CLIO_RELAY_PLAN` equal to the card's sha and the same sealed pipeline, with parameters `port` and `public_url` filled from the kept ports; with the kept desktop port taken before the yes, JARVIS refused once, relay showed a second card and the second yes submitted bound. No secret appears in the sealed pipeline or the environment; `serve/secrets.env` is the only transient input. A run still binds. The same script on 0889629b: no `plan_sha256`, no script, an unbound submit, and a sealed pipeline different from the planned one.
                                                                                                                                                                Warm start, a released wheel and a light CLI import (warm-start's first-launch measurements on Ares: a cold `uvx --from git+…` install of relay took about 34 s, with 8 to 9 s of git fetch plus a source build; a hash-locked plugin project took 14.2 s). Decision: relay ships as a GitHub pre-release, `v2.0.0a1`. The version is major because the rework replaces the tool surface, and `a1` because relay-rework is not merged. `release.yml` keeps the exact-tag build (`CLIO_RELAY_RELEASE_BUILD=1`), now writes `SHA256SUMS`, and attaches the wheel, the sdist and the sums to a GitHub Release. A PEP 440 pre-release is marked pre-release and not latest, and skips PyPI; a final version still publishes to PyPI as before. The marketplace plugin depends on the wheel by URL in a locked `server/` project. Fact: importing `clio_relay.cli` loaded the whole MCP stack (6.9 s warm on the Windows box), because `clio_relay.surface.__init__` imported `RelayService` and `clio_relay.jobs.__init__` imported every jobs module. Both packages now load those names on first use (PEP 562 `__getattr__`, with `TYPE_CHECKING` imports for pyright), so `import clio_relay.cli` takes 1.3 s and `serve` still loads the stack when it builds the server. Each of the 202 `clio_relay` modules imports alone in a fresh process. No test suite run. Fact: `v2.0.0a1` is 1df037bb, e35f5ce2 plus this entry's commit. Its release run built, attached and skipped PyPI; the tag's `ci` run was cancelled (no CI). The wheel's sha256 is `44087dbc0a4f44f0fbd13e0341e4f98e4adc9f7eb38b8a1496eac5e40d7b6b8b` and the sdist's is `75324d99fbb8cd60c1fabd928f5b91337b9c601d7125b666183e0eccd5a41473`. clio-kit's spack server got the same treatment, as the pre-release `spack-mcp-v1.0.0` at ef42fcf3 (wheel `3d5f6080cab94bc16250ffecbb86ba7a96098984bc9947823f21c724580d3f27`); its tag avoids `v*.*.*`, which clio-kit publishes to PyPI. Marketplace 6110bb7d locks both in `server/` projects with `no-build` (atomicwrites held at 1.4.0, the last with a wheel). Cold on Ares (uv 0.13.9, empty `UV_CACHE_DIR`, Python 3.14.10), the first `tools/list` came after 9.5 s for relay and 5.3 s for spack; warm, after 2.5 s and 1.5 s. Fact: `v2.0.0a2` is 5c8ce8dd: the retake-1u findings (5d30514a) and the `jaimecernuda-relay-cleanup` merge (860a0ac3, no conflicts, f817d1b4), a pre-release and not latest. Its wheel's sha256 is `f49de54e8ab193a140df7c48ac8558dc5576eb8e86ed3540ff23f91b5052c7c1` and the sdist's is `de65fe47f9a2b17995fc7782458c1c9aa80da0c8d13b1a0853267dffe62b61ad`. The tag's `ci` run was cancelled. Marketplace 5c76a9df (clio-relay 1.6.43) locks it; launched from that lock, relay reported 2.0.0a2 and listed its thirteen tools. Fact: `v2.0.0a3` is 45187727: the job card's `resource` URI (4422f9d3) and the Win32-OpenSSH overlapped stdin (4378ffb1), a pre-release and not latest. Its wheel's sha256 is `245ab1eff154a36a57ae44067deb65357aef0fa206ef45e60861875d725c1ef5` and the sdist's is `c4963b16d3aff5e53d5c1578ce5c6963ea40172c7a3ee29e251eb69f996e775e`. The tag's `ci` run was cancelled. Marketplace f5cb67e3 (clio-relay 1.6.44) locks it; launched from that lock, relay reported 2.0.0a3 and listed its thirteen tools.
                                                                                                                                                                Job card, its resource URI on the card (W17's request). Every card now names its own resource, `resource: relay://jobs/{operation_id}` (`job_card.job_uri`), so a host reads the URI it watches from the card and does not build it from the operation id. The card is the same snapshot everywhere, so the field rides on the confirm ask's `_meta`, `structuredContent.job`, each task status's `_meta`, `describe(site)`'s `jobs` and the resource itself. Proof (in-process, a local site, the host's `io.iowarp/confirm: server` flag; no test suite): the confirm card (phase planned), the `run` result, the task `_meta` while working and when completed, the card read back through its own URI, and every job `describe(site)` lists all carried `relay://jobs/<op>`.
                                                                                                                                                                ### Published interfaces (M1)
Owned by W1, not frozen. A change goes through W1, which reruns every consumer's contract tests first.

- Adapters (`clio_relay.adapters.base`): the `Adapter` Protocol (`name`, `Config`, `describe`, `run(spec, operation_id)`, `status`, `cancel`, `find(operation_id)`, `exec(command, *, timeout_s=None, max_output_bytes, on_stall=None)`, `files`, `serve`), `BaseAdapter[ConfigT]`, `SiteInfo`, `SignInNeed`, `AdapterContext`, and the optional `SiteAdded` (`on_site_added(site)`).
- Jobs (`clio_relay.jobs`): `JobSpec` (PSI/J names, `JobResources.to_psij()` builds `ResourceSpecV1`), `JobResources`, `JobAttributes`, `InputRef`, `PackageRef`, `ServiceSpec`, `ServicePort`, `ServiceLifetime`, `canonical_digest`, `JobHandle`, `JobStatus`, `JobState`, `ExecResult`, `StallReport` and `STALL_S`, `Endpoint`, `EndpointPort`, `FileEntry`, `FileAccess`, `OutputRef`.
- Records: `OperationRecord`, `OperationState`, `RecordStore`. Layout: `<state>/operations/`, `<state>/asks/`, `<state>/sites/<site>/<adapter>/`, and `sites.yaml` in the config directory.
- Content store: `ContentStore` at `<state>/content/cas/sha256/<first two hex>/<digest>`.
- `RefusalReason`, 24 typed reasons: `verb_not_implemented`, `site_not_found`, `site_exists`, `unknown_adapter`, `adapter_unavailable`, `invalid_config`, `invalid_spec`, `invalid_arguments`, `guard_unavailable`, `policy_refused`, `needs_approval`, `needs_sign_in`, `declined`, `interactive_auth_required`, `operation_not_found`, `ask_not_found`, `approval_mismatch`, `input_missing`, `path_not_found`, `path_exists`, `not_cancellable`, `submit_failed`, `unreachable`, `timeout`.
- Hooks (`clio_relay.adapters.hooks`): `Ask`, `AskProvider` (`choose`, `approve`, `url`) for W6; `GuardHook` (`check_run`, `check_exec`, `price`), `Charge` and `GuardDecision` for W13; `ServeHook` (`start`, `status`, `stop`, `attach`, `ui_data`) for W9; the optional `MountsPages` (`mount`) for an ask provider's pages. `HookContext.settings` holds `grant_files`, `config_dir` and, over HTTP, `public_base_url`.
- Serve runtime, provided by W9 and imported lazily: `clio_relay.serve.factory`, `start_for(adapter, spec, *, operation_id)`, `connect(handle, *, paths)` and `settings(paths, changes)`, where `changes` is None to show. `SiteEntry.serve` holds a site's serve settings.
- Change events: `ServerChanges` (above).
- Skills (`clio_relay.surface.skills`): `skill_uri(name)`, `site_skill_uri(site)`, `skills(config_dir)`, `site_skill`, `skill_link`, and the `SkillResources` provider.
- Routing (`clio_relay.sites.routing`): `route(entry, verb, check) -> Routed(adapter, config, skipped)`, `routes(entry, check) -> list[Route]`, and `Skipped(site, adapter, reason, message)` in `Route.skipped`.
- Command line `clio-relay` (`clio-relay-next` before the cutover): `serve` (stdio, or HTTP with a bearer token, `--grant-file`, `--tls-cert` and `--tls-key`), `adapters`, `site`, `asks`, `connect <handle>`, `rendezvous set|show`, `worker`.

### Conformance kit

`clio_relay.testing.conformance.AdapterConformance`, parameterised by a `ConformanceTarget(adapter, config, state_dir, ...)`. It holds 11 executable contract tests per adapter: config schema, describe shape, typed refusal of unimplemented verbs, bounded exec, exec with no time limit, files round trip, job lifecycle, job failure, job cancel, the same job observed after a restart, and inputs by digest. An adapter package subclasses it in its own tests (see the module docstring). W7 and W8 run it for `ssh` and `worker` (LQ4). The polish wave adds the three files tests and four more (typed errors, cancel after finish, exec cancelled, open and guarded), so it holds 18.

### M1 evidence (Ares)

All runs are in `~/clio-relay-rework/clio-relay-w1`, branch `jaimecernuda-relay-rework-w1`.

- **relay_next suite.** `uv run pytest tests/relay_next` gives 68 passed and 0 skipped. It covers:
  - `describe`, plus an ordinary `run` and a negotiated task on the `fixture` adapter (`test_server.py`) and on `local` (`test_server_local.py`);
  - form and URL elicitation through the stub ask provider, on both eras;
  - `input_required` answered with `tasks/update`;
  - decline and cancel;
  - a task outliving the server;
  - the typed refusal with a loopback page when the client has no elicitation;
  - the conformance kit on `fixture` and on `local` with no scheduler (20 tests);
  - listen-stream and legacy change events.
- **LQ41.** `test_transports.py` starts the real `clio-relay-next serve` as a subprocess. The plain `mcp` 2.3.0 `Client` and the fastmcp `Client` each run a job and answer a form and a URL question, over stdio and over Streamable HTTP, on `2025-11-25` and `2026-07-28`. A wrong bearer token is refused.
- **LQ4 for `local`.** The conformance kit passes for `local` without a scheduler (above). It also passes for `local` on Ares Slurm: `RELAY_TEST_SLURM_DIR` set, `tests/relay_next/test_conformance_slurm.py`, 10 passed. The Slurm suite is collected only when that directory is set, so it is never a skip.
- **LQ2 (relay half).** `describe` with no site lists the sites and adapters with status. With a site it reports the route for each verb (`test_describe`). The SSH-fixture half waits for W7's adapter.
- **LQ31a.** `uv build` gives `clio_relay-1.6.8-py3-none-any.whl`. A clean venv install runs `clio-relay-next --help` and `adapters` (lists `fixture`, `local`), and a stdio `describe` lists the five tools. `pip-audit` on the exported lock reports no known vulnerabilities.
- **Lint and types.** ruff check and ruff format are clean. pyright on the new packages has 0 errors. Repository-wide pyright reports 7514 errors, identical to origin/develop 034117b: 0 new and 0 gone.
- **W2 fixtures.** With `CLIO_RELAY_FIXTURES_ENV` set to W2's env file, `tests/test_fixtures_smoke.py` gives 13 passed.
- **Full suite.** It runs in Slurm on a compute node (`pytest -n 24`). The pre-existing failures are compared by node id with origin/develop 034117b on the same node type: develop gives 139 failed, 6482 passed, 7 skipped and 3 errors.
  - Job 27716 gives 143 failed, 6546 passed, 7 skipped and 3 errors. Job 27717 gives 138 failed, 6551 passed, 7 skipped and 3 errors. The 7 skips are the same as develop's (6 need systemd `--user`, 1 is Windows only).
  - Two tests that fail on develop pass on w1: `test_wheel_record_closure::test_native_jarvis_record_closure_verifies_standard_wheel_data`, and the bootstrap repair test, which no longer reads the real `~/.local/share/clio-relay`.
  - Some failures appear only on w1, and they differ between runs: 6 in `test_process_containment` (27716) and 1 in `test_storage_process_guard` (27717). Each is `systemd-run --user` failing with "Failed to connect to bus". Ares compute nodes have no user manager for this uid unless a session happens to exist there. w1 changes no containment code. On the login node, which has linger and a stable user bus, both modules pass 43 of 43 in 3 runs out of 3. This is recorded as an open item, not written off.
  - The dependency bumps needed these test changes:
    - The embedded bootstrap verifier now requires every console script in the wheel's `entry_points.txt`, so `clio-relay-next` verifies. The empty `catalog/{sites,skills}/.gitkeep` files became a non-empty `README.txt`.
    - FastMCP 4.2 always derives a tool title.
    - The mcp 2.3 client stamps `Mcp-Name` on each request, so the forged-name test rewrites it in an httpx request hook.
    - filelock 4 applies the explicit mode only to a lock file it creates, so the late-start test makes `transition.lock` owner-private.

## W2. Fixtures and the Ares harness

Branch `jaimecernuda-relay-rework-w2`. Files: `tests/fixtures/sites/` (one Dockerfile and entrypoint per site, the two services, a README), `scripts/ares/fixtures.sh`, `tests/test_fixtures_smoke.py`.

### How to use them

```bash
scripts/ares/fixtures.sh up        # builds images the first time, then starts all seven sites
scripts/ares/fixtures.sh status
scripts/ares/fixtures.sh down      # --images also removes the images
```

`up` writes `~/clio-relay-rework/state/w2-fixtures/fixtures.env` (override with `FX_STATE_DIR`). It names every port, the client key, `ssh_config` (hosts `fx-ssh`, `fx-slurm`, `fx-pbs`, `fx-otp`), `known_hosts`, the `frpc` binary, and each secret by file path, never inline. Tests read it from `$CLIO_RELAY_FIXTURES_ENV` or that default path. Containers on the fixture network reach each other as `fx-<site>`; the env file also carries the inner addresses (`FX_INNER_*`).

| Site | Container port | Ares port (127.0.0.1) | What it is |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `fx-ssh` | 22 | 47100 | SSH host, user `relay`, key only, scp and sftp |
| `fx-slurm` | 22 | 47101 | SSH plus Slurm 21.08.5, one node, partitions `compute` (default) and `debug`, `sacct` through slurmdbd |
| `fx-pbs` | 22 | 47102 | SSH plus OpenPBS 23.06.06, one node, queues `workq` and `debug`, job history on |
| `fx-otp` | 22 | 47103 | SSH that asks for the key and then `Verification code:` (TOTP) |
| `fx-frps` | 7000 | 47104 | frps 0.69.1, tcp and websocket, token auth, `tcpMux` as `render_frps_config` |
| `fx-frps` | 7443 | 47105 | wss: a TLS front (socat) handing the connection to frps |
| `fx-secret` | 8080 | 47106 | HTTP and WebSocket service that checks a secret |
| `fx-counter` | 8000 | 47107 | Counter MCP server at `/mcp`, `count_slowly` is a task tool |

Ports are chosen from 47100 up, skipping any port in use, and kept in `ports.env` so later runs reuse them. Every SSH site also carries `clio-fixture-secret-service` and `clio-fixture-counter-mcp` on the PATH, for LQ12 to LQ15 to start on a site.

### Decisions

- Plain `docker`, no compose file. Ares has docker 29.1.3 with no compose plugin, no `docker-compose` and no `podman-compose`. Every container, network and image carries `clio-relay-rework=1`, `com.docker.compose.project=clio-relay-rework` and `clio-relay-rework.owner=w2-fixtures`. Containers the script runs also carry `clio-relay-rework.fx-run=1`, because image labels reach a build's intermediate containers; `down` selects on all of them, so it never stops a running build or anything another session owns.
- All SSH sites build on `ubuntu:22.04` with OpenSSH 8.9, as on the clusters relay targets. The fixture Python (3.12, fastmcp 4.0.0b1 and fastmcp-tasks) lives in `/opt/clio-fixtures/venv`, off the user's PATH, so the site looks like a plain host to relay's bootstrap. The system Python is 3.10.
- PBS is OpenPBS built from source. psij's PBS executor reads `qstat -f -F json -x`, which Torque does not answer. Only `/opt/pbs` is copied into the runtime image; copying the whole install root replaced Ubuntu's `/lib` symlink.
- Slurm includes slurmdbd and MariaDB because relay's Slurm provider calls `sacct`. The database password is fixture-local and in the repo on purpose.
- The one-time-code site uses `pam_google_authenticator` with `AuthenticationMethods publickey,keyboard-interactive:pam`. A BatchMode client is refused after the key; a client that answers the prompt with the current TOTP gets in.
- frps 0.69.1 does not serve wss itself (`transport.protocol = "wss"` against the bind port fails with EOF). The fixture fronts it with TLS on a second port, as Cloudflare does for a public frps. frpc logs in over wss without being given the CA, so frpc 0.69.1 does not verify the wss certificate by default. The self-signed certificate is in the env file as `FX_WSS_CA_FILE` for anyone who wants to pin it.
- Secrets are made at `up` and never baked into an image. Each container gets only its role's files, copied in rather than bind-mounted, since the state directory may sit on NFS. The client's private key never enters a container.
- `authorized_keys` on each site is copied once and stays writable by `relay`, so a test can add and remove its own key (LQ40).

### Findings for other workstreams

- Protocol `2026-07-28` has no MCP session. Against the counter, three calls on one fastmcp 4.0.0b1 `Client` gave three different `ctx.session_id` values over Streamable HTTP (no `mcp-session-id` header is sent) and also over stdio. The counter therefore reports `server_instance` (one per process) beside `session_id`, and the smoke test proves continuity with that. LQ14's "one session served all three" needs reading as one server instance and one client connection; W9 and W16 should confirm that reading with the coordinator.
- `scripts/check_file_size.py` reports that the tests distribution ratchet can rise to 63.84%. That file is W1's.
- The smoke module skips as a whole when the env file is missing, so a run anywhere but a fixture host shows one module skip.

### Smoke evidence on Ares

Run at `a774081` (fixture code identical to `36219c0`) after a fresh `down`, `build`, `up`, from `~/clio-relay-rework/clio-relay-w2` with the shared toolchain (`uv run`).

- `scripts/ares/fixtures.sh up`: all seven sites ready in 80 s with images built.
- `uv run pytest tests/test_fixtures_smoke.py`: `13 passed in 58.31s`. The tests:
  - `fx-ssh`: hostname, user and Python; a 64 KiB file round-trips through scp and sftp with a matching SHA-256; a key not in `authorized_keys` is refused.
  - `fx-slurm`: `sinfo` shows both partitions; psij's `squeue --me -o %i -h -r -t all` runs; an `sbatch --wrap` job reaches `COMPLETED` in `sacct` and its output names `fx-slurm`.
  - `fx-pbs`: a `qsub` job reaches `job_state F` with `Exit_status 0` in `qstat -f -F json -x`, and its output is staged back.
  - `fx-otp`: BatchMode is refused with `Permission denied`; askpass answering `Verification code:` with the computed TOTP gets in.
  - Secret service: `/health` is open; `/` gives 401 without the secret and with a wrong one, and 200 with it; `/ws` gives 401 without the secret and echoes with it. Started on `fx-ssh` over SSH, it writes its final artifact on SIGTERM.
  - frp: an stcp proxy and visitor through frps reach the secret service over `tcp`, `websocket` and `wss`; a wrong token is refused at login.
  - Counter: three increments read `start+1..start+3` from one server instance; `count_slowly` through `fastmcp_tasks.call_tool_task` returns a task id and a result with `background_task: true`. Over SSH stdio on `fx-ssh` the counter reads 1, 2, 3.
- `scripts/ares/fixtures.sh down`: removed 7 containers and 1 network; the 12 containers of other owners on Ares were still running afterwards.
- `ruff check`, `ruff format --check`, strict `pyright` (0 errors) and `shellcheck -x` are clean on the touched files; `scripts/check_file_size.py` passes.
- Image sizes: ssh 429 MB, slurm 814 MB, pbs 789 MB, otp 431 MB, frps 52 MB.

## W3: host semantics wire contract (clio-agent)
**Merged** into the clio-agent integration branch `jaimecernuda-relay-rework` as 6cb4e6656; W3's gate has passed.

Full text: clio-agent `docs/design/host-semantics-contract.md` (branch jaimecernuda-relay-rework-w3). The excerpt below is what other workstreams build against.

**O9.** No connector entry and no new manifest capability. A plugin declares its own server in `mcp.json` beside `plugin.json` (Agent Plugins 1.0.0 layout). Proven on Ares with the generic fixture `tests/fixtures/plugins/own_server/`: `plugin install` plus `plugin trust` were enough, and the production `discover_declared_mcp_servers` and `transport_for` spawned the server with spec `source='plugin:own-server'` and `connector=''`. W14 ships relay's server in its plugin's `mcp.json`.

**Manifest.** `plugin.json` `extensions["ai.iowarp.clio"]` gains three fields:
- `tabs: [{id, title, icon (Lucide name), server (mcp.json name, optional if only one), resource: "a2ui://..."}]`
- `accounts: [{provider: github|google_drive|globus|ndp, scopes: [..]}]`
- `elicitation: {loopback: bool, origins: ["https://host[:port]"]}`

`catalogs` stays as it is, as the server's producible set.

**HS1a.** The client advertises MCP extension `org.a2ui/a2ui` with settings `{versions: ["v0.9","v0.9.1"], clientCapabilities: {"v0.9": {supportedCatalogIds: [<the server's plugin catalogs>]}}}`.
- Under 2026-07-28 the SDK stamps it on every request in `_meta["io.modelcontextprotocol/clientCapabilities"].extensions`.
- Send A2UI only when that request carries it, and always send the full text result too.
- A server with no plugin catalogs gets no entry.

**HS1.**
- A2UI is a tool-result content item `{"type":"resource","resource":{"mimeType":"application/a2ui+json","text":...}}`, or a `resources/read` item with that mimeType. `text` is one A2UI message or an array of them, applied as one batch. `blob` and `resource_link` are not used.
- Versions: only `v0.9` or `v0.9.1`.
- Typed refusals: `a2ui_version_unsupported`, `a2ui_payload_invalid`, `a2ui_catalog_unknown`, `a2ui_catalog_not_producible`, `a2ui_surface_not_owned`, `a2ui_surface_owner_conflict`, `a2ui_validation_failed`.
- The model never sees the A2UI JSON, only a placeholder plus the text result.
- The surface gets `owner: {kind:"server", plugin, server, namespace}`.
- For a task-augmented call, A2UI is applied from the terminal result.

**HS2.**
- Actions on a server-owned surface go to `POST /v1/sessions/{sid}/a2ui/actions`, with the usual envelope.
- `action.name` is the server's tool name. CLIO sends `tools/call` with `arguments` = `action.context`, verbatim, and no model turn.
- The tool must have `_meta.ui.visibility` absent or including `"app"`. Otherwise CLIO returns 403 `a2ui_server_tool_not_app_visible`.
- A2UI in the result is applied only to surfaces that server owns.
- A task's `input_required` becomes a session question.
- Response: `{status:"delivered", destination:"server", action_id, tool, outcome: applied|no_update|task_pending|tool_error, surface_ids}`. Errors: 503 `a2ui_server_unavailable`, 422 for HS1 reasons.
- Exception: `data_source/login/<provider>`, sent with an empty context, is a host action. CLIO's own sign-in runs.
- The context is opaque to CLIO, so action tokens belong in it.

**HS3.**
- Routes:
  - `GET /v1/sessions/{sid}/plugin-tabs` returns `{tabs:[{id:"<plugin>/<tab>", plugin, title, icon, server, resource, available, reason}]}`.
  - `POST .../plugin-tabs/{plugin}/{tab}/open` returns `{tab, surfaces, revision}`.
  - `POST .../close`.
  - `POST .../actions` behaves as in HS2.
- `open` sends `resources/read`, plus `resources/subscribe` if the server supports it. The read result replaces all of the tab's surfaces, and its first batch must start with `createSurface`.
- `notifications/resources/updated` for that URI triggers a re-read and replace. CLIO does not poll.
- Tab surfaces live in memory only. They are not in the transcript and not seen by the model.
- Unavailable reasons: `plugin_tab_server_unavailable`, `plugin_tab_resource_failed`.

**HS4.**
- Declared scopes are added to that provider's consent. For Globus they go beside transfer and `offline_access`.
- For each declared provider and each launched (stdio) server, CLIO sets `CLIO_ACCOUNT_<PROVIDER>_FILE` (for example `CLIO_ACCOUNT_GLOBUS_FILE`). It also expands `${CLIO_ACCOUNT_<PROVIDER>_FILE}` in `mcp.json` args and env, so a plugin can map it to the server's own setting name (HS4a).
- The variable is always set. The file exists only while a grant does.
- File protection: private dir 0700, file 0600, atomic replace. CLIO rewrites the file at least 5 minutes before expiry and deletes it on disconnect.
- File format: `{version:1, provider, written_at, tokens:[{resource_server, access_token, token_type, expires_at, scopes:[..]}]}`. One entry per Globus resource server, and no refresh token.
- No token ever goes in env or args.

**HS5.** URL elicitation is accepted from:
- Any server: the configured `tools.mcp.elicitation.url_trusted_origins`.
- That server only: the plugin's `elicitation.origins` (https).
- A launched server whose plugin has `loopback: true`: `http://127.0.0.1:<any port>` and `http://localhost:<any port>`.

Everything else is refused with the existing reasons: `elicitation_url_insecure_scheme`, `_untrusted_origin` or `_not_declared`. Question metadata gains `origin`, `origin_trust: configured|plugin_origin|plugin_loopback`, `plugin` and `server`.

**SSE on /v1/sessions/{sid}/events:**
- `a2ui.surface.upserted`, now with `owner`
- `a2ui.server_surface.refused` `{plugin, server, tool, uri, reason, detail}`
- `plugin_tab.surface.upserted` `{tab_id, revision, surface}`
- `plugin_tab.surface.deleted`
- `plugin_tab.refreshed` `{tab_id, revision, surface_ids}`
- `plugin_tab.unavailable` `{tab_id, reason, detail}`

**Decisions (coordinator):**
- `org.a2ui/a2ui` accepted; advertise an official ID too if A2UI publishes one.
- Tabs are per session. The relay plugin goes in the default agent (W14).

## W4. CLIO removals and SSH through the fence

RL37 done in clio-agent (W4, `jaimecernuda-relay-rework-w4`). Fenced-shell ssh, scp, sftp and Git over SSH run through `runtime/net_connect.py`, a CONNECT helper used as ProxyCommand. The shims come from `runtime/fence_ssh.py` and are on PATH only inside an enforced fence. LQ29 passed on Ares inside codex-linux-sandbox 0.167.0: `ssh <host> hostname` succeeded and net.egress showed host and port 22. scp, sftp, `git ls-remote` and `git clone` over SSH passed. A host the policy denies is refused with an explicit message. Open: the Linux Codex profile grants network "enabled" (host netns), so egress that does not go through the proxy is neither recorded nor gated.

RL36: "ssh_execute removed from clio-agent. Deleted: gact/ssh_tools.py, gact/ssh_recorded_presentation.py, gact/infrastructure/ssh_execution.py, gact/infrastructure/ssh_result.py, their tests and docs/verification/ssh-agent-execution-2026-10-19.md. The native_tool_registry entry and the legacy presentation calls in tool_result_presentation.py and session_export_projection.py are gone. Recorded ssh_execute rows render through the generic recorded-presentation path; new test test_retired_tool_presentation.py. Agents reach SSH hosts from the fenced shell (RL37); docs/PERMISSIONS.md says so. Kept: the Infrastructure SSH transport (paramiko SshRoute), storage SFTP and NDP SSH routes."

RL38: "Relay leftovers removed from clio-agent.
